# E2EE (control-plane) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (- [ ]) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** End-to-end encrypt the phone↔desktop control-plane (the JSON `/ws` protocol) so the self-hosted relay (hub / nginx / frp) only ever sees ciphertext, with one-time pairing, per-device revocable keys, and a mandatory cleartext hard-cut once a bridge is E2EE-provisioned.

**Architecture:** A phone drives a second `codex app-server` sharing `~/.codex` through the desktop bridge; this plan wraps every phone↔bridge control frame in XChaCha20-Poly1305 keyed by a per-device pre-shared key (PSK) derived (HKDF-SHA256) into two direction subkeys, so the upper protocol object is unchanged but the wire carries only `{k,s,n,c}`. The PSK is delivered once during a NaCl/box pairing handshake (the machine's long-term enroll keypair seals `{keyId,psk}` to the phone's device public key), after which the relay multiplexes opaque ciphertext and the hub stays protocol-blind in both direct-LAN and hub modes.

**Tech Stack:** Go `golang.org/x/crypto` (`chacha20poly1305` NewX, `hkdf`, `nacl/box`) on the bridge; Dart `package:cryptography` (`Xchacha20.poly1305Aead`, `Hkdf`+`Hmac.sha256()`, X25519) plus `pinenacl` (NaCl box, byte-compatible with `x/crypto/nacl/box`) on the phone.

---

## Locked Decisions

These override any contract `openQuestion`; honor each verbatim.

- **D-a** — Hot-reload = zero-dependency mtime poll: `devstore.Watch(ctx, interval, onReload)` stats `devices.json` mtime every ~2s and calls `Reload()` on change. Cross-platform, no SIGHUP/fsnotify dependency. Tests call `Reload()` directly.
- **D-b** — On-disk dir = provision's existing config dir (same dir as `menubar.env`; macOS `~/.codex-remote`, Windows `%ProgramData%/codex-remote`). `enroll.key`(0600)/`enroll.pub`(0644)/`devices.json`(0600)/`pending-pairings.json`(0600) all live there. Drafters must READ provision's path resolver for the exact function name and reuse it. NOT in `~/.codex`.
- **D-c** — Hard-cut is mandatory once the bridge is E2EE-provisioned (`enroll.key` present + devices store wired). A non-provisioned bridge stays legacy (current behavior) = the migration path. No per-session legacy fallback once E2EE is on.
- **D-d** — Dart NaCl box via `pinenacl` (byte-compatible with Go `x/crypto/nacl/box` / TweetNaCl). `cryptography` package for XChaCha20-Poly1305 AEAD + HKDF-SHA256 + X25519 keygen. Add BOTH to pubspec. (`package:cryptography` does not reproduce NaCl box's HSalsa20 KDF step.)
- **D-e** — `pair_error` wire reasons in v1 = `unknown|expired` only; a reused/consumed `pairingId` reads back as `unknown` (Consume cannot distinguish). `consumed` reserved/documented; app shows the same `二维码失效，请重新生成` for all reasons.
- **D-f** — `keyId` = 16 random bytes hex (reuse `NewToken()`); safe as HKDF salt + AAD component.
- **D-g** — E2EE enabled by setting additive `Server` fields (`devices`/`enrollSec`/`pending`) — struct-literal in tests, wired in prod via a `Server.EnableE2EE(devices, enrollSec, pending)` helper. `openInbound`/`sealOutbound` are `Server` methods in package `bridge` calling the `e2ee` package. NO breaking constructor change to `NewServer`.
- **D-h** — Prod wiring lives in T8: `cmd/codexbridge/main.go` (serve) loads `enroll.key`, opens devstore + pending, calls `Server.EnableE2EE`, starts `devstore.Watch`. Drafter must READ `cmd/codexbridge/main.go` for the exact serve setup.
- **D-i** — Keep the existing token gate at the WS upgrade (`authDevice`) in direct mode. E2EE is ADDITIVE: token routes/rate-limits, PSK authenticates content. Do NOT remove `authDevice`.
- **D-j** — Miniprogram `relay.js` intentionally NOT updated (out of scope). An E2EE-mode bridge refuses miniprogram cleartext. Documented as a conscious limitation.
- **D-k** — In E2EE mode `conn.device` (audit/approval name) is set from `devstore.Lookup(keyId).Name` after keyId binding.

## ⚠️ Known Blockers — READ BEFORE EXECUTING

A full completeness-critique of this plan surfaced **8 real issues** that will block compilation or break the encrypted steady-state. They stem from several tasks editing the **same files** (`server.go`, `server_test.go`, `agent.go`) and from a producer/consumer test-vector gap. Resolve each at the task noted — do **not** start executing top-to-bottom without accounting for these.

### Compile-blockers

| # | Where | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | T5 Step 1 + T6 Step 3/4 (`server.go`) | T5 and T6 **both** add the three additive `Server` fields (`devices`, `enrollSec`, `pending`) **and** the `EnableE2EE` helper. T6 depends on T5, so re-adding => *duplicate field / redeclared method*. | T6 Step 3 (imports) + Step 4 (fields/`EnableE2EE`) become **no-ops** — already landed in T5. T6 keeps only the **conn** crypto fields + seam wiring + `handleWS` rewrite. |
| B2 | T6 Step 1 vs T11 Step 1 (`server_test.go`) | `e2eeFixture` declared **twice with incompatible kinds**: T6 = `func e2eeFixture(t) (...)`; T11 = `type e2eeFixture struct{}` + `newE2EEEnv`. A func and a type can't share a name => *redeclared*. | Rename one. Recommend T11's struct -> **`e2eeEnv`** (`newE2EEEnv` -> `newE2EEEnv`). |
| B3 | T7 Step 3 vs T11 Step 5 | `mustJSON` declared twice in package `bridge`: T7 `func mustJSON(v any) json.RawMessage` (agent.go) vs T11 `func mustMarshal(t *testing.T, v any) []byte` (server_test.go). | Rename the **T11 test helper** -> `mustMarshal(t, v)` and update its call-sites. *(Already applied in this doc; verify during T11.)* |
| B4 | T6 Step 1 vs T11 Step 1 (`server_test.go` imports) | Both rewrite the import block from the **same original 8-import form**. T11 runs after T6, so T6 already expanded it — T11's `replace` anchor no longer matches. | T11 Step 1 must **reconcile with / append to** the post-T6 import block, not re-replace the original. |
| B5 | T7 Step 12 (`agent_test.go`) | Test calls `registerApproval(id, method, params, owner)` — **does not exist** (real helper is `onApproval`, auto-mints id via `NewToken`, no explicit-id param). | Rewrite the test to insert a `pendingApproval{id, method, params, ownerDevice, ch}` **directly** into `s.approvals` under `s.apMu`. |

T11 Step 10 references `kVectorKeyId`/`kVectorKP2B`/`kVectorKB2P` — none exist in that scope. | Align on one naming scheme; ensure a base64 `kB2P` constant lives in `codec_test.dart`. |

### Functional gap (critical)

| # | Where | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| B8 | T10 (`bridge_client.dart` / `main.dart`) | `connect()` is **never** modified to rebuild `_codec` for an **already-paired** machine on normal launch / reconnect / machine-switch. The codec is armed only inside `connect_screen._pairAndConnect` during a **fresh** v2-QR scan. So a returning paired phone calls `bridge.connect(...)` with `_codec == null` -> sends **cleartext** -> the E2EE bridge hard-cuts it. **The steady-state control-plane seal (spec §1/§8) is broken.** | Add `loadCodecFor(machine)` (reads `loadPsk`/`loadDeviceKey` + `machine.keyId`, `deriveKeys`, arms the codec) and call it **before** `connect()` on every launch/reconnect. Send `pair_init` only on first pairing. |

- **T10 Step 12** `notifyListeners()` is `@protected` on `ChangeNotifier`; calling it from `connect_screen` will be analyzer-flagged. Commit to adding `MachineStore.setKeyId(...)`.
- **Vector placeholders** (`PASTE_BASE64_*_FROM_GO_TEST` in T1/T9) are an **intentional** TDD producer->consumer handoff: run the Go vector test, capture the printed base64, paste before the Dart tests pass.
- **`PairError.Reason` = `unknown|expired`** (2 values) is the **intended D-e override** of the contract's 3-value list — `consumed` collapses to `unknown`. Do not "fix" it back.

**Files:**
- Modify: `bridge/go.mod` + `bridge/go.sum` (add `golang.org/x/crypto`, finalized via `go mod tidy`)
- Create: `bridge/internal/e2ee/doc.go` (package doc-comment = spec; also records the pinned cross-language vector)
- Create: `bridge/internal/e2ee/kdf.go` (`DeriveKeys`)
- Create: `bridge/internal/e2ee/codec.go` (`Envelope`, `Dir`, `aad`, `Seal`/`Open`, `sealWithNonce`, sentinel errors)
- Test: `bridge/internal/e2ee/kdf_test.go` (HKDF determinism + `kP2B != kB2P` + PRINTS pinned `kP2B`/`kB2P`)
- Test: `bridge/internal/e2ee/codec_test.go` (round-trip table, tamper/wrong-key→`ErrAuth`, seq→`ErrSeq`, keyId→`ErrKeyMismatch`, nonce-len→`ErrNonceLen`, PRINTS pinned ciphertext)

**Depends on:** nothing (layer `go-crypto`; this is the root task — its printed vector seeds T9's Dart tests).

> Conventions honored: module is `github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge` (go 1.26); repo favors many small files + an authoritative top-of-file package doc-comment; immutability + comprehensive error handling. Per locked decision **D-f**, `keyId` is 16 random bytes hex (the existing `bridge.NewToken()` shape) and is used here only as the HKDF **salt** + **AAD** component — this task does not generate it, it only consumes a caller-supplied `keyID` string. This task is pure crypto: no disk, no `Server`, no `conn`; the additive `Server` E2EE fields + `EnableE2EE` (D-g/D-h) and `authDevice` retention (D-i) belong to later tasks (T6/T8) and are out of scope here.
>
> Dependency handling: the `golang.org/x/crypto` require is NOT pre-committed on its own. It is added with `go get` immediately before its first consumer (kdf.go) and finalized with `go mod tidy` in the SAME step, so `go.mod`/`go.sum` land in one consistent state (direct require, complete `go.sum`) and are committed together with the code that imports them. (Verified: a bare `go get` with no importing file writes the require as `// indirect` with a partial `go.sum`; a plain `go build` does NOT drop the `// indirect` marker — only `go mod tidy` does.)

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the FAILING kdf test (RED).** Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/kdf_test.go`. It pins the fixed inputs from the contract (`psk = 0x01..0x20`, `keyId = "k_test_0001"`), asserts determinism, asserts `kP2B != kB2P`, asserts both subkeys are 32 bytes, and PRINTS `base64(kP2B)` / `base64(kB2P)` (these printed constants are recorded in `doc.go` in Step 6 and copied verbatim into the Dart `kdf_test.dart` in T9 — Go is the oracle). Use `t.Log` so the values surface under `-v`.

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/base64"
	"testing"
)

// fixedPSK is the cross-language test vector PSK: byte i == i+1, i in [0,32).
func fixedPSK() []byte {
	psk := make([]byte, 32)
	for i := range psk {
		psk[i] = byte(i + 1)
	}
	return psk
}

const fixedKeyID = "k_test_0001"

func TestDeriveKeysDeterministic(t *testing.T) {
	a1, b1, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	a2, b2, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys (2nd): %v", err)
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(a1, a2) || !bytes.Equal(b1, b2) {
		t.Fatal("DeriveKeys not deterministic for identical inputs")
	}
}

func TestDeriveKeysDirectionsDiffer(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, kB2P, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	if len(kP2B) != 32 || len(kB2P) != 32 {
		t.Fatalf("subkey length: kP2B=%d kB2P=%d want 32/32", len(kP2B), len(kB2P))
	}
	if bytes.Equal(kP2B, kB2P) {
		t.Fatal("kP2B == kB2P: two directions MUST derive distinct subkeys")
	}
}

func TestDeriveKeysRejectsBadInput(t *testing.T) {
	if _, _, err := DeriveKeys(nil, fixedKeyID); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected error for nil psk")
	}
	if _, _, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), ""); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected error for empty keyId")
	}
}

// TestPrintKdfVector is the producer half of the cross-language vector.
// It prints base64(kP2B)/base64(kB2P) for the FIXED inputs; these are recorded
// in doc.go (Step 6) and copied into app/test/e2ee/kdf_test.dart (T9). Never
// fails; run with -v to read it.
func TestPrintKdfVector(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, kB2P, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	t.Logf("VECTOR kP2B (base64) = %s", base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(kP2B))
	t.Logf("VECTOR kB2P (base64) = %s", base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(kB2P))
}
```

Run it (expect a package BUILD failure — `DeriveKeys` does not exist yet; the package has no non-test source):

```bash
cd bridge && go test ./internal/e2ee/ -run TestDeriveKeys -v
```

Expected: FAIL — a package build error, e.g. `# github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/e2ee [build failed]` with `./kdf_test.go: undefined: DeriveKeys`. (No tests run because the package does not compile yet — that is the RED state.)

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the dependency, then implement `kdf.go` to make the kdf test PASS (GREEN).** First add `golang.org/x/crypto` from the bridge module (its first importer, kdf.go, is created in this same step), then create `bridge/internal/e2ee/kdf.go`. HKDF-SHA256 over the 32-byte PSK, `salt = []byte(keyID)`, the two pinned info strings, each output 32 bytes (`chacha20poly1305.KeySize`). Per contract: `info` for `kP2B` is `"codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge"`, for `kB2P` is `"codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone"`. Do NOT touch the existing `crypto/subtle` usage elsewhere.

```bash
cd bridge && go get golang.org/x/crypto@v0.53.0
```

Then create `bridge/internal/e2ee/kdf.go`:

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"crypto/sha256"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20poly1305"
	"golang.org/x/crypto/hkdf"
)

// HKDF info labels. These ASCII strings MUST byte-match the Dart side
// (app/lib/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart). Versioned so a future scheme can
// coexist without colliding the derived keyspace.
const (
	infoP2B = "codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge"
	infoB2P = "codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone"
)

// errEmptyPSK / errEmptyKeyID guard the two ways a caller can hand us
// unusable derivation inputs. They are local (not part of the wire sentinel
// set) because they signal a programming bug, not a peer-controlled failure.
var (
	errEmptyPSK   = errors.New("e2ee: psk must be non-empty")
	errEmptyKeyID = errors.New("e2ee: keyID must be non-empty")
)

// DeriveKeys expands the per-device PSK into the two direction subkeys with
// HKDF-SHA256. salt is the keyID bytes; the two info strings separate the
// phone->bridge and bridge->phone keyspaces so the two directions never share
// a nonce space. Each output is exactly chacha20poly1305.KeySize (32) bytes.
//
// The PSK and keyID are caller-supplied (PSK from devstore, keyID from the
// envelope); this function performs NO disk or network I/O.
func DeriveKeys(psk []byte, keyID string) (kP2B, kB2P []byte, err error) {
	if len(psk) == 0 {
		return nil, nil, errEmptyPSK
	}
	if keyID == "" {
		return nil, nil, errEmptyKeyID
	}
	salt := []byte(keyID)
	kP2B, err = expand(psk, salt, infoP2B)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: derive p2b: %w", err)
	}
	kB2P, err = expand(psk, salt, infoB2P)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: derive b2p: %w", err)
	}
	return kP2B, kB2P, nil
}

// expand runs one HKDF-SHA256 extract+expand and returns a 32-byte key.
func expand(secret, salt []byte, info string) ([]byte, error) {
	r := hkdf.New(sha256.New, secret, salt, []byte(info))
	out := make([]byte, chacha20poly1305.KeySize)
	if _, err := io.ReadFull(r, out); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return out, nil
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the FAILING codec test (RED).** Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/codec_test.go`. It covers: round-trip table (p2b + b2p), tamper-C → `ErrAuth`, wrong-key → `ErrAuth`, AAD/seq mismatch → `ErrSeq`, keyId mismatch → `ErrKeyMismatch`, nonce-length guard → `ErrNonceLen` (via `sealWithNonce`), and the pinned-vector producer printing `base64(ciphertext)`. The fixed inputs are exactly per contract: `nonce = 0xA0..0xB7` (24 bytes), `seq = 0`, `dir = DirP2B`, plaintext = compact JSON `{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}`. It reuses `fixedPSK()` / `fixedKeyID` from `kdf_test.go` (same `package e2ee` test binary).

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/base64"
	"errors"
	"testing"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20poly1305"
)

// fixedNonce is the pinned 24-byte vector nonce: byte i == 0xA0+i.
func fixedNonce() []byte {
	n := make([]byte, chacha20poly1305.NonceSizeX)
	for i := range n {
		n[i] = byte(0xA0 + i)
	}
	return n
}

// fixedPlaintext is the exact compact-JSON bytes of the vector prompt frame.
var fixedPlaintext = []byte(`{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}`)

func TestSealOpenRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, kB2P, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	cases := []struct {
		name string
		key  []byte
		dir  Dir
		seq  uint64
		msg  []byte
	}{
		{"p2b/seq0", kP2B, DirP2B, 0, fixedPlaintext},
		{"p2b/seq7", kP2B, DirP2B, 7, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`)},
		{"b2p/seq0", kB2P, DirB2P, 0, []byte(`{"type":"sessions","data":[]}`)},
		{"b2p/empty", kB2P, DirB2P, 3, []byte(``)},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			env, err := Seal(tc.key, fixedKeyID, tc.dir, tc.seq, tc.msg)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
			}
			if env.K != fixedKeyID || env.S != tc.seq {
				t.Fatalf("envelope header K=%q S=%d want %q/%d", env.K, env.S, fixedKeyID, tc.seq)
			}
			got, err := Open(tc.key, fixedKeyID, tc.dir, tc.seq, env)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("Open: %v", err)
			}
			if !bytes.Equal(got, tc.msg) {
				t.Fatalf("round-trip mismatch: got %q want %q", got, tc.msg)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestOpenTamperCiphertextRejects(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := Seal(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	raw, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(env.C)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("decode C: %v", err)
	}
	raw[0] ^= 0x01 // flip one ciphertext bit
	env.C = base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(raw)
	if _, err := Open(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrAuth) {
		t.Fatalf("tampered ciphertext: got %v want ErrAuth", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenWrongKeyRejects(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, kB2P, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := Seal(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	// Open with the other direction's subkey: AEAD tag must fail.
	if _, err := Open(kB2P, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrAuth) {
		t.Fatalf("wrong key: got %v want ErrAuth", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenSeqMismatchRejects(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := Seal(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 5, fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	// expectSeq != env.S -> ErrSeq, BEFORE any AEAD work.
	if _, err := Open(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 4, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrSeq) {
		t.Fatalf("seq rollback: got %v want ErrSeq", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenAADBoundToSeq(t *testing.T) {
	// Same key+nonce, but env.S forged to a value != the seq sealed into the
	// AAD. expectSeq matches env.S so we pass the seq gate, but the AEAD AAD
	// (built from env.S) no longer matches what was sealed -> ErrAuth.
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := sealWithNonce(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 2, fixedNonce(), fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("sealWithNonce: %v", err)
	}
	env.S = 9
	if _, err := Open(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 9, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrAuth) {
		t.Fatalf("forged seq in AAD: got %v want ErrAuth", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenKeyIDMismatchRejects(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := Seal(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := Open(kP2B, "k_other_9999", DirP2B, 0, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrKeyMismatch) {
		t.Fatalf("keyId mismatch: got %v want ErrKeyMismatch", err)
	}
}

func TestSealWithNonceLengthGuard(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	short := make([]byte, chacha20poly1305.NonceSizeX-1)
	if _, err := sealWithNonce(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, short, fixedPlaintext); !errors.Is(err, ErrNonceLen) {
		t.Fatalf("short nonce: got %v want ErrNonceLen", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenBadNonceLengthGuard(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, _ := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	env, err := Seal(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	// Truncate the on-wire nonce -> Open must reject with ErrNonceLen.
	raw, _ := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(env.N)
	env.N = base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(raw[:len(raw)-1])
	if _, err := Open(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, env); !errors.Is(err, ErrNonceLen) {
		t.Fatalf("short on-wire nonce: got %v want ErrNonceLen", err)
	}
}

func TestDirString(t *testing.T) {
	if DirP2B.String() != "p2b" || DirB2P.String() != "b2p" {
		t.Fatalf("Dir.String: p2b=%q b2p=%q", DirP2B.String(), DirB2P.String())
	}
}

func TestAADBytes(t *testing.T) {
	got := string(aad(fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0))
	if got != "k_test_0001|p2b|0" {
		t.Fatalf("aad = %q want %q", got, "k_test_0001|p2b|0")
	}
}

// TestPrintCodecVector is the producer half of the cross-language vector.
// It seals the FIXED inputs with the FIXED nonce and prints base64(ciphertext);
// the value is recorded in doc.go (Step 6) and copied into
// app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart (T9). Also self-checks that Open of the pinned
// envelope recovers the plaintext. Run with -v to read it.
func TestPrintCodecVector(t *testing.T) {
	kP2B, _, err := DeriveKeys(fixedPSK(), fixedKeyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	env, err := sealWithNonce(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, fixedNonce(), fixedPlaintext)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("sealWithNonce: %v", err)
	}
	if env.N != base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(fixedNonce()) {
		t.Fatalf("pinned nonce not echoed: %q", env.N)
	}
	got, err := Open(kP2B, fixedKeyID, DirP2B, 0, env)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Open pinned: %v", err)
	}
	if !bytes.Equal(got, fixedPlaintext) {
		t.Fatalf("pinned round-trip mismatch: %q", got)
	}
	t.Logf("VECTOR nonce (base64)      = %s", env.N)
	t.Logf("VECTOR ciphertext (base64) = %s", env.C)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `codec.go` to make the codec test PASS (GREEN).** Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/codec.go`. `Envelope{K,S,N,C}` with json tags `k,s,n,c`; `Dir`/`DirP2B`/`DirB2P`/`String()`; the four sentinel errors; `aad(keyID,dir,seq)` building ASCII `keyID|dir|seq`; `Seal` (fresh 24-byte `crypto/rand` nonce) and `Open` (verifies `env.K==keyID` then `env.S==expectSeq`, then AEAD); and the internal `sealWithNonce` that `Seal` and the pinned vector both route through.

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"crypto/rand"
	"encoding/base64"
	"errors"
	"io"
	"strconv"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20poly1305"
)

// Sentinel errors. Callers map ALL of them to the same action: audit +
// disconnect, leaking no detail to the peer. They are matched with errors.Is.
var (
	ErrSeq         = errors.New("e2ee: sequence mismatch")
	ErrAuth        = errors.New("e2ee: auth failed")
	ErrNonceLen    = errors.New("e2ee: bad nonce length")
	ErrKeyMismatch = errors.New("e2ee: keyId mismatch")
)

// Envelope is the wire form of one encrypted frame. The JSON field names
// k,s,n,c MUST byte-match the Dart side (app/lib/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart).
// K is plaintext so the recipient can select the PSK before decrypting; it is
// also bound into the AEAD AAD so it cannot be swapped.
type Envelope struct {
	K string `json:"k"` // keyId (plaintext, selects the PSK)
	S uint64 `json:"s"` // seq, monotonic per direction
	N string `json:"n"` // nonce, base64 std, 24 bytes
	C string `json:"c"` // ciphertext+tag, base64 std
}

// Dir tags a direction; it picks the subkey and is part of the AAD.
type Dir uint8

const (
	DirP2B Dir = iota // phone -> bridge
	DirB2P            // bridge -> phone
)

// String returns the AAD/wire tag for the direction: "p2b" or "b2p".
func (d Dir) String() string {
	if d == DirB2P {
		return "b2p"
	}
	return "p2b"
}

// aad builds the additional-authenticated-data: the ASCII bytes of
// keyID|dir|seq (dir is "p2b"/"b2p", seq decimal, no padding). Unexported so
// Seal and Open stay in lockstep. The Dart side MUST produce identical bytes.
func aad(keyID string, dir Dir, seq uint64) []byte {
	out := make([]byte, 0, len(keyID)+1+3+1+20)
	out = append(out, keyID...)
	out = append(out, '|')
	out = append(out, dir.String()...)
	out = append(out, '|')
	out = strconv.AppendUint(out, seq, 10)
	return out
}

// Seal encrypts plaintext with XChaCha20-Poly1305 under key (a 32-byte
// direction subkey from DeriveKeys), using a fresh 24-byte crypto/rand nonce.
// AAD = aad(keyID,dir,seq). The returned Envelope carries K=keyID, S=seq,
// N=base64(nonce), C=base64(ciphertext+tag).
func Seal(key []byte, keyID string, dir Dir, seq uint64, plaintext []byte) (Envelope, error) {
	nonce := make([]byte, chacha20poly1305.NonceSizeX)
	if _, err := io.ReadFull(rand.Reader, nonce); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	return sealWithNonce(key, keyID, dir, seq, nonce, plaintext)
}

// sealWithNonce is the deterministic core of Seal, with the nonce injected.
// It is used by the pinned cross-language test vector (so the ciphertext is
// reproducible); production code always goes through Seal with a random nonce.
// Reusing a nonce under the same key breaks XChaCha20-Poly1305 — callers other
// than the vector test MUST NOT call this directly.
func sealWithNonce(key []byte, keyID string, dir Dir, seq uint64, nonce, plaintext []byte) (Envelope, error) {
	if len(nonce) != chacha20poly1305.NonceSizeX {
		return Envelope{}, ErrNonceLen
	}
	aead, err := chacha20poly1305.NewX(key)
	if err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, err
	}
	ct := aead.Seal(nil, nonce, plaintext, aad(keyID, dir, seq))
	return Envelope{
		K: keyID,
		S: seq,
		N: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(nonce),
		C: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(ct),
	}, nil
}

// Open decrypts env. It first verifies env.K == keyID (ErrKeyMismatch) and
// env.S == expectSeq (ErrSeq) — the caller enforces monotonicity by passing
// the next expected seq. It then decodes the nonce/ciphertext, rebuilds
// AAD = aad(keyID,dir,env.S), and runs the AEAD open (ErrAuth on tag failure).
// All errors are opaque to callers and MUST never be surfaced to the peer.
func Open(key []byte, keyID string, dir Dir, expectSeq uint64, env Envelope) ([]byte, error) {
	if env.K != keyID {
		return nil, ErrKeyMismatch
	}
	if env.S != expectSeq {
		return nil, ErrSeq
	}
	nonce, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(env.N)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrNonceLen
	}
	if len(nonce) != chacha20poly1305.NonceSizeX {
		return nil, ErrNonceLen
	}
	ct, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(env.C)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrAuth
	}
	aead, err := chacha20poly1305.NewX(key)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrAuth
	}
	plaintext, err := aead.Open(nil, nonce, ct, aad(keyID, dir, env.S))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ErrAuth
	}
	return plaintext, nil
}
```

Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/doc.go` capturing the wire envelope, AAD shape, dir constants, HKDF info strings, primitive choices, AND the pinned cross-language vector (the base64 values printed by Steps 2 and 5). The vector lives HERE (the package T9 mirrors), NOT in the approved design spec. Paste the four real base64 values you captured (kP2B, kB2P, ciphertext) into the marked lines — do not leave the placeholders.

```go
// Package e2ee is the content-plane crypto core for the phone<->bridge link.
// It is pure cryptography: no disk, no network, no Server/conn state — every
// input (PSK, keyID, subkey, seq, nonce, plaintext) is passed in, so it can be
// unit-tested in isolation and shared verbatim by the direct-LAN and hub paths.
//
// # Wire envelope (every E2EE session frame; on the wire there is ONLY this)
//
//	{ "k":"<keyId>", "s":<seq>, "n":"<24B nonce b64>", "c":"<ciphertext b64>" }
//
//   - k  plaintext keyId, only so the bridge can select the PSK; also bound
//     into the AEAD AAD so it cannot be swapped. Not a secret.
//   - s  monotonic per-direction seq; the receiver rejects a non-increasing s
//     (replay defense). Bound into the AAD.
//   - n  fresh 24-byte crypto/rand nonce per frame (XChaCha20's large nonce
//     makes random nonces safe).
//   - c  XChaCha20-Poly1305 ciphertext+tag of the compact-JSON plaintext.
//
// Recovered plaintext is the unchanged {"type":...} phone-protocol object, so
// the upper protocol is untouched by E2EE.
//
// # AAD
//
// AAD = ASCII bytes of "keyId|dir|seq" (dir ∈ {p2b,b2p}, seq decimal, no
// padding). It is NOT transmitted; both sides rebuild it. The Dart mirror
// (app/lib/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart) MUST produce identical bytes.
//
// # Key derivation
//
//	kP2B = HKDF-SHA256(psk, salt=keyId, info="codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge")
//	kB2P = HKDF-SHA256(psk, salt=keyId, info="codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone")
//
// Two subkeys so the two directions never share a nonce space. The phone
// encrypts with kP2B and decrypts with kB2P; the bridge is symmetric.
//
// # Primitives
//
//   - Content frames: XChaCha20-Poly1305 (golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20poly1305
//     NewX, 24-byte nonce). Dart mirror: package:cryptography Xchacha20.poly1305Aead.
//   - Pairing seal (NaCl box, Curve25519 + XSalsa20-Poly1305,
//     golang.org/x/crypto/nacl/box) is implemented in a later task (see
//     pairing.go, added then). Dart mirror: pinenacl (cryptography's
//     X25519+secretbox does not reproduce NaCl box's HSalsa20 KDF step).
//
// # Errors
//
// Open/Seal return only the sentinel set (ErrSeq, ErrAuth, ErrNonceLen,
// ErrKeyMismatch). Callers map ALL of them to one action: audit + disconnect,
// leaking no detail to the peer.
//
// # Pinned cross-language vector
//
// Fixed inputs: psk = 32 bytes 0x01..0x20 (byte i = i+1); keyId =
// "k_test_0001"; nonce = 24 bytes 0xA0..0xB7 (byte i = 0xA0+i); seq = 0;
// dir = p2b; plaintext = `{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}` (compact).
// The Go producer tests (TestPrintKdfVector / TestPrintCodecVector) print these
// base64 outputs; T9's Dart tests pin them byte-for-byte, proving HKDF + AEAD +
// AAD parity. Replace the <...> below with the captured values from Steps 2/5:
//
//	kP2B       (base64) = <paste from TestPrintKdfVector>
//	kB2P       (base64) = <paste from TestPrintKdfVector>
//	nonce      (base64) = oKGio6SlpqeoqaqrrK2ur7CxsrO0tba3
//	ciphertext (base64) = <paste from TestPrintCodecVector>
package e2ee
```

### T2: e2ee pairing: nacl/box seal/open + keypair gen (TDD)

**Files:**
- Create: `bridge/internal/e2ee/pairing.go`
- Test: `bridge/internal/e2ee/pairing_test.go`

**Depends on:** T1 (adds the `bridge/internal/e2ee` package, its `doc.go` package clause, and the `golang.org/x/crypto` require line to `bridge/go.mod`; T2 imports the `golang.org/x/crypto/nacl/box` subpackage of that same already-required module — no new `require` line, `go test` auto-resolves it from `go.sum`).

> Pure-crypto, no disk, no `Server`/`devstore` deps. This is the NaCl-box pairing seal used by the bridge's `handlePairInit` (a later task) to deliver `{keyId, psk}` to the phone, and by the Dart `box.open` parity tests. Per locked decision **D-d**, the Dart side opens these with `pinenacl` (byte-compatible with `x/crypto/nacl/box` / TweetNaCl). No fixed cross-language vector here (the NaCl-box nonce is `crypto/rand`); correctness is proven by round-trip + the three negative cases. `GenEnrollKeypair` is the contract-named generator; `GenDeviceKeypair` is its alias (a device identity keypair is the same Curve25519/nacl-box keypair) so the pairing handler and tests have a clearly-named device-side generator. Both are listed in the contract's `fileStructure` responsibility and the T2 summary.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test file (round-trip + 3 negative cases).** Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/pairing_test.go` with the COMPLETE contents below. It exercises `GenEnrollKeypair`, `GenDeviceKeypair`, `SealPairing`, and `OpenPairing` — none of which exist yet, so the package will not compile.

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"testing"
)

// helper: random keypairs from the real generators.
func mustEnrollKP(t *testing.T) (pub, sec *[32]byte) {
	t.Helper()
	pub, sec, err := GenEnrollKeypair()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GenEnrollKeypair: %v", err)
	}
	return pub, sec
}

func mustDeviceKP(t *testing.T) (pub, sec *[32]byte) {
	t.Helper()
	pub, sec, err := GenDeviceKeypair()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GenDeviceKeypair: %v", err)
	}
	return pub, sec
}

// Sealed PairPayload round-trips: bridge seals to the device's pub with the
// enroll secret; the device opens with the enroll pub + its own secret.
func TestSealOpenPairingRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	enrollPub, enrollSec := mustEnrollKP(t)
	devPub, devSec := mustDeviceKP(t)

	want := PairPayload{KeyID: "k_test_0001", PSK: "c29tZS1iYXNlNjQtMzJiLXBzay1nb2VzLWhlcmU9PQ=="}

	sealed, err := SealPairing(want, devPub, enrollSec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SealPairing: %v", err)
	}
	// Framing: nonce(24) || box. Box adds box.Overhead(16) over the marshalled
	// JSON, so sealed must be strictly longer than the bare nonce.
	if len(sealed) <= 24 {
		t.Fatalf("sealed too short: %d bytes (want nonce||box)", len(sealed))
	}

	got, err := OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, devSec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("OpenPairing: %v", err)
	}
	if got.KeyID != want.KeyID || got.PSK != want.PSK {
		t.Fatalf("round-trip mismatch: got %+v want %+v", got, want)
	}
}

// Opening with the WRONG sender (enroll) public key must fail authentication:
// the device-side check that "PSK came from the real machine" is load-bearing.
func TestOpenPairingWrongEnrollPubFails(t *testing.T) {
	_, enrollSec := mustEnrollKP(t)
	devPub, devSec := mustDeviceKP(t)
	otherPub, _ := mustEnrollKP(t) // a different enroll keypair

	sealed, err := SealPairing(PairPayload{KeyID: "k", PSK: "p"}, devPub, enrollSec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SealPairing: %v", err)
	}

	if _, err := OpenPairing(sealed, otherPub, devSec); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected OpenPairing to fail with wrong enroll pub, got nil error")
	}
}

// Opening with the WRONG recipient (device) secret must fail: only the device
// holding D.sec can recover the PSK.
func TestOpenPairingWrongDeviceSecFails(t *testing.T) {
	enrollPub, enrollSec := mustEnrollKP(t)
	devPub, _ := mustDeviceKP(t)
	_, otherSec := mustDeviceKP(t) // a different device keypair's secret

	sealed, err := SealPairing(PairPayload{KeyID: "k", PSK: "p"}, devPub, enrollSec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SealPairing: %v", err)
	}

	if _, err := OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, otherSec); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected OpenPairing to fail with wrong device sec, got nil error")
	}
}

// Any single-bit tamper of the sealed blob (nonce or ciphertext region) must be
// rejected by the Poly1305 tag.
func TestOpenPairingTamperedFails(t *testing.T) {
	enrollPub, enrollSec := mustEnrollKP(t)
	devPub, devSec := mustDeviceKP(t)

	sealed, err := SealPairing(PairPayload{KeyID: "k", PSK: "p"}, devPub, enrollSec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("SealPairing: %v", err)
	}

	// Flip a bit in the ciphertext region (last byte) — must fail.
	tcCipher := append([]byte(nil), sealed...)
	tcCipher[len(tcCipher)-1] ^= 0x01
	if _, err := OpenPairing(tcCipher, enrollPub, devSec); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected OpenPairing to fail on tampered ciphertext, got nil error")
	}

	// Flip a bit in the nonce region (first byte) — must also fail.
	tcNonce := append([]byte(nil), sealed...)
	tcNonce[0] ^= 0x01
	if _, err := OpenPairing(tcNonce, enrollPub, devSec); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected OpenPairing to fail on tampered nonce, got nil error")
	}

	// And a too-short blob (no room for nonce||box) must error, not panic.
	if _, err := OpenPairing(sealed[:10], enrollPub, devSec); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected OpenPairing to fail on truncated sealed blob, got nil error")
	}

	// Sanity: the untouched blob still opens (guards against a test that flipped
	// the wrong bytes or a generator that silently produces a dead keypair).
	if _, err := OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, devSec); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("untouched sealed should still open: %v", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the minimal implementation.** Create `bridge/internal/e2ee/pairing.go` with the COMPLETE contents below. Signatures, field names, and json tags are taken VERBATIM from the contract `goApi` (`PairPayload{KeyID json:"keyId", PSK json:"psk"}`, `GenEnrollKeypair() (pub, sec *[32]byte, err error)`, `SealPairing(payload PairPayload, devicePub, enrollSec *[32]byte) (sealed []byte, err error)`, `OpenPairing(sealed []byte, enrollPub, deviceSec *[32]byte) (PairPayload, error)`). Framing is `nonce(24) || box.Seal(...)` per the contract doc. `ErrPairOpen` is an additive, package-internal sentinel (the contract names no pairing-open error; tests assert only `err == nil` / `err != nil`, never the specific value, so this introduces no signature drift).

```go
package e2ee

import (
	"crypto/rand"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/nacl/box"
)

// ErrPairOpen is returned when a sealed pairing blob cannot be authenticated or
// is malformed (wrong sender/recipient key, tampered bytes, or too short to hold
// nonce||box). It is intentionally coarse: the bridge audits and disconnects
// without telling the peer which check failed.
var ErrPairOpen = errors.New("e2ee: pairing open failed")

// pairNonceLen is the NaCl box nonce size (XSalsa20-Poly1305, Curve25519).
const pairNonceLen = 24

// PairPayload is the plaintext sealed inside the nacl/box pair_result. Only the
// device holding D.sec (its secret half) can open it. KeyID selects the PSK on
// later frames; PSK is the per-device channel secret (base64 std, 32 bytes).
type PairPayload struct {
	KeyID string `json:"keyId"`
	PSK   string `json:"psk"` // base64 std, 32 bytes
}

// genBoxKeypair generates a Curve25519 / nacl-box keypair from crypto/rand.
// Both the machine enroll key and the device identity key are this same shape.
func genBoxKeypair() (pub, sec *[32]byte, err error) {
	pub, sec, err = box.GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: generate nacl box keypair: %w", err)
	}
	return pub, sec, nil
}

// GenEnrollKeypair generates the machine's long-term enroll keypair (Curve25519 /
// nacl box). pub is published in the QR; sec stays on disk 0600 and seals the
// pair_result so the device can verify the PSK came from this machine.
func GenEnrollKeypair() (pub, sec *[32]byte, err error) {
	return genBoxKeypair()
}

// GenDeviceKeypair generates a phone's long-term device identity keypair. It is
// the same Curve25519 / nacl-box shape as the enroll keypair; pub goes to the
// bridge in pair_init, sec stays in the device keychain and opens the sealed PSK.
func GenDeviceKeypair() (pub, sec *[32]byte, err error) {
	return genBoxKeypair()
}

// SealPairing seals payload to the device: box.Seal of json.Marshal(payload)
// under a fresh random 24-byte nonce, framed as nonce||box. recipient=devicePub,
// sender=enrollSec. The Dart side opens with senderPub=enrollPub (from the QR)
// and recipientSec=D.sec; this binds the PSK to the real machine (authenticated)
// and to the one device that holds D.sec (confidential).
func SealPairing(payload PairPayload, devicePub, enrollSec *[32]byte) (sealed []byte, err error) {
	if devicePub == nil || enrollSec == nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: SealPairing: nil key")
	}
	plain, err := json.Marshal(payload)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: marshal pair payload: %w", err)
	}
	var nonce [pairNonceLen]byte
	if _, err := io.ReadFull(rand.Reader, nonce[:]); err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: read pairing nonce: %w", err)
	}
	// box.Seal appends the boxed bytes to its first arg; prefix the nonce so the
	// recipient can recover it (nonce is public; secrecy comes from the box).
	sealed = box.Seal(nonce[:], plain, &nonce, devicePub, enrollSec)
	return sealed, nil
}

// OpenPairing is the inverse of SealPairing (used by the bridge's parity tests
// and as the Go reference for the Dart implementation). It splits nonce||box,
// box.Open with senderPub=enrollPub and recipientSec=deviceSec, and unmarshals
// the JSON into a PairPayload. Any failure (short blob, wrong key, tampered
// bytes) returns ErrPairOpen — opaque to the caller.
func OpenPairing(sealed []byte, enrollPub, deviceSec *[32]byte) (PairPayload, error) {
	var out PairPayload
	if enrollPub == nil || deviceSec == nil {
		return out, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: OpenPairing: nil key")
	}
	if len(sealed) < pairNonceLen {
		return out, ErrPairOpen
	}
	var nonce [pairNonceLen]byte
	copy(nonce[:], sealed[:pairNonceLen])
	plain, ok := box.Open(nil, sealed[pairNonceLen:], &nonce, enrollPub, deviceSec)
	if !ok {
		return out, ErrPairOpen
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(plain, &out); err != nil {
		return out, ErrPairOpen
	}
	return out, nil
}
```

### T3: devstore: device registry load/save/lookup/revoke + hot-reload

**Files:**
- Create: `bridge/internal/devstore/doc.go`
- Create: `bridge/internal/devstore/store.go`
- Create: `bridge/internal/devstore/watch.go`
- Test: `bridge/internal/devstore/store_test.go`

**Depends on:** `[]` (none — independent of e2ee; can run in parallel with T1/T2). Note: T4 (`pending.go`) and T5 (the pairing handler) depend on T3 because they extend the same `devstore` package, so T3 must land first.

**Contract anchors (use these EXACT names — do not rename):**
- `type Device struct` json tags: `keyId`, `name`, `devicePub`, `account`, `psk`, `added` (RFC3339).
- `func Open(path string) (*Store, error)` — loads `devices.json` into an in-memory `keyId -> Device` map (empty if file missing).
- `func (s *Store) Lookup(keyID string) (Device, bool)` — RLock map read.
- `func (s *Store) Add(d Device) error` — add/replace under Lock, then atomic 0600 persist.
- `func (s *Store) Revoke(keyID string) (bool, error)` — remove + persist; `false` if absent.
- `func (s *Store) All() []Device` — snapshot copy.
- `func (s *Store) Reload() error` — re-read into a fresh map and swap under Lock; on read/parse error KEEP the old map and return the error.
- **D-a OVERRIDES the contract's SIGHUP Watch.** The contract `goApi` literally specifies `Watch(ctx, onReload)` via `signal.Notify`/`syscall.SIGHUP`; locked decision D-a replaces it with a zero-dependency mtime poll. Implement this exact signature: `func (s *Store) Watch(ctx context.Context, interval time.Duration, onReload func(error))` — stat `devices.json`'s mtime every `interval` (prod uses ~2s); on a changed mtime call `Reload()` and report the outcome via `onReload`. NO `signal.Notify`/`syscall.SIGHUP`, NO `fsnotify`. Tests call `Reload()` directly.

> Persistence note: atomic write = write `path + ".new"` with mode `0o600`, then `os.Rename` over `path` — the exact pattern already used by `provision.setEnvKey` (`bridge/internal/provision/provision.go`, lines 90-94). House style: immutable swaps (build a brand-new map, never mutate the live one in place under read), comprehensive error wrapping with `fmt.Errorf("...: %w", err)`. T3 is path-agnostic: it never resolves the on-disk directory itself, so all tests here use `t.TempDir()`. The real `~/.codex-remote` / `%ProgramData%/codex-remote` directory (D-b) is resolved by package `provision` (the parent of `configPath()` in `reset_darwin.go`, which returns `~/.codex-remote/menubar.env`) and wired in at startup by T8 — never in this package.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing store test (round-trip, Lookup hit/miss, Add-replace, Revoke, Reload external-edit, parse-error-keeps-old-map, 0600 perms, Watch mtime poll).**
  Create `bridge/internal/devstore/store_test.go` with the COMPLETE content below. It references `Open`, `Add`, `Lookup`, `Revoke`, `All`, `Reload`, `Watch`, `Device` — none exist yet, so it will not compile (RED).

  ```go
  package devstore

  import (
  	"context"
  	"encoding/json"
  	"os"
  	"path/filepath"
  	"runtime"
  	"testing"
  	"time"
  )

  // dev builds a Device fixture with stable, distinguishable fields.
  func dev(keyID, name string) Device {
  	return Device{
  		KeyID:     keyID,
  		Name:      name,
  		DevicePub: "cHViLTMyLWJ5dGVzLWJhc2U2NC1leGFtcGxlLXBhZA==",
  		Account:   "",
  		PSK:       "cHNrLTMyLWJ5dGVzLWJhc2U2NC1leGFtcGxlLXBhZGRk",
  		Added:     time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
  	}
  }

  func TestOpenMissingFileIsEmpty(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, err := Open(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Open on missing file: %v", err)
  	}
  	if got := s.All(); len(got) != 0 {
  		t.Fatalf("expected empty store, got %d devices", len(got))
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("nope"); ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Lookup on empty store should miss")
  	}
  }

  func TestAddRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, err := Open(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	want := dev("k_aaa", "phone")
  	if err := s.Add(want); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Add: %v", err)
  	}

  	// Reopen from disk: the device must have persisted.
  	s2, err := Open(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("reopen: %v", err)
  	}
  	got, ok := s2.Lookup("k_aaa")
  	if !ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Lookup k_aaa missed after reopen")
  	}
  	if got != want {
  		t.Fatalf("round-trip mismatch:\n got=%+v\nwant=%+v", got, want)
  	}
  }

  func TestLookupHitAndMiss(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	if err := s.Add(dev("k_hit", "ipad")); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Add: %v", err)
  	}
  	if d, ok := s.Lookup("k_hit"); !ok || d.Name != "ipad" {
  		t.Fatalf("Lookup hit failed: %+v ok=%v", d, ok)
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_miss"); ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Lookup miss should return ok=false")
  	}
  }

  func TestAddReplacesSameKeyID(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_dup", "old-name"))
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_dup", "new-name"))
  	if got := s.All(); len(got) != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("Add same keyId should replace, got %d entries", len(got))
  	}
  	if d, _ := s.Lookup("k_dup"); d.Name != "new-name" {
  		t.Fatalf("replace failed, name=%q", d.Name)
  	}
  }

  func TestRevokeRemovesAndPersists(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_keep", "keep"))
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_gone", "gone"))

  	removed, err := s.Revoke("k_gone")
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke: %v", err)
  	}
  	if !removed {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke should report removed=true for present keyId")
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_gone"); ok {
  		t.Fatalf("revoked keyId still resolvable in memory")
  	}

  	// Persisted: a reopen must not see the revoked device.
  	s2, _ := Open(path)
  	if _, ok := s2.Lookup("k_gone"); ok {
  		t.Fatalf("revoked keyId still on disk after reopen")
  	}
  	if _, ok := s2.Lookup("k_keep"); !ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke wrongly dropped k_keep")
  	}

  	// Revoking an absent keyId reports removed=false, no error.
  	absent, err := s.Revoke("k_never")
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke absent: %v", err)
  	}
  	if absent {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke of absent keyId should report removed=false")
  	}
  }

  func TestReloadPicksUpExternalEdit(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_initial", "initial"))

  	// Simulate the tray/another process rewriting devices.json out-of-band.
  	external := []Device{dev("k_external", "external")}
  	raw, err := json.Marshal(external)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal external: %v", err)
  	}
  	if err := os.WriteFile(path, raw, 0o600); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("write external: %v", err)
  	}

  	if err := s.Reload(); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Reload: %v", err)
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_external"); !ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Reload did not pick up external device")
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_initial"); ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Reload should have dropped the pre-edit device")
  	}
  }

  func TestReloadParseErrorKeepsOldMap(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_good", "good"))

  	// Corrupt the file on disk.
  	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte("{ this is not json"), 0o600); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("corrupt write: %v", err)
  	}

  	if err := s.Reload(); err == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Reload on corrupt file should return an error")
  	}
  	// The old map MUST survive a parse error.
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_good"); !ok {
  		t.Fatalf("parse error dropped the good device — old map not preserved")
  	}
  }

  func TestPersistedFilePerms(t *testing.T) {
  	if runtime.GOOS == "windows" {
  		t.Skip("unix file mode bits not meaningful on Windows")
  	}
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	if err := s.Add(dev("k_perm", "perm")); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Add: %v", err)
  	}
  	fi, err := os.Stat(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("stat: %v", err)
  	}
  	if perm := fi.Mode().Perm(); perm != 0o600 {
  		t.Fatalf("devices.json perms = %o, want 600", perm)
  	}
  }

  func TestWatchReloadsOnMtimeChange(t *testing.T) {
  	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "devices.json")
  	s, _ := Open(path)
  	_ = s.Add(dev("k_before", "before"))

  	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
  	defer cancel()

  	reloaded := make(chan error, 8)
  	go s.Watch(ctx, 20*time.Millisecond, func(err error) { reloaded <- err })

  	// Rewrite the file out-of-band; bump mtime to guarantee the poll notices.
  	external := []Device{dev("k_after", "after")}
  	raw, _ := json.Marshal(external)
  	if err := os.WriteFile(path, raw, 0o600); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("external write: %v", err)
  	}
  	future := time.Now().Add(2 * time.Second)
  	if err := os.Chtimes(path, future, future); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("chtimes: %v", err)
  	}

  	select {
  	case err := <-reloaded:
  		if err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("Watch reported reload error: %v", err)
  		}
  	case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
  		t.Fatalf("Watch did not fire onReload after mtime change")
  	}
  	if _, ok := s.Lookup("k_after"); !ok {
  		t.Fatalf("Watch reload did not apply external edit")
  	}
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the package doc-comment header (the spec).**
  Create `bridge/internal/devstore/doc.go` with the COMPLETE content below (repo convention: the top-of-file package comment is authoritative).

  ```go
  // Package devstore is the on-disk registry of paired phone devices for the
  // E2EE bridge. It owns devices.json — an array of Device records, one per
  // paired phone — and serves the bridge a hot, in-memory keyId -> Device map so
  // every inbound encrypted frame can resolve its pre-shared key (PSK) without a
  // disk hit.
  //
  // # File format
  //
  // devices.json is a JSON array of Device:
  //
  //	[
  //	  {
  //	    "keyId":     "<16 random bytes, hex>",   // selects the PSK; also HKDF salt + AAD component
  //	    "name":      "<human device name>",      // audit / approval display name
  //	    "devicePub": "<base64 std, 32 bytes>",   // device's X25519 public key (pairing)
  //	    "account":   "",                          // reserved, "" for now
  //	    "psk":       "<base64 std, 32 bytes>",   // per-device pre-shared key
  //	    "added":     "<RFC3339 timestamp>"
  //	  }
  //	]
  //
  // The file lives in the machine's config directory (the same directory as
  // menubar.env: ~/.codex-remote on macOS, %ProgramData%/codex-remote on Windows;
  // resolved by package provision, NOT here). It is written with mode 0600 and
  // updated atomically (write "<path>.new", then rename over the live file) so a
  // concurrent reader never observes a half-written array.
  //
  // # In-memory model & concurrency
  //
  // Store keeps a keyId -> Device map guarded by an RWMutex. Reads (Lookup, All)
  // take the read lock; mutations (Add, Revoke, Reload) take the write lock and
  // swap in a freshly built map rather than mutating the live one in place, so a
  // reader holding a snapshot is never torn. Add and Revoke persist the whole
  // array atomically after updating memory.
  //
  // # Hot-reload
  //
  // The tray edits devices.json out-of-band (pairing a new device, revoking a
  // lost one). Watch is a zero-dependency mtime poll: it stats devices.json every
  // `interval` and, when the modification time changes, calls Reload to swap in
  // the new map, reporting each outcome through onReload for auditing. There is
  // deliberately no SIGHUP/fsnotify dependency — the poll is fully cross-platform
  // (macOS/Windows/Linux) and tests drive Reload directly.
  //
  // Reload is fail-safe: if devices.json is missing, unreadable, or unparseable,
  // Reload KEEPS the previously loaded map and returns the error (the caller
  // audit-warns; it never crashes the bridge or silently empties the registry).
  // Revocation therefore takes effect for new frames as soon as the file changes;
  // an already-open socket is dropped on its next decrypt-time Lookup miss.
  package devstore
  ```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the Store (store.go) — make every test except Watch pass.**
  Create `bridge/internal/devstore/store.go` with the COMPLETE content below.

  ```go
  package devstore

  import (
  	"encoding/json"
  	"errors"
  	"fmt"
  	"os"
  	"sync"
  )

  // Device is one paired phone, persisted as an element of devices.json.
  // The JSON field names are part of the cross-language wire contract and MUST
  // NOT change without updating every reader (bridge, tray).
  type Device struct {
  	KeyID     string `json:"keyId"`     // selects the PSK; also HKDF salt + AAD component
  	Name      string `json:"name"`      // audit / approval display name
  	DevicePub string `json:"devicePub"` // base64 std, 32 bytes (device X25519 public key)
  	Account   string `json:"account"`   // reserved, "" for now
  	PSK       string `json:"psk"`       // base64 std, 32 bytes (pre-shared key)
  	Added     string `json:"added"`     // RFC3339
  }

  // Store is the in-memory keyId -> Device registry backed by devices.json.
  // The zero value is not usable; construct one with Open.
  type Store struct {
  	path string
  	mu   sync.RWMutex
  	byID map[string]Device
  }

  // Open loads devices.json at path into an in-memory keyId -> Device map. A
  // missing file is treated as an empty registry (not an error); a present but
  // unreadable or malformed file is an error.
  func Open(path string) (*Store, error) {
  	s := &Store{path: path, byID: map[string]Device{}}
  	m, err := readDevices(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		return nil, err
  	}
  	s.byID = m
  	return s, nil
  }

  // Lookup returns the Device for keyID from the in-memory map under the read
  // lock. The bool reports whether the keyId is currently registered.
  func (s *Store) Lookup(keyID string) (Device, bool) {
  	s.mu.RLock()
  	defer s.mu.RUnlock()
  	d, ok := s.byID[keyID]
  	return d, ok
  }

  // All returns a snapshot copy of every registered device (for the tray's
  // manage/revoke list). The returned slice is owned by the caller.
  func (s *Store) All() []Device {
  	s.mu.RLock()
  	defer s.mu.RUnlock()
  	out := make([]Device, 0, len(s.byID))
  	for _, d := range s.byID {
  		out = append(out, d)
  	}
  	return out
  }

  // Add inserts or replaces d (keyed by d.KeyID) and persists the whole array
  // atomically. The in-memory map is swapped for a fresh copy so concurrent
  // readers holding a snapshot are never torn.
  func (s *Store) Add(d Device) error {
  	if d.KeyID == "" {
  		return errors.New("devstore: Add requires a non-empty keyId")
  	}
  	s.mu.Lock()
  	defer s.mu.Unlock()
  	next := cloneMap(s.byID)
  	next[d.KeyID] = d
  	if err := writeDevices(s.path, next); err != nil {
  		return err
  	}
  	s.byID = next
  	return nil
  }

  // Revoke removes keyID and persists. It returns (false, nil) if the keyId was
  // not registered (a no-op), (true, nil) on success, and the error untouched if
  // persistence failed (the in-memory map is left as it was on a write error).
  func (s *Store) Revoke(keyID string) (bool, error) {
  	s.mu.Lock()
  	defer s.mu.Unlock()
  	if _, ok := s.byID[keyID]; !ok {
  		return false, nil
  	}
  	next := cloneMap(s.byID)
  	delete(next, keyID)
  	if err := writeDevices(s.path, next); err != nil {
  		return false, err
  	}
  	s.byID = next
  	return true, nil
  }

  // Reload re-reads devices.json into a fresh map and swaps it under the write
  // lock. On any read/parse error it KEEPS the existing map and returns the
  // error, so a corrupt or transiently-unreadable file never empties the live
  // registry.
  func (s *Store) Reload() error {
  	m, err := readDevices(s.path)
  	if err != nil {
  		return err
  	}
  	s.mu.Lock()
  	s.byID = m
  	s.mu.Unlock()
  	return nil
  }

  // readDevices loads path into a keyId -> Device map. A missing file yields an
  // empty map and no error.
  func readDevices(path string) (map[string]Device, error) {
  	b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		if errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist) {
  			return map[string]Device{}, nil
  		}
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("devstore: read %s: %w", path, err)
  	}
  	var arr []Device
  	if len(b) > 0 {
  		if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &arr); err != nil {
  			return nil, fmt.Errorf("devstore: parse %s: %w", path, err)
  		}
  	}
  	m := make(map[string]Device, len(arr))
  	for _, d := range arr {
  		if d.KeyID == "" {
  			continue
  		}
  		m[d.KeyID] = d
  	}
  	return m, nil
  }

  // writeDevices persists the map as a JSON array, atomically and 0600 (write a
  // sibling ".new" then rename over the target — the house atomic-write pattern,
  // mirroring provision.setEnvKey).
  func writeDevices(path string, m map[string]Device) error {
  	arr := make([]Device, 0, len(m))
  	for _, d := range m {
  		arr = append(arr, d)
  	}
  	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(arr, "", "  ")
  	if err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("devstore: marshal: %w", err)
  	}
  	tmp := path + ".new"
  	if err := os.WriteFile(tmp, b, 0o600); err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("devstore: write %s: %w", tmp, err)
  	}
  	if err := os.Rename(tmp, path); err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("devstore: rename %s -> %s: %w", tmp, path, err)
  	}
  	return nil
  }

  // cloneMap returns a shallow copy of m (Device is a value type, so this is a
  // full copy). Used to build the next map for an immutable swap.
  func cloneMap(m map[string]Device) map[string]Device {
  	out := make(map[string]Device, len(m)+1)
  	for k, v := range m {
  		out[k] = v
  	}
  	return out
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement Watch (watch.go) — make TestWatchReloadsOnMtimeChange pass.**
  Create `bridge/internal/devstore/watch.go` with the COMPLETE content below. This is the D-a zero-dependency mtime poll — no signal/fsnotify imports.

  ```go
  package devstore

  import (
  	"context"
  	"os"
  	"time"
  )

  // Watch blocks until ctx is done, polling devices.json's modification time
  // every interval. Whenever the mtime changes (the tray paired or revoked a
  // device out-of-band), it calls Reload and reports the outcome via onReload so
  // the caller can audit each reload. This is a deliberately dependency-free
  // hot-reload: no SIGHUP, no fsnotify — just a cross-platform stat poll.
  //
  // A non-existent or transiently-unreadable file is treated as an unchanged
  // mtime (zero time), so Watch neither fires nor errors until the file appears
  // and stabilizes. onReload is invoked once per detected change with the result
  // of Reload (nil on success; the Reload error if the new file failed to parse,
  // in which case the previous in-memory map is preserved by Reload itself).
  func (s *Store) Watch(ctx context.Context, interval time.Duration, onReload func(error)) {
  	if interval <= 0 {
  		interval = 2 * time.Second
  	}
  	t := time.NewTicker(interval)
  	defer t.Stop()

  	last := mtimeOf(s.path)
  	for {
  		select {
  		case <-ctx.Done():
  			return
  		case <-t.C:
  			cur := mtimeOf(s.path)
  			if cur.Equal(last) {
  				continue
  			}
  			last = cur
  			err := s.Reload()
  			if onReload != nil {
  				onReload(err)
  			}
  		}
  	}
  }

  // mtimeOf returns the file's modification time, or the zero Time if the file is
  // missing/unstattable (so Watch treats "absent" as a stable, non-triggering
  // state).
  func mtimeOf(path string) time.Time {
  	fi, err := os.Stat(path)
  	if err != nil {
  		return time.Time{}
  	}
  	return fi.ModTime()
  }
  ```

### T4: devstore: pending-pairings single-use consumption

Adds the `pending-pairings.json` store to the existing `devstore` package (created in T3). The tray writes a one-time `pairingId` (via `AddPending`); the bridge's pairing handler (T5) atomically `Consume`s it on a `pair_init`. Consumption is single-use and three-state: a valid id succeeds and is removed; an expired id returns `ErrPairExpired`; an absent OR already-consumed id returns `ErrPairUnknown` (per locked decision **D-e** — `Consume` cannot distinguish a never-existed id from a consumed one, so reuse reads back as `unknown`; the on-wire `pair_error` reasons in v1 are `unknown|expired` only, and the app shows the same `二维码失效，请重新生成` for both). Persistence uses the same atomic `.new`+`rename`, `0o600` pattern as `provision.setEnvKey` (the file is operator-sensitive: it lives in provision's config dir per **D-b**, next to `devices.json`). Every `AddPending`/`Consume` purges already-expired entries so the file self-cleans. All public signatures and field names/json tags are taken verbatim from `.contract.goApi` (pkg `devstore`) and `.contract.dataFormats` (`pending-pairings.json`) — do not rename.

**Files:**
- Create: `bridge/internal/devstore/pending.go`
- Test:   `bridge/internal/devstore/pending_test.go`

**Depends on:** T3 (devstore package: `doc.go` + `store.go` establish `package devstore`, the atomic-write convention, and the config-dir ownership; T4 only ADDS `pending.go` to that package — it does not modify any T3 file).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing three-state + single-use test.** Create `bridge/internal/devstore/pending_test.go` with the COMPLETE code below. It pins the contract exactly: `OpenPending(path)` (no error), `AddPending(id, ttl)`, `Consume(id, now)` taking an injectable `now` (so expiry is deterministic — no `time.Sleep`), the sentinels `ErrPairUnknown`/`ErrPairExpired`, the `0o600` mode, and a concurrent double-`Consume` race that must yield exactly one success. Uses only stdlib `testing` + `t.TempDir()`, table-driven + `t.Run` + `errors.Is`, per repo test conventions (no testify).

  Note: `AddPending` stamps `ExpiresAt` from the real wall clock (its signature takes only `(pairingID, ttl)` — no injectable clock), so it CANNOT produce an entry that is expired relative to a fixed past `now`. The three-state test therefore seeds `valid-1`/`expired-1` through the unexported `save()` with absolute `ExpiresAt` values computed from the fixed `now`, making expiry a pure function of the injected `Consume` clock (the same seeding pattern used by `TestAddPendingPurgesExpiredAndPerms`).

```go
package devstore

import (
	"errors"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

func TestPendingConsumeThreeState(t *testing.T) {
	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "pending-pairings.json")
	p := OpenPending(path)

	now := time.Date(2026, 6, 8, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)

	// Seed deterministic absolute ExpiresAt values relative to the FIXED `now`
	// via the unexported save(): AddPending stamps ExpiresAt from the real wall
	// clock (it takes no injectable clock), so it cannot produce an entry that
	// is "expired" relative to a fixed past `now`. save() lets the expiry be a
	// pure function of the injected Consume clock.
	seed := []Pending{
		{PairingID: "valid-1", ExpiresAt: now.Add(5 * time.Minute).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)},
		{PairingID: "expired-1", ExpiresAt: now.Add(1 * time.Minute).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)},
	}
	if err := p.save(seed); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("seed save: %v", err)
	}

	cases := []struct {
		name      string
		pairingID string
		now       time.Time
		wantErr   error // nil = success
	}{
		// expired-1's ExpiresAt is now+1min; consuming 2min after now must fail expired.
		{"expired -> ErrPairExpired", "expired-1", now.Add(2 * time.Minute), ErrPairExpired},
		// valid-1's ExpiresAt is now+5min; consuming at now succeeds and removes it.
		{"valid -> success + removed", "valid-1", now, nil},
		// reusing the just-consumed valid id reads back as unknown (D-e).
		{"reused -> ErrPairUnknown", "valid-1", now, ErrPairUnknown},
		// an id that never existed is also unknown.
		{"absent -> ErrPairUnknown", "never-seen", now, ErrPairUnknown},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			err := p.Consume(c.pairingID, c.now)
			if c.wantErr == nil {
				if err != nil {
					t.Fatalf("Consume(%q) = %v, want nil", c.pairingID, err)
				}
				return
			}
			if !errors.Is(err, c.wantErr) {
				t.Fatalf("Consume(%q) = %v, want %v", c.pairingID, err, c.wantErr)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestPendingConsumeSingleUseConcurrent(t *testing.T) {
	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "pending-pairings.json")
	p := OpenPending(path)
	if err := p.AddPending("race-1", 5*time.Minute); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
	}
	now := time.Now()

	const racers = 16
	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	wg.Add(racers)
	var mu sync.Mutex
	var ok, unknown int
	for i := 0; i < racers; i++ {
		go func() {
			defer wg.Done()
			err := p.Consume("race-1", now)
			mu.Lock()
			defer mu.Unlock()
			switch {
			case err == nil:
				ok++
			case errors.Is(err, ErrPairUnknown):
				unknown++
			default:
				t.Errorf("Consume race = unexpected error %v", err)
			}
		}()
	}
	wg.Wait()
	if ok != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("concurrent double-consume: got %d successes, want exactly 1", ok)
	}
	if unknown != racers-1 {
		t.Fatalf("concurrent double-consume: got %d unknowns, want %d", unknown, racers-1)
	}
}

func TestAddPendingPurgesExpiredAndPerms(t *testing.T) {
	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "pending-pairings.json")
	p := OpenPending(path)

	now := time.Now()
	// Hand-write a file containing one already-expired and one live entry.
	seed := []Pending{
		{PairingID: "stale", ExpiresAt: now.Add(-1 * time.Hour).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)},
		{PairingID: "live", ExpiresAt: now.Add(1 * time.Hour).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)},
	}
	if err := p.save(seed); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("seed save: %v", err)
	}

	// AddPending must purge the expired "stale" entry while persisting.
	if err := p.AddPending("fresh", 5*time.Minute); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
	}

	got, err := p.load()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("load: %v", err)
	}
	ids := map[string]bool{}
	for _, e := range got {
		ids[e.PairingID] = true
	}
	if ids["stale"] {
		t.Fatalf("expired entry was not purged: %+v", got)
	}
	if !ids["live"] || !ids["fresh"] {
		t.Fatalf("live/fresh entries missing: %+v", got)
	}

	info, err := os.Stat(path)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("stat: %v", err)
	}
	if perm := info.Mode().Perm(); perm != 0o600 {
		t.Fatalf("pending-pairings.json perm = %o, want 600", perm)
	}

	// Consuming the still-live entry succeeds; the purged one is unknown.
	if err := p.Consume("live", now); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Consume(live) = %v, want nil", err)
	}
	if err := p.Consume("stale", now); !errors.Is(err, ErrPairUnknown) {
		t.Fatalf("Consume(stale) = %v, want ErrPairUnknown", err)
	}
}

func TestOpenPendingMissingFile(t *testing.T) {
	// A non-existent file is an empty store, not an error.
	path := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "does-not-exist.json")
	p := OpenPending(path)
	if err := p.Consume("anything", time.Now()); !errors.Is(err, ErrPairUnknown) {
		t.Fatalf("Consume on empty store = %v, want ErrPairUnknown", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `pending.go` (minimal, GREEN).** Create `bridge/internal/devstore/pending.go` with the COMPLETE code below. `Pending`/`PendingStore`/`OpenPending`/`AddPending`/`Consume` and the `ErrPairUnknown`/`ErrPairExpired` sentinels match `.contract.goApi` verbatim (json tags `pairingId`/`expiresAt`, RFC3339, `0o600`). `load`/`save` are unexported helpers (used by the test and by `AddPending`/`Consume`); `save` writes atomically via `path+".new"` + `os.Rename` exactly like `provision.setEnvKey`. A missing file loads as an empty slice (not an error). `Consume` and `AddPending` both hold `mu` for the whole read-modify-write so concurrent callers serialize — that is what makes consumption single-use. The top-of-file comment is the spec for this file (repo convention).

```go
// Package devstore — pending-pairings store.
//
// pending.go manages pending-pairings.json (a JSON array of Pending), the
// one-time-pairing ledger that lives next to devices.json in provision's config
// dir (0600). The tray writes a fresh pairingId + TTL via AddPending; the
// bridge's pair_init handler atomically Consumes it.
//
// Single-use is the security property: Consume holds the store mutex across the
// whole re-read / find / remove / persist cycle, so two concurrent Consume calls
// for the same id yield exactly one success — the loser sees the id already gone.
//
// Three-state result (mapped by the caller to pair_error reasons unknown|expired):
//   - present & not expired -> remove + persist, return nil (success)
//   - present but ExpiresAt <= now -> ErrPairExpired (left for purge)
//   - absent (never existed OR already consumed) -> ErrPairUnknown
//
// Consume cannot distinguish "never existed" from "already consumed": both are
// absent after the fact, so reuse reads back as ErrPairUnknown. The wire only
// surfaces unknown|expired; the app shows one message for both. Every mutation
// purges already-expired entries so the file self-cleans. All writes are atomic
// (.new + rename, 0600), mirroring provision.setEnvKey.
package devstore

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"os"
	"sync"
	"time"
)

// Pending is one outstanding one-time pairing offer, persisted in
// pending-pairings.json. ExpiresAt is an RFC3339 timestamp.
type Pending struct {
	PairingID string `json:"pairingId"`
	ExpiresAt string `json:"expiresAt"` // RFC3339
}

// Pairing-consumption errors. Callers map these to the pair_error wire reasons
// unknown|expired; a reused (already-consumed) id reads back as ErrPairUnknown.
var (
	ErrPairUnknown = errors.New("devstore: unknown pairingId")
	ErrPairExpired = errors.New("devstore: pairing expired")
)

// PendingStore guards pending-pairings.json. It is stateless beyond the file
// path: every operation re-reads the file under the mutex, so it stays correct
// even if another process (the tray) appended an entry between calls.
type PendingStore struct {
	path string
	mu   sync.Mutex
}

// OpenPending returns a PendingStore bound to path. The file need not exist yet;
// a missing file behaves as an empty store. There is no I/O here, so it cannot
// fail (matching the contract's error-free signature).
func OpenPending(path string) *PendingStore {
	return &PendingStore{path: path}
}

// AddPending appends a Pending with ExpiresAt = now+ttl, purges any already
// expired entries, and persists atomically (0600). Used by the tray's
// 配对新设备 to register a one-time pairingId before showing the QR.
func (p *PendingStore) AddPending(pairingID string, ttl time.Duration) error {
	p.mu.Lock()
	defer p.mu.Unlock()

	list, err := p.load()
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	now := time.Now()
	kept := purgeExpired(list, now)
	kept = append(kept, Pending{
		PairingID: pairingID,
		ExpiresAt: now.Add(ttl).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
	})
	return p.save(kept)
}

// Consume is single-use: under the mutex it re-reads the file, finds pairingID,
// and either removes+persists it (returning nil) or returns ErrPairExpired /
// ErrPairUnknown. now is injected so callers/tests control the expiry clock.
// Expired entries are purged on the way out so the file self-cleans.
func (p *PendingStore) Consume(pairingID string, now time.Time) error {
	p.mu.Lock()
	defer p.mu.Unlock()

	list, err := p.load()
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}

	idx := -1
	for i := range list {
		if list[i].PairingID == pairingID {
			idx = i
			break
		}
	}
	if idx < 0 {
		// Absent: never existed or already consumed. Still persist a purge of
		// any stale neighbours so the file does not grow unbounded.
		if kept := purgeExpired(list, now); len(kept) != len(list) {
			_ = p.save(kept)
		}
		return ErrPairUnknown
	}

	match := list[idx]
	exp, perr := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, match.ExpiresAt)
	if perr != nil || !exp.After(now) {
		// Expired (or unparseable -> treat as expired). Leave it for purge; a
		// retry would also see expired until purged, then unknown.
		return ErrPairExpired
	}

	// Success: remove the consumed id AND any expired neighbours, then persist.
	kept := make([]Pending, 0, len(list))
	for i := range list {
		if i == idx {
			continue
		}
		kept = append(kept, list[i])
	}
	kept = purgeExpired(kept, now)
	if err := p.save(kept); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	return nil
}

// purgeExpired returns the entries whose ExpiresAt is strictly after now.
// Unparseable timestamps are dropped (treated as expired).
func purgeExpired(list []Pending, now time.Time) []Pending {
	kept := make([]Pending, 0, len(list))
	for _, e := range list {
		exp, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, e.ExpiresAt)
		if err != nil {
			continue
		}
		if exp.After(now) {
			kept = append(kept, e)
		}
	}
	return kept
}

// load reads pending-pairings.json into a slice. A missing file yields an empty
// slice and no error; a malformed file surfaces the parse error to the caller.
func (p *PendingStore) load() ([]Pending, error) {
	b, err := os.ReadFile(p.path)
	if err != nil {
		if errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist) {
			return nil, nil
		}
		return nil, err
	}
	if len(b) == 0 {
		return nil, nil
	}
	var list []Pending
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &list); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return list, nil
}

// save marshals list and writes it atomically: write path+".new" (0600) then
// rename over path. Mirrors provision.setEnvKey's atomic-write convention.
func (p *PendingStore) save(list []Pending) error {
	if list == nil {
		list = []Pending{}
	}
	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(list, "", "  ")
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	tmp := p.path + ".new"
	if err := os.WriteFile(tmp, b, 0o600); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	return os.Rename(tmp, p.path)
}
```

### T5: Bridge pairing handler + first-frame router

Adds the bridge-side pairing handshake and the hard-cut first-frame classifier. `handlePairInit` consumes a one-time `pairingId` (T4 `PendingStore.Consume`), mints a `keyId`+`psk`, persists a `Device` (T3 `Store.Add`), seals `PairPayload{keyId,psk}` to the phone's device pubkey with the machine enroll secret (T2 `e2ee.SealPairing`), and returns a `PairResult`; consume failure returns a coarse `*PairError`. `firstFrameKind` classifies a connection's first frame for the §7.4 hard-cut router (`pair_init` | `envelope` | `reject`). This task only adds the handler + classifier and the three additive `Server` fields they need — wiring them into the read/write loops (direct mode) is T6, hub mode is T7, prod construction is T8.

**Files:**
- Create: `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing.go`
- Modify: `bridge/internal/bridge/server.go` (add 3 additive `Server` fields + `EnableE2EE` helper — per D-g; T6 reuses, does NOT re-add them)
- Test: `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing_test.go`

**Depends on:** T2 (`e2ee.PairPayload`/`SealPairing`/`OpenPairing`/`GenDeviceKeypair`), T3 (`devstore.Store`/`Device`), T4 (`devstore.PendingStore.Consume` + `ErrPairUnknown`/`ErrPairExpired`).

  ```go
  // EnableE2EE puts the server into E2EE mode by wiring the device store, the
  // machine enroll secret, and the pending-pairings store (D-g: additive, no
  // breaking change to NewServer). Prod wiring lives in T8 (cmd/codexbridge serve
  // loads enroll.key, opens the stores, calls EnableE2EE, starts devstore.Watch);
  // tests set the fields struct-literally. e2eeOn() reports whether E2EE is active.
  func (s *Server) EnableE2EE(devices *devstore.Store, enrollSec *[32]byte, pending *devstore.PendingStore) {
  	s.devices = devices
  	s.enrollSec = enrollSec
  	s.pending = pending
  }

  // e2eeOn reports whether the bridge has been E2EE-provisioned. Once true the
  // hard-cut is mandatory (D-c): there is no per-session legacy fallback.
  func (s *Server) e2eeOn() bool { return s.devices != nil && s.enrollSec != nil && s.pending != nil }
  ```
