- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test `pairing_test.go`.**
  TDD RED. Table-driven per the house convention (no testify). Covers: `firstFrameKind` classification table; `handlePairInit` valid / expired / consumed (reused) / unknown / bad-devicePub paths; device persisted with the exact `DevicePub`; and the sealed `PairResult` opens ONLY with the matching device secret (and fails with a wrong secret). Build the `Server` struct-literally (D-g) wiring real `devstore` + `e2ee` from temp files.

  The expired case is seeded by writing `pending-pairings.json` DIRECTLY with a past `expiresAt`, NOT via `AddPending(id, -ttl)`: `AddPending` purges already-expired entries on write (T4 contract), so a negative TTL never persists and would read back as `unknown`, not `expired`. `Consume` re-reads the file on every call (T4 contract), so a hand-written expired entry deterministically hits the `ErrPairExpired` branch. `newE2EEServer` therefore also returns the temp `dir` so the expired test can write that file.

  Create `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing_test.go`:
  ```go
  package bridge

  import (
  	"encoding/base64"
  	"encoding/json"
  	"os"
  	"path/filepath"
  	"testing"
  	"time"

  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/devstore"
  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/e2ee"
  )

  // newE2EEServer builds a struct-literal Server in E2EE mode backed by real
  // devstore files in a temp dir, plus a freshly generated enroll keypair and one
  // device keypair (the "phone"). It returns the server, the temp dir (so a test
  // can write pending-pairings.json directly), the enroll public key (what the
  // phone gets from the QR), and the device key pair.
  func newE2EEServer(t *testing.T) (s *Server, dir string, enrollPub, devicePub, deviceSec *[32]byte) {
  	t.Helper()
  	dir = t.TempDir()
  	store, err := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))
  	ePub, eSec, err := e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenEnrollKeypair: %v", err)
  	}
  	dPub, dSec, err := e2ee.GenDeviceKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenDeviceKeypair: %v", err)
  	}
  	s = &Server{devices: store, enrollSec: eSec, pending: pending}
  	return s, dir, ePub, dPub, dSec
  }

  func devicePubB64(t *testing.T, pub *[32]byte) string {
  	t.Helper()
  	return base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(pub[:])
  }

  // firstFrameKind classifies the connection's FIRST frame for the §7.4 hard-cut
  // router. pair_init (plaintext, type=="pair_init") and an envelope (k+n+c) are
  // the only accepted first frames; anything else rejects -> disconnect.
  func TestFirstFrameKind(t *testing.T) {
  	cases := []struct {
  		name string
  		raw  string
  		want string
  	}{
  		{"pair_init", `{"type":"pair_init","pairingId":"p1","devicePub":"AAAA","name":"iPhone"}`, "pair_init"},
  		{"envelope", `{"k":"k_abc","s":0,"n":"bm9uY2U=","c":"Y2lwaGVy"}`, "envelope"},
  		{"envelope_extra_seq", `{"k":"k_abc","s":7,"n":"bm9uY2U=","c":"Y2lwaGVy","x":1}`, "envelope"},
  		{"cleartext_list", `{"type":"list"}`, "reject"},
  		{"cleartext_prompt", `{"type":"prompt","text":"hi"}`, "reject"},
  		{"envelope_missing_c", `{"k":"k_abc","s":0,"n":"bm9uY2U="}`, "reject"},
  		{"pair_result_not_first", `{"type":"pair_result","sealed":"AAAA"}`, "reject"},
  		{"empty_object", `{}`, "reject"},
  		{"not_json", `not json`, "reject"},
  		{"json_array", `[1,2,3]`, "reject"},
  	}
  	for _, c := range cases {
  		if got := firstFrameKind([]byte(c.raw)); got != c.want {
  			t.Errorf("firstFrameKind(%s) = %q, want %q", c.name, got, c.want)
  		}
  	}
  }

  // handlePairInit must: consume the pairingId once, mint keyId+psk, persist a
  // Device carrying the phone's DevicePub, and return a PairResult whose sealed
  // blob the phone can open with its device secret to recover {keyId, psk}.
  func TestHandlePairInitValid(t *testing.T) {
  	s, _, enrollPub, devicePub, deviceSec := newE2EEServer(t)
  	if err := s.pending.AddPending("p-valid", time.Minute); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
  	}

  	res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{
  		Type:      "pair_init",
  		PairingID: "p-valid",
  		DevicePub: devicePubB64(t, devicePub),
  		Name:      "我的 iPhone",
  	})
  	if perr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("handlePairInit returned error: %+v", perr)
  	}
  	if res.Type != "pair_result" || res.Sealed == "" {
  		t.Fatalf("bad PairResult: %+v", res)
  	}

  	// The device must be persisted with the exact DevicePub the phone sent, and
  	// exactly one device exists.
  	all := s.devices.All()
  	if len(all) != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("got %d devices, want 1", len(all))
  	}
  	d := all[0]
  	if d.DevicePub != devicePubB64(t, devicePub) {
  		t.Fatalf("persisted DevicePub = %q, want %q", d.DevicePub, devicePubB64(t, devicePub))
  	}
  	if d.Name != "我的 iPhone" || d.Account != "" || d.KeyID == "" || d.PSK == "" {
  		t.Fatalf("persisted Device fields off: %+v", d)
  	}
  	// keyId is NewToken()-style: 16 random bytes hex == 32 hex chars (D-f).
  	if len(d.KeyID) != 32 {
  		t.Fatalf("keyId len = %d, want 32 hex chars", len(d.KeyID))
  	}
  	// PSK is base64-std of 32 bytes.
  	pskBytes, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(d.PSK)
  	if err != nil || len(pskBytes) != 32 {
  		t.Fatalf("PSK not base64(32B): err=%v len=%d", err, len(pskBytes))
  	}

  	// The sealed blob opens ONLY with the matching device secret, and yields the
  	// SAME keyId+psk that was persisted.
  	sealed, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(res.Sealed)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("decode sealed: %v", err)
  	}
  	payload, err := e2ee.OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, deviceSec)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("OpenPairing with matching device sec failed: %v", err)
  	}
  	if payload.KeyID != d.KeyID || payload.PSK != d.PSK {
  		t.Fatalf("sealed payload {%s,%s} != persisted {%s,%s}", payload.KeyID, payload.PSK, d.KeyID, d.PSK)
  	}

  	// A WRONG device secret must NOT open the sealed result. GenDeviceKeypair
  	// returns (pub, sec, err) — discard pub, keep the wrong sec.
  	_, wrongSec, err := e2ee.GenDeviceKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenDeviceKeypair: %v", err)
  	}
  	if _, err := e2ee.OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, wrongSec); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("sealed result opened with the WRONG device secret")
  	}
  }

  // An expired pairingId must yield PairError reason "expired" and persist nothing.
  // We write pending-pairings.json directly with a past expiresAt (Consume re-reads
  // the file on every call), because AddPending purges already-expired entries on
  // write — so AddPending(id, -ttl) would store nothing and read back as "unknown".
  func TestHandlePairInitExpired(t *testing.T) {
  	s, dir, _, devicePub, _ := newE2EEServer(t)
  	past := time.Now().Add(-time.Hour).UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)
  	entry := []map[string]string{{"pairingId": "p-exp", "expiresAt": past}}
  	raw, err := json.Marshal(entry)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal pending: %v", err)
  	}
  	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"), raw, 0o600); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("write pending: %v", err)
  	}

  	res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{
  		Type:      "pair_init",
  		PairingID: "p-exp",
  		DevicePub: devicePubB64(t, devicePub),
  		Name:      "iPhone",
  	})
  	if perr == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("expected PairError, got PairResult %+v", res)
  	}
  	if perr.Type != "pair_error" || perr.Reason != "expired" {
  		t.Fatalf("PairError = %+v, want reason=expired", perr)
  	}
  	if n := len(s.devices.All()); n != 0 {
  		t.Fatalf("expired pairing persisted %d devices, want 0", n)
  	}
  }

  // A reused (already-consumed) pairingId reads back as ErrPairUnknown, so the
  // wire reason is "unknown" (D-e: consumed collapses to unknown in v1).
  func TestHandlePairInitConsumed(t *testing.T) {
  	s, _, _, devicePub, _ := newE2EEServer(t)
  	if err := s.pending.AddPending("p-once", time.Minute); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
  	}
  	pub := devicePubB64(t, devicePub)
  	if _, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{Type: "pair_init", PairingID: "p-once", DevicePub: pub, Name: "iPhone"}); perr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("first pair failed: %+v", perr)
  	}
  	// Second use of the same id: consumed -> reads back unknown.
  	res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{Type: "pair_init", PairingID: "p-once", DevicePub: pub, Name: "iPhone-2"})
  	if perr == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("reused pairingId accepted, got PairResult %+v", res)
  	}
  	if perr.Reason != "unknown" {
  		t.Fatalf("reused reason = %q, want unknown", perr.Reason)
  	}
  	// Still exactly one device (the second attempt persisted nothing).
  	if n := len(s.devices.All()); n != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("after reuse got %d devices, want 1", n)
  	}
  }

  // A never-seen pairingId is reason "unknown".
  func TestHandlePairInitUnknown(t *testing.T) {
  	s, _, _, devicePub, _ := newE2EEServer(t)
  	res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{
  		Type:      "pair_init",
  		PairingID: "never-existed",
  		DevicePub: devicePubB64(t, devicePub),
  		Name:      "iPhone",
  	})
  	if perr == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("unknown pairingId accepted, got %+v", res)
  	}
  	if perr.Reason != "unknown" {
  		t.Fatalf("reason = %q, want unknown", perr.Reason)
  	}
  }

  // A malformed DevicePub (not base64, or wrong length) must be refused with a
  // coarse reason BEFORE the pairingId is burned — the phone can retry the QR.
  func TestHandlePairInitBadDevicePub(t *testing.T) {
  	s, _, _, _, _ := newE2EEServer(t)
  	if err := s.pending.AddPending("p-bad", time.Minute); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
  	}
  	for _, bad := range []string{"", "!!!not-base64!!!", base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte("short"))} {
  		res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{Type: "pair_init", PairingID: "p-bad", DevicePub: bad, Name: "iPhone"})
  		if perr == nil {
  			t.Fatalf("bad devicePub %q accepted: %+v", bad, res)
  		}
  		if perr.Reason != "unknown" {
  			t.Fatalf("bad devicePub %q reason = %q, want unknown", bad, perr.Reason)
  		}
  	}
  	// pairingId must NOT have been consumed by the bad-pub attempts.
  	if err := s.pending.Consume("p-bad", time.Now()); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("pairingId burned by bad-devicePub attempt: %v", err)
  	}
  	// And nothing was persisted.
  	if n := len(s.devices.All()); n != 0 {
  		t.Fatalf("bad devicePub persisted %d devices, want 0", n)
  	}
  	// sanity: PairResult/PairError JSON tags match the wire contract.
  	pe := PairError{Type: "pair_error", Reason: "unknown"}
  	b, _ := json.Marshal(pe)
  	if string(b) != `{"type":"pair_error","reason":"unknown"}` {
  		t.Fatalf("PairError json = %s", b)
  	}
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `pairing.go` (minimal GREEN).**
  Create `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing.go`. `handlePairInit` validates `DevicePub` first (so a malformed pub can't burn the pairingId), then `Consume`s, then mints `keyId`+`psk`, persists the `Device`, and seals. All failures map to a coarse `*PairError` reason per D-e (`expired` for `ErrPairExpired`, else `unknown`). `firstFrameKind` peeks the raw JSON for the §7.4 router. This is the only file in T5 that imports `e2ee`. Everything is audited; no internal detail leaks to the peer.
  ```go
  package bridge

  import (
  	"crypto/rand"
  	"encoding/base64"
  	"encoding/json"
  	"errors"
  	"time"

  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/devstore"
  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/e2ee"
  )

  // Pairing frames (plaintext; the payload carries only public keys / a sealed
  // blob — never a secret in the clear). They are the ONLY plaintext exchange the
  // bridge accepts once E2EE-provisioned (§7.4 hard-cut): a connection's first frame
  // is either a pair_init (one-shot handshake, then the socket closes) or an E2EE
  // envelope {k,s,n,c}; any other cleartext first frame is rejected and the socket
  // dropped. The routing for that lives in T6 (direct) / T7 (hub); this file owns
  // the handshake itself plus firstFrameKind, the classifier those routers call.
  //
  // pair_result.Sealed is box(enrollSec -> devicePub){keyId, psk}: only the device
  // holding D.sec can open it, and the device verifies the sender is the real
  // machine via the enrollPub it carries in the QR (D5/§6.3). PairError stays coarse
  // (D-e): a reused/consumed pairingId is indistinguishable from a never-seen one
  // (Consume returns ErrPairUnknown for both), so v1's wire reasons are
  // unknown|expired only; the app shows the same "二维码失效，请重新生成" for all.

  // PairInit is the phone's plaintext pairing request (first frame).
  type PairInit struct {
  	Type      string `json:"type"`      // "pair_init"
  	PairingID string `json:"pairingId"` // one-time, consumed on success
  	DevicePub string `json:"devicePub"` // base64 std, 32 bytes (long-term device identity)
  	Name      string `json:"name"`      // human label for the device list / audit
  }

  // PairResult is the bridge's success reply: a sealed {keyId, psk} only the phone
  // (holding its device secret) can open.
  type PairResult struct {
  	Type   string `json:"type"`   // "pair_result"
  	Sealed string `json:"sealed"` // base64 std of nonce||box({keyId,psk})
  }

  // PairError is the bridge's coarse failure reply (D-e: unknown|expired in v1).
  type PairError struct {
  	Type   string `json:"type"`   // "pair_error"
  	Reason string `json:"reason"` // "unknown" | "expired" ("consumed" reserved, reads as unknown)
  }

  // pairError builds a PairError for the given reason, audited with context.
  func (s *Server) pairError(pairingID, reason, detail string) *PairError {
  	s.audit("pair FAILED pairingId=%s reason=%s (%s)", trunc(pairingID, 64), reason, detail)
  	return &PairError{Type: "pair_error", Reason: reason}
  }

  // handlePairInit runs the bridge side of the one-shot pairing handshake:
  //
  //  1. parse + bound-check the phone's DevicePub (32 raw bytes) FIRST, so a
  //     malformed request can't consume (burn) the one-time pairingId;
  //  2. Consume the pairingId exactly once (ErrPairExpired -> "expired",
  //     ErrPairUnknown / consumed -> "unknown");
  //  3. mint a fresh keyId (NewToken-style hex, D-f) + a 32-byte PSK from crypto/rand;
  //  4. persist the Device (DevicePub, Name, Account="" reserved) via the store,
  //     which writes devices.json atomically (0600);
  //  5. seal PairPayload{keyId, psk} to the device pubkey with the machine enroll
  //     secret and return it base64'd in a PairResult.
  //
  // It never reveals which check failed beyond the coarse reason, and is safe to
  // call concurrently (Consume + Store.Add are internally synchronized).
  func (s *Server) handlePairInit(in PairInit) (PairResult, *PairError) {
  	// (1) Validate the device public key before touching the pending store, so an
  	// invalid pub can't burn a still-good pairingId. We never echo the raw bytes.
  	devPub, ok := decodeBoxKey(in.DevicePub)
  	if !ok {
  		return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "unknown", "bad devicePub")
  	}

  	// (2) Single-use consumption of the one-time pairingId.
  	if err := s.pending.Consume(in.PairingID, time.Now()); err != nil {
  		switch {
  		case errors.Is(err, devstore.ErrPairExpired):
  			return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "expired", "ttl elapsed")
  		default: // ErrPairUnknown (never existed OR already consumed) + any I/O error
  			return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "unknown", err.Error())
  		}
  	}

  	// (3) Mint the per-device channel material. keyId doubles as the HKDF salt +
  	// AAD component downstream, so a 16-byte random hex id (NewToken) is unique and
  	// salt-safe (D-f). The PSK is 32 random bytes, stored base64.
  	keyID := NewToken()
  	pskBytes := make([]byte, 32)
  	if _, err := rand.Read(pskBytes); err != nil {
  		return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "unknown", "psk gen failed")
  	}
  	pskB64 := base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(pskBytes)

  	// (4) Persist the device. Account is reserved ("") for the future account merge.
  	dev := devstore.Device{
  		KeyID:     keyID,
  		Name:      in.Name,
  		DevicePub: in.DevicePub,
  		Account:   "",
  		PSK:       pskB64,
  		Added:     time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
  	}
  	if err := s.devices.Add(dev); err != nil {
  		// The pairingId is already consumed; the phone must restart the QR flow.
  		return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "unknown", "persist failed: "+err.Error())
  	}

  	// (5) Seal {keyId, psk} to the device pubkey with the machine enroll secret.
  	sealed, err := e2ee.SealPairing(e2ee.PairPayload{KeyID: keyID, PSK: pskB64}, devPub, s.enrollSec)
  	if err != nil {
  		return PairResult{}, s.pairError(in.PairingID, "unknown", "seal failed: "+err.Error())
  	}

  	s.audit("pair OK keyId=%s device=%q", keyID, trunc(in.Name, 64))
  	return PairResult{Type: "pair_result", Sealed: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(sealed)}, nil
  }

  // decodeBoxKey base64(std)-decodes a 32-byte Curve25519 key into a fixed array.
  // Returns ok=false for malformed or wrong-length input.
  func decodeBoxKey(b64 string) (*[32]byte, bool) {
  	if b64 == "" {
  		return nil, false
  	}
  	raw, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(b64)
  	if err != nil || len(raw) != 32 {
  		return nil, false
  	}
  	var k [32]byte
  	copy(k[:], raw)
  	return &k, true
  }

  // firstFrameKind classifies a connection's FIRST frame for the §7.4 hard-cut
  // router (T6 direct / T7 hub). It returns:
  //
  //	"pair_init" — a plaintext JSON object with type=="pair_init"
  //	"envelope"  — an E2EE frame: a JSON object carrying k, n and c
  //	"reject"    — anything else (non-JSON, an array, a cleartext session frame,
  //	              a half-formed envelope) -> caller audits + disconnects.
  //
  // It only peeks at the shape; full validation happens in handlePairInit (pair_init)
  // or openInbound (envelope, added in T6).
  func firstFrameKind(raw []byte) string {
  	var probe struct {
  		Type string           `json:"type"`
  		K    *string          `json:"k"`
  		N    *string          `json:"n"`
  		C    *json.RawMessage `json:"c"`
  	}
  	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &probe); err != nil {
  		return "reject" // non-object JSON (array/number/string) or malformed
  	}
  	if probe.Type == "pair_init" {
  		return "pair_init"
  	}
  	if probe.K != nil && probe.N != nil && probe.C != nil {
  		return "envelope"
  	}
  	return "reject"
  }
  ```

### T6: Server E2EE seams: direct-mode hard-cut + seal/open

**Files:**
- Modify: `bridge/internal/bridge/server.go`
- Test: `bridge/internal/bridge/server_test.go`

**Depends on:** T5 (`PairInit`/`PairResult`/`PairError` structs, `(*Server).handlePairInit`, `firstFrameKind` — all in package `bridge`), T3 (`devstore.Store`, `devstore.Device`, `devstore.PendingStore`), T1 (`e2ee.Envelope`, `e2ee.Seal`, `e2ee.Open`, `e2ee.DeriveKeys`, `e2ee.DirP2B`/`e2ee.DirB2P`, `e2ee.ErrSeq`/`ErrAuth`/`ErrKeyMismatch`/`ErrNonceLen`).

> Context for the drafter/executor: T5 already added to package `bridge` the plaintext pairing frame types and the first-frame router/handler that T6 calls:
> - `type PairInit struct { Type string \`json:"type"\`; PairingID string \`json:"pairingId"\`; DevicePub string \`json:"devicePub"\`; Name string \`json:"name"\` }`
> - `type PairResult struct { Type string \`json:"type"\`; Sealed string \`json:"sealed"\` }`
> - `type PairError struct { Type string \`json:"type"\`; Reason string \`json:"reason"\` }`
> - `func (s *Server) handlePairInit(in PairInit) (PairResult, *PairError)` — validates `in.PairingID` via `s.pending.Consume`, generates `(keyId, psk)`, persists a `devstore.Device` via `s.devices.Add`, seals `e2ee.PairPayload{KeyID,PSK}` to `in.DevicePub` with `s.enrollSec`, returns `PairResult`; on failure returns `*PairError` with reason `unknown|expired`.
> - `func firstFrameKind(raw []byte) string` — returns `"pair_init"` for a plaintext JSON object with `type=="pair_init"`, `"envelope"` for an object carrying `k`+`n`+`c`, else `"reject"`.
>
> T6 is the wiring task: it adds the additive `Server`/`conn` crypto fields, the `EnableE2EE` enabler (D-g), the shared `openInbound`/`sealOutbound` seams, and rewrites the `handleWS` read loop + write closure to do the direct-mode hard-cut. **Prod wiring of `enroll.key` + `devstore.Watch` lives in T8 (`cmd/codexbridge/main.go`), NOT here** (D-h). T6 leaves `NewServer` unchanged (no breaking constructor change, D-g) and KEEPS the `authDevice` token gate at the WS upgrade (D-i).

  ```go
  // e2eeFixture builds a Server in E2EE mode with one paired device and returns
  // the conn already bound to that device's keyId (as openInbound would leave it
  // after the first envelope), plus the two direction subkeys for the test to
  // craft envelopes. The bridge encrypts B2P and decrypts P2B; the "phone" in the
  // test does the mirror. maxConn is set so the same fixture can feed the handleWS
  // hard-cut test (a zero maxConn would make atCap=len>=0 true and 503 every dial).
  func e2eeFixture(t *testing.T) (s *Server, c *conn, kP2B, kB2P []byte, keyID string) {
  	t.Helper()
  	keyID = "k_test_t6_0001"
  	psk := make([]byte, 32)
  	for i := range psk {
  		psk[i] = byte(i + 1)
  	}
  	var derr error
  	kP2B, kB2P, derr = e2ee.DeriveKeys(psk, keyID)
  	if derr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", derr)
  	}
  	dir := t.TempDir()
  	store, serr := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if serr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", serr)
  	}
  	if err := store.Add(devstore.Device{
  		KeyID:     keyID,
  		Name:      "ipad",
  		DevicePub: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(make([]byte, 32)),
  		PSK:       base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(psk),
  		Added:     time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
  	}); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("store.Add: %v", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))
  	s = &Server{
  		clients:   make(map[*conn]struct{}),
  		approvals: make(map[string]*pendingApproval),
  		tokens:    map[string]string{"good": "default"},
  		maxConn:   8,
  	}
  	s.EnableE2EE(store, &[32]byte{}, pending)
  	c = &conn{send: make(chan any, 4), done: make(chan struct{}), id: "c1", e2ee: true}
  	return s, c, kP2B, kB2P, keyID
  }

  // EnableE2EE must set the additive Server fields so the bridge enters E2EE mode
  // without a breaking change to NewServer (D-g).
  func TestEnableE2EE(t *testing.T) {
  	s := &Server{clients: make(map[*conn]struct{})}
  	if s.devices != nil || s.pending != nil {
  		t.Fatal("fresh Server must not be E2EE-provisioned")
  	}
  	dir := t.TempDir()
  	store, err := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))
  	s.EnableE2EE(store, &[32]byte{}, pending)
  	if s.devices != store || s.pending != pending || s.enrollSec == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("EnableE2EE did not wire fields: devices=%v pending=%v enrollSec=%v", s.devices, s.pending, s.enrollSec)
  	}
  }
  ```

  Then extend the `conn` struct. Old:

  ```go
  type conn struct {
  ```

  ```go
  type conn struct {
  	send      chan any
  	done      chan struct{}
  	closeOnce sync.Once
  	id        string                                 // short session id, for audit logs
  	device    string                                 // device name (pairing token / hub session)
  	write     func(ctx context.Context, v any) error // transport-specific frame writer

  	// Per-connection E2EE state (set after the first envelope binds a keyId).
  	// seqIn/seqOut start at 0 and increment by 1; a non-increasing inbound seq is
  	// rejected (drop + disconnect). Zero value = legacy/cleartext conn.
  	e2ee   bool
  	keyID  string // bound keyId (selects PSK); "" until the first envelope resolves it
  	kP2B   []byte // inbound  subkey (phone -> bridge)
  	kB2P   []byte // outbound subkey (bridge -> phone)
  	seqIn  uint64 // next expected inbound seq
  	seqOut uint64 // next outbound seq
  }
  ```

  ```go
  // EnableE2EE provisions the bridge for end-to-end encryption. Call only at
  // startup (before Handler is served): once devices != nil the bridge is in E2EE
  // mode and hard-cuts cleartext session frames (D-c). This is additive — NewServer
  // is unchanged, so existing construction and tests keep working (D-g). Prod loads
  // enrollSec from enroll.key and starts devices.Watch in cmd/codexbridge (T8/D-h).
  func (s *Server) EnableE2EE(devices *devstore.Store, enrollSec *[32]byte, pending *devstore.PendingStore) {
  	s.devices = devices
  	s.enrollSec = enrollSec
  	s.pending = pending
  }
  ```

  Append these tests at the END of `server_test.go`. They reference `s.openInbound`/`s.sealOutbound` (Step 9) and the bound-conn semantics. A small helper crafts a phone->bridge envelope with `e2ee.Seal`.
  ```go
  // mkEnvelope builds the JSON bytes of a phone->bridge envelope (what arrives on
  // the wire) for the given seq + plaintext, encrypted with the phone-side subkey.
  func mkEnvelope(t *testing.T, kP2B []byte, keyID string, seq uint64, plain []byte) []byte {
  	t.Helper()
  	env, err := e2ee.Seal(kP2B, keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, seq, plain)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
  	}
  	raw, err := json.Marshal(env)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal envelope: %v", err)
  	}
  	return raw
  }

  // openInbound happy path: the first envelope binds the keyId (sets conn keys +
  // device name from devices.Lookup, D-k), decrypts, and increments seqIn. A second
  // envelope at the next seq round-trips too.
  func TestOpenInboundHappyPath(t *testing.T) {
  	s, c, kP2B, _, keyID := e2eeFixture(t)

  	raw0 := mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))
  	in, err := s.openInbound(c, raw0)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("openInbound[0]: %v", err)
  	}
  	if in.Type != "list" {
  		t.Fatalf("decrypted type = %q, want list", in.Type)
  	}
  	if c.keyID != keyID {
  		t.Fatalf("conn.keyID = %q, want %q", c.keyID, keyID)
  	}
  	if c.device != "ipad" {
  		t.Fatalf("conn.device = %q, want ipad (from devices.Lookup, D-k)", c.device)
  	}
  	if c.seqIn != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqIn = %d, want 1 after first frame", c.seqIn)
  	}

  	raw1 := mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 1, []byte(`{"type":"sync"}`))
  	if in, err = s.openInbound(c, raw1); err != nil || in.Type != "sync" {
  		t.Fatalf("openInbound[1] = %v, %v", in.Type, err)
  	}
  	if c.seqIn != 2 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqIn = %d, want 2", c.seqIn)
  	}
  }

  // Unknown keyId: an envelope whose k is not in the device store is dropped (the
  // device was never paired or was revoked). conn stays unbound.
  func TestOpenInboundUnknownKeyID(t *testing.T) {
  	s, c, kP2B, _, _ := e2eeFixture(t)
  	raw := mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, "k_not_paired", 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, raw); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("unknown keyId must be rejected")
  	}
  	if c.keyID != "" {
  		t.Fatalf("conn must stay unbound on unknown keyId, got %q", c.keyID)
  	}
  }

  // Sequence rollback / replay: after binding at seq 0, a frame re-using seq 0
  // (replay) and a frame jumping forwards must both be dropped (ErrSeq), and seqIn
  // must NOT advance.
  func TestOpenInboundSeqReplayAndRollback(t *testing.T) {
  	s, c, kP2B, _, keyID := e2eeFixture(t)

  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("openInbound[0]: %v", err)
  	}
  	// Replay seq 0 (already consumed) -> drop.
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("replayed seq 0 must be rejected")
  	}
  	if c.seqIn != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqIn must stay 1 after a rejected replay, got %d", c.seqIn)
  	}
  	// Skip to a future seq when 1 is expected -> drop (caller enforces strict +1).
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 5, []byte(`{"type":"sync"}`))); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("out-of-order seq must be rejected")
  	}
  }

  // Tampered / wrong-key ciphertext: a frame for the bound keyId whose ciphertext
  // was encrypted under the WRONG subkey (or mutated) fails the AEAD tag -> drop.
  func TestOpenInboundAuthFail(t *testing.T) {
  	s, c, kP2B, kB2P, keyID := e2eeFixture(t)
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("bind: %v", err)
  	}
  	// Encrypt seq 1 with the WRONG direction subkey (kB2P) -> AAD/key mismatch -> tag fails.
  	bad := mkEnvelope(t, kB2P, keyID, 1, []byte(`{"type":"sync"}`))
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, bad); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("ciphertext under the wrong key must fail the AEAD tag")
  	}
  }

  // sealOutbound must round-trip: the bytes it emits decrypt back (with the B2P
  // subkey) to the marshaled message, and seqOut advances by exactly 1 per call.
  func TestSealOutboundRoundTrips(t *testing.T) {
  	s, c, kP2B, kB2P, keyID := e2eeFixture(t)
  	// Bind the conn first (sealOutbound needs c.kB2P + c.keyID, set by openInbound).
  	if _, err := s.openInbound(c, mkEnvelope(t, kP2B, keyID, 0, []byte(`{"type":"list"}`))); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("bind: %v", err)
  	}

  	out, err := s.sealOutbound(c, map[string]any{"type": "sessions", "data": []any{}})
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("sealOutbound: %v", err)
  	}
  	var env e2ee.Envelope
  	if err := json.Unmarshal(out, &env); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("emitted bytes are not an envelope: %v", err)
  	}
  	if env.K != keyID || env.S != 0 {
  		t.Fatalf("envelope k=%q s=%d, want %q 0", env.K, env.S, keyID)
  	}
  	plain, err := e2ee.Open(kB2P, keyID, e2ee.DirB2P, 0, env)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("phone-side Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	if !json.Valid(plain) || !bytes.Contains(plain, []byte(`"sessions"`)) {
  		t.Fatalf("decrypted outbound = %s", plain)
  	}
  	if c.seqOut != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqOut = %d, want 1", c.seqOut)
  	}
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 9: Implement the `openInbound` / `sealOutbound` seams.**
  Insert these two methods in `server.go` right AFTER `EnableE2EE` (Step 4). They are the shared content-plane seams used by both the direct read loop (Step 10) and the hub agent path (T7). All crypto failures return an opaque error — the caller audits + disconnects and never tells the peer why.
  ```go
  // openInbound is the E2EE inbound seam shared by the direct (server.go) and hub
  // (agent.go) read paths. raw is the on-wire envelope bytes. On the FIRST frame of
  // a conn (c.keyID == "") it resolves env.K against the device store, derives the
  // direction subkeys, and BINDS them to the conn — including c.device = the
  // device's name (D-k), used for audit + approval ownership. It then enforces
  // strict monotonic seq (env.S must equal c.seqIn), decrypts with the inbound
  // subkey, unmarshals the plaintext into inbound, and advances c.seqIn. ANY
  // failure (unknown keyId, seq mismatch, auth/decrypt failure, malformed frame)
  // returns an error so the caller can audit + disconnect — never surfaced to the
  // peer.
  func (s *Server) openInbound(c *conn, raw []byte) (inbound, error) {
  	var env e2ee.Envelope
  	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &env); err != nil {
  		return inbound{}, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: malformed envelope: %w", err)
  	}
  	// Bind keyId on the first frame: look up the PSK, derive subkeys, pin identity.
  	if c.keyID == "" {
  		dev, ok := s.devices.Lookup(env.K)
  		if !ok {
  			return inbound{}, e2ee.ErrKeyMismatch // unknown/revoked device
  		}
  		psk, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(dev.PSK)
  		if err != nil {
  			return inbound{}, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: device %q has bad psk: %w", env.K, err)
  		}
  		kP2B, kB2P, err := e2ee.DeriveKeys(psk, env.K)
  		if err != nil {
  			return inbound{}, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: derive keys for %q: %w", env.K, err)
  		}
  		c.keyID, c.kP2B, c.kB2P = env.K, kP2B, kB2P
  		c.device = dev.Name // D-k: audit/approval identity from the device store
  	}
  	plain, err := e2ee.Open(c.kP2B, c.keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, c.seqIn, env)
  	if err != nil {
  		return inbound{}, err // ErrSeq / ErrAuth / ErrKeyMismatch / ErrNonceLen
  	}
  	var in inbound
  	if err := json.Unmarshal(plain, &in); err != nil {
  		return inbound{}, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: plaintext is not a phone frame: %w", err)
  	}
  	c.seqIn++
  	return in, nil
  }

  // sealOutbound is the E2EE outbound seam shared by the direct + hub write paths.
  // It marshals v, encrypts it with the bound outbound subkey (bridge -> phone) at
  // the conn's next outbound seq, advances c.seqOut, and returns the envelope JSON
  // bytes for the transport to send as a single text frame. It requires the conn to
  // be bound already (the inbound side binds it on the first frame); an unbound conn
  // returns an error — the write closures detect the unbound state and DROP the
  // frame (see handleWS Edit 14a) rather than tearing down the socket.
  func (s *Server) sealOutbound(c *conn, v any) ([]byte, error) {
  	if c.keyID == "" || c.kB2P == nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: sealOutbound on an unbound conn")
  	}
  	plain, err := json.Marshal(v)
  	if err != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("e2ee: marshal outbound: %w", err)
  	}
  	env, err := e2ee.Seal(c.kB2P, c.keyID, e2ee.DirB2P, c.seqOut, plain)
  	if err != nil {
  		return nil, err
  	}
  	c.seqOut++
  	return json.Marshal(env)
  }
  ```

This test connects, sends a CLEARTEXT session frame as the FIRST frame to an E2EE-provisioned bridge, and asserts the bridge closes the connection (hard-cut, D-c) WITHOUT processing it.

  Test body:
  ```go
  // dialWS starts the Server's Handler on an httptest server and opens a phone-side
  // websocket to /ws with the given token. Returns the client conn + a cleanup.
  func dialWS(t *testing.T, s *Server, token string) (*websocket.Conn, func()) {
  	t.Helper()
  	hs := httptest.NewServer(s.Handler())
  	wsURL := "ws" + strings.TrimPrefix(hs.URL, "http") + "/ws?token=" + token
  	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
  	cli, _, err := websocket.Dial(ctx, wsURL, nil)
  	if err != nil {
  		cancel()
  		hs.Close()
  		t.Fatalf("dial /ws: %v", err)
  	}
  	return cli, func() {
  		_ = cli.Close(websocket.StatusNormalClosure, "test done")
  		cancel()
  		hs.Close()
  	}
  }

  // E2EE hard-cut (D-c): once the bridge is E2EE-provisioned, a connection whose
  // FIRST frame is plaintext session JSON (not pair_init, not an envelope) is
  // refused — the bridge closes the socket and never calls handle().
  func TestHandleWSHardCutCleartextFirstFrame(t *testing.T) {
  	s, _, _, _, _ := e2eeFixture(t)
  	cli, cleanup := dialWS(t, s, "good")
  	defer cleanup()

  	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
  	defer cancel()
  	// Plaintext session frame as the first frame -> must be hard-cut.
  	if err := wsjson.Write(ctx, cli, map[string]any{"type": "list"}); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("write cleartext frame: %v", err)
  	}
  	// The bridge must close the socket; the next read errors out.
  	var anyMsg json.RawMessage
  	err := wsjson.Read(ctx, cli, &anyMsg)
  	if err == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("expected the bridge to hard-cut the connection, got frame %s", anyMsg)
  	}
  }

  // A non-provisioned bridge stays legacy: a plaintext first frame is handled
  // normally (the migration path, D-c). An unknown "type" yields an
  // {"type":"error"} frame, proving the frame was processed (not hard-cut).
  func TestHandleWSLegacyWhenNotProvisioned(t *testing.T) {
  	s := &Server{
  		clients:   make(map[*conn]struct{}),
  		approvals: make(map[string]*pendingApproval),
  		resumed:   make(map[string]bool),
  		turns:     make(map[string]string),
  		turnOwner: make(map[string]string),
  		pushes:    make(map[string]PushReg),
  		pusher:    LogPusher{},
  		maxConn:   8,
  		tokens:    map[string]string{"good": "default"},
  	}
  	cli, cleanup := dialWS(t, s, "good")
  	defer cleanup()

  	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
  	defer cancel()
  	if err := wsjson.Write(ctx, cli, map[string]any{"type": "bogusType"}); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
  	}
  	var msg map[string]any
  	if err := wsjson.Read(ctx, cli, &msg); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("legacy bridge must process a plaintext frame, got read error: %v", err)
  	}
  	if msg["type"] != "error" {
  		t.Fatalf("expected an error reply for an unknown type, got %v", msg)
  	}
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 14: Rewrite the `handleWS` read loop + write closure for the direct-mode hard-cut.**
  Two edits in `handleWS`.

  Edit 14a — the conn's write closure. Old:
  ```go
  	c := &conn{
  		send:   make(chan any, 256),
  		done:   make(chan struct{}),
  		id:     NewToken()[:8],
  		device: device,
  		write:  func(ctx context.Context, v any) error { return wsjson.Write(ctx, ws, v) },
  	}
  ```
  New (when E2EE is on, the conn enters E2EE mode and its write closure seals every outbound frame, sending the envelope as a text frame; an outbound that arrives BEFORE the conn is bound by the first inbound envelope — e.g. the resync approval re-surfacing below — is silently dropped, never an error, so writePump is not torn down; legacy conns keep the plaintext writer unchanged):
  ```go
  	e2eeOn := s.devices != nil
  	c := &conn{
  		send:   make(chan any, 256),
  		done:   make(chan struct{}),
  		id:     NewToken()[:8],
  		device: device,
  		e2ee:   e2eeOn,
  	}
  	if e2eeOn {
  		// Outbound seal: marshal+encrypt under the conn's bound subkey, send the
  		// envelope bytes as a single text frame. The conn is bound by the first
  		// inbound envelope. An outbound BEFORE binding (c.keyID == "") has no crypto
  		// state to seal with — drop it (return nil) so writePump keeps running. This
  		// matters for the resync block below, which may push pending approvals into
  		// c.send before the phone's first frame arrives; in E2EE mode nothing
  		// legitimately precedes that first frame, and the phone re-requests state
  		// after binding.
  		c.write = func(ctx context.Context, v any) error {
  			if c.keyID == "" {
  				return nil // unbound: drop, do not error (would kill writePump)
  			}
  			out, err := s.sealOutbound(c, v)
  			if err != nil {
  				return err
  			}
  			return ws.Write(ctx, websocket.MessageText, out)
  		}
  	} else {
  		c.write = func(ctx context.Context, v any) error { return wsjson.Write(ctx, ws, v) }
  	}
  ```

  Edit 14b — the read loop. Old:
  ```go
  	for {
  		var in inbound
  		if err := wsjson.Read(ctx, ws, &in); err != nil {
  			return
  		}
  		go s.handle(ctx, c, in)
  	}
  }
  ```
  New (read RAW bytes; in legacy mode unmarshal as before; in E2EE mode route the FIRST frame through `firstFrameKind` — pair_init runs the handshake then closes, envelope goes through `openInbound`; anything else is hard-cut; after the first frame every frame must be an envelope):
  ```go
  	for {
  		_, raw, err := ws.Read(ctx)
  		if err != nil {
  			return
  		}
  		if !c.e2ee {
  			// Legacy bridge (not E2EE-provisioned): the migration path — plaintext
  			// session frames are processed exactly as before.
  			var in inbound
  			if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &in); err != nil {
  				return
  			}
  			go s.handle(ctx, c, in)
  			continue
  		}
  		// E2EE mode. The hard-cut first-frame rule (D-c): the very first frame may be
  		// a plaintext pair_init (public-key-only) or an encrypted envelope; once the
  		// conn is bound (c.keyID != "") only envelopes are accepted — any cleartext
  		// session frame is refused.
  		if c.keyID == "" {
  			switch firstFrameKind(raw) {
  			case "pair_init":
  				var pi PairInit
  				if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &pi); err != nil {
  					s.audit("pair_init malformed conn=%s from %s", c.id, clientIP(r))
  					return
  				}
  				res, perr := s.handlePairInit(pi)
  				if perr != nil {
  					_ = wsjson.Write(ctx, ws, perr) // plaintext pair_error
  				} else {
  					_ = wsjson.Write(ctx, ws, res) // plaintext pair_result
  				}
  				// Pairing is a one-shot exchange on its own connection: close after.
  				return
  			case "envelope":
  				in, err := s.openInbound(c, raw)
  				if err != nil {
  					s.audit("e2ee bind FAILED conn=%s from %s: %v", c.id, clientIP(r), err)
  					return
  				}
  				s.audit("e2ee bound conn=%s device=%s keyId=%s", c.id, c.device, trunc(c.keyID, 16))
  				go s.handle(ctx, c, in)
  			default:
  				// Hard-cut: any other cleartext first frame is refused.
  				s.audit("e2ee hard-cut (cleartext first frame) conn=%s from %s", c.id, clientIP(r))
  				return
  			}
  			continue
  		}
  		// Post-binding: every frame MUST be an envelope; a cleartext session frame
  		// here is a downgrade attempt -> drop + disconnect.
  		if firstFrameKind(raw) != "envelope" {
  			s.audit("e2ee hard-cut (cleartext post-pairing) conn=%s device=%s", c.id, c.device)
  			return
  		}
  		in, err := s.openInbound(c, raw)
  		if err != nil {
  			s.audit("e2ee inbound FAILED conn=%s device=%s: %v", c.id, c.device, err)
  			return
  		}
  		go s.handle(ctx, c, in)
  	}
  }
  ```

  > Note on the audit line at connect time: the existing `s.audit("ws connected conn=%s device=%s ...")` (just below the conn construction) still runs with the token-derived `device`. In E2EE mode `c.device` is overwritten to the PSK-bound name by `openInbound`; the post-bind `e2ee bound` audit records the authoritative identity. No change needed there.
  >
  > Note on the resync block: the existing block just below the conn construction re-surfaces still-pending approvals via `c.push(...)` BEFORE the first inbound frame binds the conn. In E2EE mode the write closure (Edit 14a) drops any pre-binding outbound (returns nil), so this resync push is a harmless no-op until the phone sends its first envelope — it does NOT tear down `writePump`. No change to the resync block itself.

- [ ] **Step 16: Update the `server.go` package doc-comment header (the spec) to document the E2EE hard-cut + the kept token gate.**
  Old (the SECURITY MODEL block — anchor on the Auth bullet and append an E2EE bullet after it):
  ```go
  //   - Auth: every /ws and /file request is gated by a pairing token compared in
  //     constant time (SHA-256). An empty token rejects everything. Multiple named
  //     device tokens are supported (AddDeviceToken) so a lost device can be revoked
  //     individually instead of rotating one shared secret.
  ```
  New:
  ```go
  //   - Auth: every /ws and /file request is gated by a pairing token compared in
  //     constant time (SHA-256). An empty token rejects everything. Multiple named
  //     device tokens are supported (AddDeviceToken) so a lost device can be revoked
  //     individually instead of rotating one shared secret.
  //   - E2EE (EnableE2EE): when the bridge is E2EE-provisioned (a device store +
  //     enroll key are wired), the control-plane phone protocol is end-to-end
  //     encrypted so a relay sees only ciphertext. The token gate above is KEPT and
  //     still routes/rate-limits the socket; the per-device PSK (from devices.json)
  //     becomes the real per-frame content authenticator, and the bound device's
  //     name supplies the audit/approval identity. A connection's FIRST frame may be
  //     a plaintext pair_init (public-key-only handshake, then the socket closes) or
  //     an encrypted envelope {k,s,n,c}; ANY other cleartext frame — or any one once
  //     the conn is bound — is refused (hard-cut, anti-downgrade). Decrypt failures,
  //     unknown keyIds, and non-monotonic sequence numbers audit + disconnect,
  //     leaking nothing to the peer. A NON-provisioned bridge stays legacy (plaintext
  //     over the relay's TLS) — that is the migration path. The miniprogram front-end
  //     is intentionally NOT E2EE-aware, so an E2EE bridge refuses its cleartext
  //     (a conscious v1 limitation).
  ```


### T7: Agent (hub-mode) E2EE seams

The hub stays protocol-blind: it relays `hubFrame.Data` opaquely. Encryption/decryption happens at the **agent boundary**, exactly mirroring the direct-mode seams T6 added to `server.go`. T7 wires the agent's per-SID `conn` into the SAME `Server.openInbound`/`Server.sealOutbound`/`Server.handlePairInit`/`firstFrameKind` helpers, so there is no second crypto implementation — only a second transport. Per-SID `keyId`/`seq` already live on `conn` (the additive fields T6 added). When the bridge is **not** E2EE-provisioned (`s.devices == nil`) the agent keeps its current cleartext behavior — that is the migration path (decision D-c).

**Files:**
- Modify: `bridge/internal/bridge/agent.go`
- Modify: `bridge/internal/bridge/server.go` *(factor the existing approval-resync loop into a reusable `(s *Server) resyncApprovals(c *conn)` helper so the agent can fire it AFTER the keyId bind — see Step 11)*
- Test (create): `bridge/internal/bridge/agent_test.go` *(new file in the existing `package bridge` test set; lives alongside `server_test.go`, reuses its struct-literal + `c.send`-channel harness)*

**Depends on:** T6 (consumes, all in `package bridge`: the additive `conn` fields `e2ee/keyID/kP2B/kB2P/seqIn/seqOut`; `Server.devices *devstore.Store`; `Server.EnableE2EE`; and the shared seams `firstFrameKind(raw []byte) string`, `(s *Server) handlePairInit(in PairInit) (PairResult, *PairError)`, `(s *Server) openInbound(c *conn, raw []byte) (inbound, error)`, `(s *Server) sealOutbound(c *conn, v any) ([]byte, error)`). Transitively: T1–T5 (`e2ee`, `devstore`, bridge pairing types `PairInit`/`PairResult`/`PairError`).

> NOTE: This task adds no new import to `agent.go` beyond what T6 already pulled in transitively. `message()` already receives `f.Data` (a `json.RawMessage`, i.e. `[]byte`), which is exactly what the seam helpers take. Routing, sealing, and opening all go through the T6 helpers, which own the `e2ee`/`devstore` imports.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test for the hub-mode first-frame router.**

This asserts that an E2EE-provisioned agent conn whose first frame is a sealed envelope is decrypted via the shared `openInbound`, that a cleartext (non-`pair_init`, non-envelope) first frame is hard-cut rejected, and that a revoked keyId no longer resolves. The router unit is `routeFirstFrame` (the `Server`-method wrapper we extract in Step 3); the production path calls the underlying `routeFirstFrameDispatch` on a live `agentLink`. Pairing-reply writes (`pair_init` -> `writeFrame`) are exercised separately in `pairing_test.go` (T11), so these unit tests only cover the envelope + reject + revoke branches, which never touch `a.ws`.

Create the test file `bridge/internal/bridge/agent_test.go`:

```go
package bridge

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/base64"
	"encoding/json"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/devstore"
	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/e2ee"
)

// e2eeServer builds an E2EE-provisioned Server with a single paired device and
// returns the Server plus the device's derived subkeys, so a test can act as the
// phone (seal phone->bridge with kP2B, open bridge->phone with kB2P).
func e2eeServer(t *testing.T) (s *Server, keyID string, kP2B, kB2P []byte) {
	t.Helper()
	dir := t.TempDir()
	devices, err := devstore.Open(dir + "/devices.json")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", err)
	}
	pending := devstore.OpenPending(dir + "/pending-pairings.json")

	_, enrollSec, err := e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("GenEnrollKeypair: %v", err)
	}
	// The test acts only as the already-paired phone, so the enroll PUBLIC key is
	// unused here (pairing/sealing is covered in pairing_test.go).

	keyID = NewToken()
	psk := make([]byte, 32)
	for i := range psk {
		psk[i] = byte(i + 1)
	}
	kP2B, kB2P, err = e2ee.DeriveKeys(psk, keyID)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
	}
	if err := devices.Add(devstore.Device{
		KeyID: keyID,
		Name:  "ipad",
		PSK:   base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(psk),
		Added: time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
	}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("devices.Add: %v", err)
	}

	s = &Server{
		clients: make(map[*conn]struct{}),
		pushes:  make(map[string]PushReg),
		pusher:  LogPusher{},
	}
	s.EnableE2EE(devices, enrollSec, pending)
	return s, keyID, kP2B, kB2P
}

// TestAgentRouteFirstFrameEnvelope: an E2EE-provisioned agent conn whose first
// frame is a sealed envelope is decrypted via openInbound and handled. The
// per-SID conn binds keyId + device name from the store (decision D-k).
func TestAgentRouteFirstFrameEnvelope(t *testing.T) {
	s, keyID, kP2B, _ := e2eeServer(t)

	c := &conn{
		send:   make(chan any, 4),
		done:   make(chan struct{}),
		id:     "sid1",
		device: "phone", // placeholder set by open(); overwritten on bind
		e2ee:   true,
		write:  func(ctx context.Context, v any) error { return nil },
	}

	// Phone seals {"type":"registerPush","pushToken":"dev9","platform":"ios"} as
	// the FIRST phone->bridge frame (seq 0) under kP2B.
	plain := []byte(`{"type":"registerPush","pushToken":"dev9","platform":"ios"}`)
	env, err := e2ee.Seal(kP2B, keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, 0, plain)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
	}
	raw, err := json.Marshal(env)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("marshal env: %v", err)
	}

	in, ok := s.routeFirstFrame(c, raw)
	if !ok {
		t.Fatal("routeFirstFrame rejected a valid envelope")
	}
	if in.Type != "registerPush" || in.PushToken != "dev9" {
		t.Fatalf("decoded inbound mismatch: %+v", in)
	}
	if c.keyID != keyID {
		t.Fatalf("keyId not bound: got %q want %q", c.keyID, keyID)
	}
	if c.device != "ipad" {
		t.Fatalf("device not bound from store: got %q want %q", c.device, "ipad")
	}
}

// TestAgentRouteFirstFrameReject: a cleartext (non-pair_init, non-envelope) first
// frame on an E2EE agent is rejected (hard-cut) — routeFirstFrame returns
// ok=false and the session is dropped by the caller.
func TestAgentRouteFirstFrameReject(t *testing.T) {
	s, _, _, _ := e2eeServer(t)
	c := &conn{
		send:  make(chan any, 4),
		done:  make(chan struct{}),
		id:    "sid2",
		e2ee:  true,
		write: func(ctx context.Context, v any) error { return nil },
	}
	if _, ok := s.routeFirstFrame(c, []byte(`{"type":"prompt","text":"hi"}`)); ok {
		t.Fatal("routeFirstFrame accepted a cleartext session frame post-cut")
	}
}

// TestAgentRouteRevokedKeyDrops: after the device is revoked from the store, an
// otherwise-valid envelope from that keyId no longer resolves -> ok=false. This
// proves revocation works in hub mode (devstore.Lookup miss -> drop).
func TestAgentRouteRevokedKeyDrops(t *testing.T) {
	s, keyID, kP2B, _ := e2eeServer(t)
	if _, err := s.devices.Revoke(keyID); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Revoke: %v", err)
	}
	c := &conn{send: make(chan any, 4), done: make(chan struct{}), id: "sid3", e2ee: true,
		write: func(ctx context.Context, v any) error { return nil }}
	env, _ := e2ee.Seal(kP2B, keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, 0, []byte(`{"type":"resume"}`))
	raw, _ := json.Marshal(env)
	if _, ok := s.routeFirstFrame(c, raw); ok {
		t.Fatal("revoked keyId still accepted")
	}
}
```

> The test calls `routeFirstFrame` (the unit we extract in Step 3), `Server.EnableE2EE` (T6 helper, D-g/D-h), `devstore.Open`/`OpenPending`/`Add`/`Revoke`/`Device` (T3/T4), `e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair`/`DeriveKeys`/`Seal`/`DirP2B`/`Envelope` (T1/T2). The PSK is encoded with the standard library `base64.StdEncoding` (the contract's `devstore.Device.PSK` is documented as "base64 std, 32 bytes") — no helper from the e2ee package is needed or assumed.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the per-SID first-frame router `routeFirstFrameDispatch` (+ `routeFirstFrame` wrapper) to `agent.go`.**

This is the hub-mode mirror of the direct-mode router T6 put in `handleWS`. It classifies the raw frame with the shared `firstFrameKind`, dispatches `pair_init` to `handlePairInit`, and decrypts an `envelope` through `openInbound` (which binds `c.keyID`/subkeys/seq on first use). After a successful inbound bind it (a) sets `c.device` from the store (decision D-k), then (b) fires the DEFERRED approval resync (Step 11) now that a key exists to seal it under. Append these to `agent.go`, after `serveFile` (they live in `package bridge`, so they can call the unexported `Server` seams directly):

```go
// routeFirstFrameDispatch applies the E2EE hard-cut router to one phone->bridge
// frame on a hub session. It mirrors the direct-mode router in handleWS exactly,
// so the crypto lives in ONE place (Server.openInbound / handlePairInit). It
// returns the decoded inbound plus ok=true when the frame is an encrypted session
// frame ready for s.handle; for a pair_init it performs the handshake reply over
// the hub and returns ok=false (no inbound to handle); for any rejected/failed
// frame it returns ok=false so the caller drops the session (hard-cut).
//
// Hub stays protocol-blind: this runs on the agent, never the hub. Revocation
// works here because openInbound resolves env.K via s.devices.Lookup — a revoked
// (deleted) keyId misses and returns an error, dropping the session.
func (a *agentLink) routeFirstFrameDispatch(ctx context.Context, c *conn, raw []byte) (inbound, bool) {
	// Only the FIRST frame is classified; once keyId is bound every later frame
	// MUST be an envelope (openInbound enforces that any cleartext fails to parse
	// as an Envelope / fails the seq+auth check).
	if c.keyID == "" {
		switch firstFrameKind(raw) {
		case "pair_init":
			var pin PairInit
			if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &pin); err != nil {
				a.srv.audit("agent: pair_init parse failed sid=%s", c.id)
				return inbound{}, false
			}
			res, perr := a.srv.handlePairInit(pin)
			if perr != nil {
				// Pairing reply rides the hub like any session frame, but as
				// PLAINTEXT (no PSK exists yet): the phone opens the sealed blob
				// inside pair_result with its device secret, not the codec.
				_ = a.writeFrame(ctx, hubFrame{T: "msg", SID: c.id, Data: mustJSON(perr)})
				return inbound{}, false
			}
			_ = a.writeFrame(ctx, hubFrame{T: "msg", SID: c.id, Data: mustJSON(res)})
			// pair_init is handshake-only: the phone re-connects (new SID) and
			// sends its first ENVELOPE frame; nothing to hand to s.handle now.
			return inbound{}, false
		case "envelope":
			// fall through to the shared openInbound below
		default:
			a.srv.audit("agent: rejecting cleartext first frame sid=%s", c.id)
			return inbound{}, false
		}
	}

	bindNow := c.keyID == "" // first envelope on this conn -> we are about to bind
	in, err := a.srv.openInbound(c, raw)
	if err != nil {
		a.srv.audit("agent: openInbound failed sid=%s: %v", c.id, err)
		return inbound{}, false
	}
	if bindNow {
		// D-k: bind the audit/approval device name from the paired device now that
		// keyId is known. In hub mode the placeholder was "phone"; after the bind
		// the conn carries the human label the operator named the device.
		if d, ok := a.srv.devices.Lookup(c.keyID); ok && d.Name != "" {
			c.device = d.Name
		}
		// Deferred resync: in E2EE mode open() does NOT push pending approvals
		// (it can't seal before the key is bound). Now that c.kB2P/c.keyID exist,
		// surface them — they go out as sealed bridge->phone frames.
		a.srv.resyncApprovals(c)
	}
	return in, true
}

// routeFirstFrame is the test-facing wrapper that exercises routeFirstFrameDispatch
// without a live hub write (pairing replies are exercised in pairing_test.go).
func (s *Server) routeFirstFrame(c *conn, raw []byte) (inbound, bool) {
	a := &agentLink{srv: s}
	return a.routeFirstFrameDispatch(context.Background(), c, raw)
}

// mustJSON marshals a pairing reply; the structs are fixed-shape and cannot fail
// to marshal, but we never panic on the hot path — an error yields nil bytes,
// which the phone treats as a malformed reply and re-scans.
func mustJSON(v any) json.RawMessage {
	b, err := json.Marshal(v)
	if err != nil {
		return nil
	}
	return b
}
```

> `routeFirstFrame` constructs a throwaway `agentLink{srv: s}` so the unit tests can drive the dispatcher; its `writeFrame` is reached only on the `pair_init` branch (covered separately in `pairing_test.go` / T11), and the envelope/reject branches the unit tests cover never touch `a.ws`. The production path (Step 10) calls `routeFirstFrameDispatch` with the real `agentLink`.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the failing test for the sealed outbound write closure.**

The agent's `open()` write closure must seal bridge->phone frames via `sealOutbound` (using `c.kB2P`/`c.keyID`/`DirB2P`/`c.seqOut`) when the conn is E2EE, so the hub relays only ciphertext. We test the closure that `open()` installs by capturing the bytes it would hand to the hub. Add to `agent_test.go`:

```go
// TestAgentSealedOutbound: when a conn is E2EE-bound, the marshalOutbound path
// the open() write closure uses seals the outbound value with kB2P (bridge->phone,
// DirB2P) and the phone can open it with the same subkey at seq 0.
func TestAgentSealedOutbound(t *testing.T) {
	s, keyID, _, kB2P := e2eeServer(t)

	captured := make(chan []byte, 1)
	a := &agentLink{srv: s, sessions: map[string]*conn{}}

	// Build the conn the way open() does (post-bind), but with a write closure that
	// routes through marshalOutbound and captures the ciphertext instead of a ws.
	c := &conn{
		send:   make(chan any, 4),
		done:   make(chan struct{}),
		id:     "sidX",
		device: "ipad",
		e2ee:   true,
		keyID:  keyID,
		kB2P:   kB2P,
	}
	c.write = func(ctx context.Context, v any) error {
		b, err := a.srv.marshalOutbound(c, v)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		captured <- b
		return nil
	}

	if err := c.write(context.Background(), map[string]any{"type": "pong"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write: %v", err)
	}

	var env e2ee.Envelope
	select {
	case b := <-captured:
		if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &env); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("captured bytes are not an Envelope: %v", err)
		}
	case <-time.After(time.Second):
		t.Fatal("write closure produced no frame")
	}
	if env.K != keyID {
		t.Fatalf("envelope keyId mismatch: got %q want %q", env.K, keyID)
	}
	if env.S != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("first outbound seq must be 0, got %d", env.S)
	}
	// Phone opens it with kB2P at the expected seq 0 (DirB2P).
	plain, err := e2ee.Open(kB2P, keyID, e2ee.DirB2P, 0, env)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("phone could not open sealed outbound: %v", err)
	}
	var got map[string]any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(plain, &got); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("plaintext not JSON: %v", err)
	}
	if got["type"] != "pong" {
		t.Fatalf("decrypted outbound mismatch: %v", got)
	}
	if c.seqOut != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("sealOutbound must increment seqOut to 1, got %d", c.seqOut)
	}
}
```

> Uses `e2ee.Envelope`/`Open`/`DirB2P` (T1) and `Server.marshalOutbound` (Step 9), which for an E2EE conn delegates to the T6 `sealOutbound` seam. The closure body here is the same `marshalOutbound`-based dispatch the production `open()` closure uses (Step 8).

- [ ] **Step 9: Add `marshalOutbound` to `agent.go` (the seal-or-plaintext dispatcher).**

```go
// marshalOutbound encodes one bridge->phone value for the hub wire. When the conn
// is E2EE-bound it seals via sealOutbound (ciphertext envelope); otherwise it
// json-marshals plaintext (legacy / non-provisioned bridge, decision D-c). It is
// agent-local: only the agent open() write closure calls it. The direct-mode
// conn.write (T6) calls sealOutbound directly, so this helper does not change the
// direct path — it only adds the legacy-plaintext fallback the hub transport needs.
func (s *Server) marshalOutbound(c *conn, v any) ([]byte, error) {
	if c.e2ee {
		return s.sealOutbound(c, v)
	}
	return json.Marshal(v)
}
```

> NOTE: a conn becomes `c.e2ee == true` when `open()` provisions it under an E2EE bridge (Step 11). In E2EE mode `open()` does NOT eagerly push pending approvals to `c.send` (Step 11 defers that resync until after the first inbound binds the key), so `sealOutbound` is never invoked with a nil `c.kB2P`/`c.keyID`: the first outbound always follows the inbound bind. As belt-and-suspenders, `sealOutbound` (T6) returns an error if `c.kB2P == nil`, which the closure surfaces and the caller drops — but with the deferred resync that path is unreachable in normal operation.

- [ ] **Step 10: MODIFY `message()` to route raw bytes through the E2EE router (hub-mode hard-cut).**

Anchor on the EXACT current `message` (lines 199–211):

OLD:
```go
func (a *agentLink) message(ctx context.Context, sid string, data json.RawMessage) {
	a.mu.Lock()
	c := a.sessions[sid]
	a.mu.Unlock()
	if c == nil {
		return
	}
	var in inbound
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &in); err != nil {
		return
	}
	go a.srv.handle(ctx, c, in)
}
```

NEW:
```go
func (a *agentLink) message(ctx context.Context, sid string, data json.RawMessage) {
	a.mu.Lock()
	c := a.sessions[sid]
	a.mu.Unlock()
	if c == nil {
		return
	}

	// E2EE mode: every phone->bridge frame goes through the shared first-frame
	// router. pair_init is answered in-line (no s.handle); an envelope is opened
	// (binding keyId/subkeys/seq + the device name on the conn, then firing the
	// deferred approval resync) and handed to s.handle; anything else — or any
	// decrypt/seq/keyId failure — drops the session (hard-cut).
	if a.srv.devices != nil {
		in, ok := a.routeFirstFrameDispatch(ctx, c, data)
		if !ok {
			a.closeSession(sid)
			return
		}
		go a.srv.handle(ctx, c, in)
		return
	}

	// Legacy (non-provisioned bridge): cleartext, current behavior unchanged.
	var in inbound
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &in); err != nil {
		return
	}
	go a.srv.handle(ctx, c, in)
}
```

> `a.srv.devices != nil` is the single "is this bridge E2EE-provisioned" gate (decision D-c, matching the contract's "When devices != nil the bridge is in E2EE mode"). `c.e2ee` is already set in `open()` (Step 11), so we do not toggle it here. `closeSession(sid)` (existing, lines 213–223) removes the conn from `a.sessions`, calls `removeConn`, and `c.stop()` — the hub then observes the dropped session. The hub itself never sees plaintext: it only ever relayed `data` opaquely.

- [ ] **Step 11: MODIFY `open()` to mark a conn E2EE up-front AND defer the approval resync in E2EE mode; factor the resync into a reusable `Server.resyncApprovals`.**

First, in `server.go`, extract the existing resync loop (server.go lines 192–196 in `agent.go::open` and lines 395–399 in `server.go::handleWS` share the identical loop) into one reusable helper so both transports — and the deferred hub-mode call from Step 3 — use one implementation:

ADD to `server.go` (near `pendingApprovals`):
```go
// resyncApprovals re-surfaces still-pending approvals to a (re)connected conn:
// its own (ownerDevice == c.device) or unowned ones. In E2EE mode it MUST be
// called only after the conn's keyId/subkeys are bound (the pushes are sealed),
// which is why hub-mode open() defers it to the first-inbound bind.
func (s *Server) resyncApprovals(c *conn) {
	for _, p := range s.pendingApprovals() {
		if p.ownerDevice == "" || p.ownerDevice == c.device {
			c.push(map[string]any{"type": "approval", "id": p.id, "method": p.method, "params": p.params})
		}
	}
}
```

(Optionally replace the inline loop in `handleWS` with `s.resyncApprovals(c)` — direct mode binds the device at the WS upgrade via `authDevice`, so its resync stays eager and unchanged in behavior.)

```go
		id:     sid,
		device: "phone",
```

NEW:

```go
		done:   make(chan struct{}),
		id:     sid,
		device: "phone", // placeholder until the first envelope binds the device name (D-k)
		e2ee:   a.srv.devices != nil,
```

And anchor on the EXACT current eager resync loop at the END of `open()` (lines 191–196):

OLD:
```go
	// Resync: re-surface still-pending approvals to the fresh session.
	for _, p := range a.srv.pendingApprovals() {
		if p.ownerDevice == "" || p.ownerDevice == c.device {
			c.push(map[string]any{"type": "approval", "id": p.id, "method": p.method, "params": p.params})
		}
	}
```

NEW:
```go
	// Resync: re-surface still-pending approvals. In E2EE mode the conn has no
	// keyId/subkeys yet, so a sealed push is impossible — defer the resync to the
	// first-inbound bind in routeFirstFrameDispatch. In legacy (cleartext) mode
	// the push goes out immediately, exactly as before.
	if !c.e2ee {
		a.srv.resyncApprovals(c)
	}
```

> This guarantees `c.e2ee` is set before `writePump` starts, so the FIRST outbound the bridge ever sends on an E2EE conn is sealed — and, critically, the bridge produces no outbound at all until the inbound bind, so `sealOutbound` is never called with a nil subkey. `c.device` stays `"phone"` until `routeFirstFrameDispatch` binds the paired device's name (D-k); in hub mode all sessions share `device="phone"` until then, which is the documented behavior (approval-ownership binding is meaningful in direct LAN mode; in hub mode all sessions share the phone identity until keyId binds a name).

- [ ] **Step 12: Add the resync-after-bind regression test (locks the ordering fix).**

Add to `agent_test.go`:

```go
// TestAgentResyncAfterBind: a pending approval registered BEFORE the phone's first
// frame is NOT pushed until the first inbound binds the conn's key (so it can be
// sealed), then is surfaced as a sealed bridge->phone frame openable with kB2P.
// This pins the deferred-resync ordering: open() must not push to a keyless conn.
func TestAgentResyncAfterBind(t *testing.T) {
	s, keyID, kP2B, kB2P := e2eeServer(t)

	// Register a pending approval owned by no specific device (unowned -> resynced
	// to any session) by inserting it directly into s.approvals under s.apMu:
	// server.go has no helper that takes an explicit id (onApproval mints its own
	// via NewToken), so the test populates the map itself — exactly the state a
	// live in-flight approval occupies.
	s.apMu.Lock()
	s.approvals["ap1"] = &pendingApproval{
		id:          "ap1",
		method:      "applyPatch",
		params:      json.RawMessage(`{}`),
		ownerDevice: "",
		ch:          make(chan string, 1),
	}
	s.apMu.Unlock()

	a := &agentLink{srv: s, sessions: map[string]*conn{}}
	c := &conn{
		send:   make(chan any, 8),
		done:   make(chan struct{}),
		id:     "sidR",
		device: "phone",
		e2ee:   true,
	}
	c.write = func(ctx context.Context, v any) error {
		// In the real open() closure this is marshalOutbound; here we only need to
		// prove the resync push happens (and is sealable) post-bind.
		_, err := a.srv.marshalOutbound(c, v)
		return err
	}
	a.sessions["sidR"] = c

	// Before any inbound: nothing should have been pushed (open() deferred it).
	select {
	case <-c.send:
		t.Fatal("approval pushed before keyId bind (would seal under a nil key)")
	default:
	}

	// First inbound envelope binds the key, then fires the deferred resync.
	env, _ := e2ee.Seal(kP2B, keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, 0, []byte(`{"type":"resume"}`))
	raw, _ := json.Marshal(env)
	if _, ok := a.routeFirstFrameDispatch(context.Background(), c, raw); !ok {
		t.Fatal("first envelope rejected")
	}

	// The deferred resync must now have queued the approval, and it must seal.
	select {
	case v := <-c.send:
		b, err := a.srv.marshalOutbound(c, v)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("resynced approval did not seal: %v", err)
		}
		var sealedEnv e2ee.Envelope
		if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &sealedEnv); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("resync push not an Envelope: %v", err)
		}
		plain, err := e2ee.Open(kB2P, keyID, e2ee.DirB2P, 0, sealedEnv)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("phone could not open resynced approval: %v", err)
		}
		var got map[string]any
		_ = json.Unmarshal(plain, &got)
		if got["type"] != "approval" || got["id"] != "ap1" {
			t.Fatalf("resync payload mismatch: %v", got)
		}
	default:
		t.Fatal("approval not resynced after keyId bind")
	}
}
```

> The test inserts the `pendingApproval` directly into `s.approvals` under `s.apMu` — server.go exposes no helper that takes an explicit id (`onApproval` mints its own via `NewToken`), and a directly-inserted entry is exactly the state a live in-flight approval occupies. Fields match `pendingApproval{ id, method string; params json.RawMessage; ownerDevice string; ch chan string }` (confirm field names against server.go). The assertion (no push pre-bind, sealed push post-bind) is the contract being pinned.

Anchor on the EXACT current comment above `hubFrame` (lines 24–26):

OLD:
```go
// hubFrame is the envelope on the agent<->hub WebSocket. The hub multiplexes many
// phone sessions over this one connection, tagged by SID; Data carries the raw

```

NEW:
```go
// hubFrame is the envelope on the agent<->hub WebSocket. The hub multiplexes many
// phone sessions over this one connection, tagged by SID; Data carries the raw
// phone/bridge protocol object unchanged.
//
// E2EE (hub mode): when the bridge is E2EE-provisioned (Server.devices != nil),
// hubFrame.Data for T:"msg" carries an e2ee.Envelope {k,s,n,c} (or a plaintext
// pair_init/pair_result/pair_error during the one-time handshake) — the hub still
// relays it OPAQUELY and never parses it (it stays protocol-blind). Each SID's
// conn holds its own keyId + seqIn/seqOut; inbound is opened and outbound is
// sealed at the agent boundary via the SAME Server.openInbound/sealOutbound seams
// the direct-mode handleWS uses, so there is one crypto implementation. The conn
// is marked e2ee in open() but produces NO outbound until the first inbound binds
// its key — open() defers the approval resync to that bind, so sealOutbound is
// never called with a nil subkey. A revoked device (devstore.Lookup miss) fails
// openInbound and drops the session. A non-provisioned bridge (devices == nil)
// keeps the legacy cleartext path.
```

### T8: Provision: enroll keys + tray pairing/manage hooks + prod bridge wiring

Layer: `go-provision`. **Depends on:** T3 (devstore: Store/Device/Open/All/Revoke + `Watch(ctx, interval, onReload)`), T4 (devstore: PendingStore/OpenPending/AddPending). Also consumes types produced by T1 (`e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair`) and T6 (`bridge.Server.EnableE2EE`). Because the prod-wiring step (Step 9) imports `e2ee`, `devstore`, and calls `Server.EnableE2EE`, this task must execute after T1, T3, T4, T6 exist; the provision-only steps (1–8) need only T1 (for `e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair`), T3, and T4.

> **Locked-decision override note (read first).** The brief contract lists `devstore.Watch(ctx, onReload)` with **SIGHUP** semantics and a `RevokeDevice` that "signals SIGHUP". **D-a overrides both.** Hot reload is a zero-dependency **mtime poll**: `devstore.Watch(ctx, interval, onReload)` (3 args) stats `devices.json` every ~2s and calls `Reload()` on change. `RevokeDevice` just rewrites `devices.json` (atomic write bumps mtime) and emits **NO signal**. Anywhere this task and the contract disagree on hot-reload, D-a wins; do not "fix" the code back to SIGHUP.

**Files:**
- Modify `bridge/internal/provision/provision.go` — add `GenEnroll`, `enrollKeyPath/enrollPubPath/devicesPath/pendingPath` helpers, `writeFileAtomic`, `randHex`.
- Modify `bridge/internal/provision/pairing.go` (created in Step 5) — `NewPairingID`, `ListDevices`, `RevokeDevice`.
- Modify `bridge/internal/provision/reset_darwin.go` — add exported `ConfigDir()`.
- Modify `bridge/internal/provision/reset_windows.go` — add exported `ConfigDir()`.
- Modify `bridge/internal/provision/reset_other.go` — add `ConfigDir()` stub.
- Create (Test) `bridge/internal/provision/provision_test.go` — GenEnroll perms + idempotency, ListDevices/RevokeDevice round-trip.
- Create (Test) `bridge/internal/provision/pairing_test.go` — NewPairingID TTL write + enrollPub return.
- Modify `bridge/cmd/codexmenubar/main.go` — `dir` field on `config`, v2 JSON `phoneURL()`, `mPair`/`mManage` menu items + handlers.
- Modify `bridge/cmd/codexbridge/main.go` — `serve`: GenEnroll, load enroll.key, open devstore + pending, `Server.EnableE2EE`, start `devstore.Watch`.

Locked-decision compliance baked into this task: **D-a** hot-reload is the mtime-poll `devstore.Watch(ctx, interval, onReload)` (T3) — Step 9 starts it with a 2s interval; `RevokeDevice` just rewrites `devices.json` (atomic write bumps mtime) and the poller reloads it, so NO SIGHUP is emitted. **D-b** all files live in provision's config dir (`filepath.Dir(configPath())` on darwin/windows, exposed as `ConfigDir()`), NOT `~/.codex`. **D-h** prod wiring is in `cmd/codexbridge/main.go serve`. **D-f** the one-time `pairingId` is 16 random bytes hex via `crypto/rand` (`randHex(16)` — byte-identical to `bridge.NewToken()`, but local + error-checked). The tray stays crypto-free (only `crypto/rand` for the id + reads `enroll.pub`).

  ```go
  func TestGenEnrollWritesKeysWithCorrectPerms(t *testing.T) {
  	dir := t.TempDir()
  	pub, err := GenEnroll(dir)
  	if err != nil {
  ```

  Then append to the end of `provision.go` (after `hostFromHub`):
  ```go
  // ---- E2EE provisioning ----------------------------------------------------
  //
  // GenEnroll (here) plus ListDevices/RevokeDevice/NewPairingID (pairing.go) are the
  // provision-layer hooks for the E2EE device model. All state lives in `dir`
  // (ConfigDir()): enroll.key (0600, the machine's long-term nacl/box secret),
  // enroll.pub (0644, published in the QR), devices.json (0600, paired devices) and
  // pending-pairings.json (0600, one-time pairing ids the bridge consumes). The tray
  // stays crypto-free — it only generates random ids and reads enroll.pub; key
  // material is touched only here.

  func enrollKeyPath(dir string) string { return filepath.Join(dir, "enroll.key") }
  func enrollPubPath(dir string) string { return filepath.Join(dir, "enroll.pub") }
  func devicesPath(dir string) string   { return filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json") }
  func pendingPath(dir string) string   { return filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json") }

  // GenEnroll ensures the machine has a long-term enroll keypair under dir and
  // returns the 32-byte public key. It is idempotent: if enroll.key already exists
  // the existing enroll.pub is READ and returned (the keypair is never regenerated),
  // so re-running the installer never invalidates already-paired devices. On a fresh
  // machine it generates the keypair, writes enroll.key (0600) and enroll.pub (0644).
  func GenEnroll(dir string) (pub []byte, err error) {
  	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700); err != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("mkdir config dir: %w", err)
  	}
  	// Idempotent path: enroll.key already present -> read & return the existing
  	// enroll.pub (contract: "existing keys are read, not regenerated").
  	if _, statErr := os.Stat(enrollKeyPath(dir)); statErr == nil {
  		onDisk, rerr := os.ReadFile(enrollPubPath(dir))
  		if rerr != nil {
  			return nil, fmt.Errorf("enroll.key exists but enroll.pub is unreadable: %w", rerr)
  		}
  		if len(onDisk) != 32 {
  			return nil, fmt.Errorf("enroll.pub is %d bytes, want 32 (corrupt enroll state)", len(onDisk))
  		}
  		return onDisk, nil
  	}
  	// Fresh machine: generate and persist the keypair. GenEnrollKeypair returns
  	// *[32]byte for both halves (T1 contract).
  	pkp, skp, gerr := e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair()
  	if gerr != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("generate enroll keypair: %w", gerr)
  	}
  	if werr := writeFileAtomic(enrollKeyPath(dir), skp[:], 0o600); werr != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("write enroll.key: %w", werr)
  	}
  	if werr := writeFileAtomic(enrollPubPath(dir), pkp[:], 0o644); werr != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("write enroll.pub: %w", werr)
  	}
  	return pkp[:], nil
  }

  // writeFileAtomic writes b to path via a .new temp + rename (same pattern as
  // setEnvKey), so a crash mid-write never leaves a half-written key file.
  func writeFileAtomic(path string, b []byte, mode os.FileMode) error {
  	tmp := path + ".new"
  	if err := os.WriteFile(tmp, b, mode); err != nil {
  		return err
  	}
  	return os.Rename(tmp, path)
  }

  // randHex returns n random bytes as a lowercase hex string (crypto/rand). Used for
  // the one-time pairingId. With n=16 this is byte-format-identical to
  // bridge.NewToken() (D-f), but kept local so provision needn't import package
  // bridge and so the crypto/rand error is propagated rather than swallowed.
  func randHex(n int) (string, error) {
  	b := make([]byte, n)
  	if _, err := rand.Read(b); err != nil {
  		return "", err
  	}
  	return hex.EncodeToString(b), nil
  }
  ```
  Note: this consumes `e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair() (pub, sec *[32]byte, err error)` from T1 verbatim — no other e2ee symbol is referenced (the idempotent path reads enroll.pub from disk, so no public-from-secret derivation is needed and no extra e2ee export is invented).
  - Run: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/provision/ -run TestGenEnroll -v`
  - Expected: PASS — `TestGenEnrollWritesKeysWithCorrectPerms` and `TestGenEnrollIdempotent` both ok.

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `NewPairingID` in a new `pairing.go` to make Step 4 PASS.**
  Create `bridge/internal/provision/pairing.go`:
  ```go
  package provision

  import (
  	"encoding/base64"
  	"fmt"
  	"os"
  	"time"

  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/devstore"
  )

  // NewPairingID is the tray's "配对新设备" helper. It mints a one-time pairingId
  // (16 random bytes hex via randHex — no crypto beyond crypto/rand), appends it to
  // pending-pairings.json with the given TTL (5min at the call site), reads
  // enroll.pub, and returns the pairingId plus the base64 enroll public key for the
  // v2 QR payload. The bridge later consumes the id exactly once during pairing.
  func NewPairingID(dir string, ttl time.Duration) (pairingID, enrollPubB64 string, err error) {
  	id, err := randHex(16)
  	if err != nil {
  		return "", "", fmt.Errorf("generate pairingId: %w", err)
  	}
  	pend := devstore.OpenPending(pendingPath(dir))
  	if err := pend.AddPending(id, ttl); err != nil {
  		return "", "", fmt.Errorf("write pending pairing: %w", err)
  	}
  	pub, err := os.ReadFile(enrollPubPath(dir))
  	if err != nil {
  		return "", "", fmt.Errorf("read enroll.pub (run GenEnroll first): %w", err)
  	}
  	return id, base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(pub), nil
  }
  ```
  This consumes `devstore.OpenPending(path string) *PendingStore` and `(*PendingStore).AddPending(pairingID string, ttl time.Duration) error` from T4 verbatim.

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement `ListDevices` / `RevokeDevice` to make Step 6 PASS.**
  Append to `bridge/internal/provision/pairing.go` (it already imports `devstore` and `fmt`):
  ```go
  // ListDevices returns every paired device for the tray's "管理/吊销设备" list.
  // It opens devices.json read-only (missing file => empty slice).
  func ListDevices(dir string) ([]devstore.Device, error) {
  	st, err := devstore.Open(devicesPath(dir))
  	if err != nil {
  		return nil, fmt.Errorf("open devices.json: %w", err)
  	}
  	return st.All(), nil
  }

  // RevokeDevice removes keyID from devices.json and returns whether it was present.
  // D-a OVERRIDES the contract's "SIGHUP on revoke": the atomic rewrite bumps the
  // file mtime, which the bridge's mtime-poll devstore.Watch picks up on its next
  // tick — so NO signal is sent. The revoked device's frames become undecryptable
  // within the poll interval (~2s).
  func RevokeDevice(dir, keyID string) (bool, error) {
  	st, err := devstore.Open(devicesPath(dir))
  	if err != nil {
  		return false, fmt.Errorf("open devices.json: %w", err)
  	}
  	removed, err := st.Revoke(keyID)
  	if err != nil {
  		return false, fmt.Errorf("revoke %s: %w", keyID, err)
  	}
  	return removed, nil
  }
  ```
  This consumes `devstore.Open(path string) (*Store, error)`, `(*Store).All() []Device`, and `(*Store).Revoke(keyID string) (bool, error)` from T3 verbatim.
