  Then add the `enableE2EE` helper at the end of `main.go` (after `fatal`):
  ```go
  // enableE2EE provisions and wires end-to-end encryption onto srv. Additive and
  // idempotent (D-g/D-h): it ensures the machine's enroll keypair exists, loads
  // enroll.key as the nacl/box secret used to seal pair_result, opens devices.json +
  // pending-pairings.json from the provision config dir (D-b), calls srv.EnableE2EE,
  // and starts the mtime-poll devstore.Watch (D-a: zero-dependency hot reload, ~2s
  // interval) so a revoked/added device takes effect without restarting the bridge.
  // The Watch goroutine ends with ctx.
  func enableE2EE(ctx context.Context, srv *bridge.Server) error {
  	dir := provision.ConfigDir()
  	if _, err := provision.GenEnroll(dir); err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("provision enroll keys: %w", err)
  	}
  	sec, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(dir, "enroll.key"))
  	if err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("read enroll.key: %w", err)
  	}
  	if len(sec) != 32 {
  		return fmt.Errorf("enroll.key is %d bytes, want 32", len(sec))
  	}
  	var enrollSec [32]byte
  	copy(enrollSec[:], sec)

  	devices, err := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if err != nil {
  		return fmt.Errorf("open devices.json: %w", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))

  	// D-g: additive setter, no breaking NewServer change.
  	srv.EnableE2EE(devices, &enrollSec, pending)

  	// D-a hot reload: poll devices.json mtime every ~2s; on change Reload() the
  	// in-memory map (Reload keeps the old map on a read/parse error and reports it
  	// for audit). 3-arg mtime-poll signature, NOT the contract's SIGHUP Watch.
  	go devices.Watch(ctx, 2*time.Second, func(err error) {
  		if err != nil {
  			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[e2ee] devices.json reload failed (keeping old map): %v\n", err)
  		}
  	})
  	fmt.Printf("codex-remote bridge: E2EE enabled (enroll keys + devices store in %s)\n", dir)
  	return nil
  }
  ```
  Consumed contract names: `srv.EnableE2EE(devices *devstore.Store, enrollSec *[32]byte, pending *devstore.PendingStore)` is the T6 additive helper (D-g). `devices.Watch(ctx, 2*time.Second, func(error){…})` is T3's locked **D-a mtime-poll** signature `Watch(ctx, interval, onReload)` (a method on `*Store`) — this supersedes the contract's 2-arg SIGHUP `Watch(ctx, onReload)`. `ctx` is the existing `ctx := context.Background()` already declared at the top of `runServe`, so the goroutine runs for the process lifetime, which is correct for a long-lived serve.

  - Expected: builds clean, no vet output.
  - Run: `cd bridge && go test ./...`
  - Expected: PASS — provision tests plus all existing bridge/devstore/e2ee tests green.
  - Commit: `git add bridge/cmd/codexbridge/main.go && git commit -m "feat(bridge): wire E2EE in serve — enroll keys, devstore, EnableE2EE, mtime Watch"`

**Out-of-scope / conscious limitations to record (D-j, D-k):** the miniprogram `relay.js` is intentionally NOT updated — an E2EE-provisioned bridge will refuse miniprogram cleartext; this is the documented v1 limitation (D-j). In E2EE mode the audit/approval device name comes from `devstore.Lookup(keyId).Name` (bound at the first envelope frame in T6's `openInbound`, D-k), so the per-device `name` the tray persists into `devices.json` (written by the T5 pairing handler) flows through to approval ownership — no change needed in this task beyond persisting `Name`, which T5 already does.

### T9: Dart e2ee codec + kdf mirror with pinned vector

Mirror of the Go `e2ee` package (T1) on the Flutter phone side: the HKDF-SHA256 key derivation and the XChaCha20-Poly1305 content codec, byte-for-byte compatible with Go so the phone and bridge can talk over the encrypted `/ws` channel. Built TDD against the **pinned cross-language vector printed by T1's Go test** — Go is the oracle, Dart pins its three printed base64 constants (`kP2B`, `kB2P`, ciphertext) and asserts equality in both directions.

**Files:**
- Modify: `app/pubspec.yaml` (add `cryptography: ^2.7.0`; `pinenacl` is added later in the pairing task — not here)
- Create: `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart`
- Create: `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart`
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/kdf_test.dart`
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart`

**Depends on:** T1 (provides the Go `e2ee` package whose `codec_test.go`/`kdf_test.go` PRINT the pinned `base64(kP2B)`, `base64(kB2P)`, and `base64(ciphertext)` for the fixed inputs — those three printed constants are copied verbatim into the Dart tests below). The fixed inputs are locked by the contract: `psk` = 32 bytes `0x01..0x20` (byte i = i+1), `keyId = "k_test_0001"`, `nonce` = 24 bytes `0xA0..0xB7` (byte i = 0xA0+i), `seq = 0`, `dir = p2b`, `plaintext` = compact JSON bytes of `{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}`. Field names / json tags / info strings / AAD format come from `contract.dartApi` + `contract.dataFormats` and must NOT be renamed.

> Locked decisions honored here: **D-d** — Dart AEAD/HKDF via `package:cryptography` (`Xchacha20.poly1305Aead`, `Hkdf`+`Hmac.sha256()`); NaCl box (`pinenacl`) belongs to the pairing task (T10), not this codec task, so this task adds only `cryptography`. The AAD wire format and HKDF info strings exactly mirror Go `aad()` / `DeriveKeys`. The locked public surface (`contract.dartApi`) is `E2eeCodec({keyId, kP2B, kB2P})` with `int outSeq=0`, `int inSeq=0`, `Future<Map> seal(plain)`, `Future<Map> open(envJson)` — these signatures are NOT changed. The only additions are two deterministic test seams that mirror Go's internal `sealWithNonce`: `sealWithNonce(plain, dir, seq, nonce)` and `openAt(env, dir, expectSeq)`. Repo house style: one small file per unit + a top-of-file doc-comment as the spec, immutability, comprehensive error handling.

> Cross-language byte-compatibility note (load-bearing): three layout details must match Go exactly or the pinned vector fails.
> 1. **Tag placement.** Go's `golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20poly1305` `Seal` **appends** the 16-byte Poly1305 tag to the ciphertext, so the wire field `c` = `cipherText || tag`. `package:cryptography` keeps the MAC separate (`SecretBox.cipherText` + `SecretBox.mac`). Therefore Dart `seal` concatenates `secretBox.cipherText + secretBox.mac.bytes` to build `c`, and Dart `open` splits `c` into `ct = c[0 .. len-16]` and `mac = c[len-16 .. len]` before constructing the `SecretBox`. Get this split wrong and the pinned vector fails.
> 2. **Plaintext key order.** The pinned ciphertext is over the exact bytes `{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}` — `type` BEFORE `text`. Dart `jsonEncode` of a map literal preserves insertion order, so the test's `fixedPlain()` MUST list `'type'` before `'text'`. (Go's oracle in T1 must encrypt those exact bytes via a struct/raw bytes, NOT a sorted `map[string]any`, since `encoding/json` would otherwise sort keys to `text,type`.) If only the pinned-vector tests fail while round-trip/tamper/seq tests pass, check the plaintext key order and the pasted base64 constant FIRST — not the AAD format or the tag split.
> 3. **AAD bytes.** `_aad` = ASCII `keyId|dir|seq`, `seq` decimal, `dir` in `{p2b,b2p}` — byte-identical to Go `aad()`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `kdf.dart` (GREEN).** Create `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart`. The signature, info strings, and salt are locked by `contract.dartApi`: `Hkdf(hmac: Hmac.sha256(), outputLength: 32)`, `nonce`(=salt)`= raw bytes of keyId`, `kP2B info = 'codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge'`, `kB2P info = 'codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone'`.

  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';

  import 'package:cryptography/cryptography.dart';

  /// HKDF-SHA256 key derivation for the E2EE content plane (mirror of Go
  /// `e2ee.DeriveKeys`).
  ///
  /// From the per-device pre-shared key (`psk`, 32 bytes) and the plaintext
  /// `keyId` we derive two one-directional sub-keys so the two directions never
  /// share a key (which would risk nonce reuse):
  ///
  ///   kP2B = HKDF-SHA256(psk, salt=keyId-bytes, info="codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge")
  ///   kB2P = HKDF-SHA256(psk, salt=keyId-bytes, info="codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone")
  ///
  /// Outputs are byte-identical to the Go bridge so the same `psk`/`keyId`
  /// produce the same sub-keys on both ends. `keyId`'s ASCII bytes are the HKDF
  /// salt; the two info strings are the only thing distinguishing the directions.
  const String kInfoP2B = 'codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge';
  const String kInfoB2P = 'codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone';

  final _hkdf = Hkdf(hmac: Hmac.sha256(), outputLength: 32);

  /// Derives `(kP2B, kB2P)` from `psk` and `keyId`.
  ///
  /// Throws [ArgumentError] if `psk` is empty (a missing PSK means the device is
  /// not actually paired and must re-scan — fail loudly rather than derive a
  /// key from nothing).
  Future<(List<int> kP2B, List<int> kB2P)> deriveKeys(
    List<int> psk,
    String keyId,
  ) async {
    if (psk.isEmpty) {
      throw ArgumentError.value(psk, 'psk', 'must not be empty');
    }
    final secret = SecretKey(psk);
    final salt = utf8.encode(keyId);
    final kP2B = await _deriveOne(secret, salt, kInfoP2B);
    final kB2P = await _deriveOne(secret, salt, kInfoB2P);
    return (kP2B, kB2P);
  }

  Future<List<int>> _deriveOne(
    SecretKey secret,
    List<int> salt,
    String info,
  ) async {
    final derived = await _hkdf.deriveKey(
      secretKey: secret,
      nonce: salt, // package:cryptography names the HKDF salt `nonce`
      info: utf8.encode(info),
    );
    return derived.extractBytes();
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the failing codec test (RED).** Create `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart` with the complete test below. Paste T1's printed `base64(ciphertext)` (the env `c` for the fixed inputs) into `kPinnedCiphertext`. The HKDF sub-keys are derived by the (now-passing) `deriveKeys`, so the codec test consumes the real keys end-to-end. `sealWithNonce` is the deterministic, test-only nonce variant (the mirror of Go's internal `sealWithNonce`) and takes an explicit `Dir` so a test can fabricate either a P2B (pinned-vector) or a B2P (round-trip) frame; production `seal` uses a fresh random 24-byte nonce and is always P2B. `openAt` is the explicit-direction decrypt seam used to read the pinned P2B vector (production `open` is always B2P, seq-checked).

  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';

  import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
  import 'package:codex_remote_app/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart';
  import 'package:codex_remote_app/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart';

  // --- Pinned cross-language vector (produced by the Go e2ee/codec_test.go oracle). ---
  // Fixed inputs (contract):
  //   psk    = bytes 0x01..0x20
  //   keyId  = "k_test_0001"
  //   nonce  = 24 bytes 0xA0..0xB7  (byte i = 0xA0 + i)
  //   seq    = 0, dir = p2b  -> aad = "k_test_0001|p2b|0"
  //   plain  = compact JSON {"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}  (type BEFORE text)
  // Paste the exact base64 ciphertext (env.C) PRINTED by the Go test:
  const String kPinnedCiphertext = 'PASTE_BASE64_CIPHERTEXT_FROM_GO_TEST';

  const String fixedKeyId = 'k_test_0001';
  List<int> fixedPsk() => List<int>.generate(32, (i) => i + 1);
  // 24 bytes, byte i = 0xA0 + i  (0xA0 .. 0xB7)
  List<int> fixedNonce() => List<int>.generate(24, (i) => 0xA0 + i);
  // Plaintext as the COMPACT JSON object map (no spaces). Key order is
  // LOAD-BEARING: 'type' MUST come before 'text' so jsonEncode reproduces the
  // exact bytes Go's oracle encrypted ({"type":...,"text":...}).
  Map<String, dynamic> fixedPlain() => {'type': 'prompt', 'text': 'hello e2ee'};
  // The exact compact-JSON bytes Go encrypts (used to assert the decrypted bytes).
  const String fixedPlainJson = '{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}';

  Future<E2eeCodec> buildCodec() async {
    final (kP2B, kB2P) = await deriveKeys(fixedPsk(), fixedKeyId);
    return E2eeCodec(keyId: fixedKeyId, kP2B: kP2B, kB2P: kB2P);
  }

  void main() {
    group('Envelope', () {
      test('toJson/fromJson round-trip with k,s,n,c tags', () {
        const env = Envelope(k: 'k_test_0001', s: 7, n: 'bm9uY2U=', c: 'Y3Q=');
        final j = env.toJson();
        expect(j, {'k': 'k_test_0001', 's': 7, 'n': 'bm9uY2U=', 'c': 'Y3Q='});
        final back = Envelope.fromJson(j);
        expect(back.k, env.k);
        expect(back.s, env.s);
        expect(back.n, env.n);
        expect(back.c, env.c);
      });
    });

    group('dirTag', () {
      test('matches Go Dir.String()', () {
        expect(dirTag(Dir.p2b), 'p2b');
        expect(dirTag(Dir.b2p), 'b2p');
      });
    });

    group('E2eeCodec sealWithNonce (pinned vector)', () {
      test('produces the pinned Go ciphertext for fixed nonce/seq/aad', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final env = await codec.sealWithNonce(fixedPlain(), Dir.p2b, 0, fixedNonce());
        expect(env.k, fixedKeyId);
        expect(env.s, 0);
        expect(env.n, base64.encode(fixedNonce()));
        expect(env.c, kPinnedCiphertext);
      });
    });

    group('E2eeCodec.openAt (pinned vector)', () {
      test('decrypts the pinned Go envelope back to the plaintext', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        // The pinned env was produced with the P2B subkey/dir; the production
        // open() is B2P-only, so read it via the direction-explicit seam.
        final env = Envelope(
          k: fixedKeyId,
          s: 0,
          n: base64.encode(fixedNonce()),
          c: kPinnedCiphertext,
        );
        final plain = await codec.openAt(env, Dir.p2b, 0);
        expect(plain, fixedPlain());
        expect(jsonEncode(plain), fixedPlainJson);
      });
    });

    group('E2eeCodec seal/open round-trip + sequencing', () {
      test('seal then open (cross sub-keys) recovers the message', () async {
        // Phone seals P2B, bridge would decrypt P2B; to test open() locally we
        // re-encrypt under the B2P subkey so the phone-side open() can read it.
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final msg = {'type': 'turn', 'id': 'abc', 'n': 1};
        // Build a bridge->phone frame by sealing under B2P at seq 0.
        final bridgeFrame =
            await codec.sealWithNonce(msg, Dir.b2p, 0, fixedNonce());
        final got = await codec.open(bridgeFrame.toJson());
        expect(got, msg);
      });

      test('seal increments outSeq each call', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        expect(codec.outSeq, 0);
        await codec.seal({'type': 'a'});
        expect(codec.outSeq, 1);
        await codec.seal({'type': 'b'});
        expect(codec.outSeq, 2);
      });

      test('open increments inSeq on success', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final f0 = await codec.sealWithNonce({'type': 'x'}, Dir.b2p, 0, fixedNonce());
        await codec.open(f0.toJson());
        expect(codec.inSeq, 1);
      });

      test('open rejects a wrong keyId', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final f = await codec.sealWithNonce({'type': 'x'}, Dir.b2p, 0, fixedNonce());
        final bad = f.toJson()..['k'] = 'k_other_0001';
        expect(() => codec.open(bad), throwsA(isA<E2eeException>()));
      });

      test('open rejects a seq rollback / non-increasing seq', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        // Deliver seq 0 then seq 0 again (replay) — second must throw.
        final f0 = await codec.sealWithNonce({'type': 'x'}, Dir.b2p, 0, fixedNonce());
        await codec.open(f0.toJson());
        final replay = f0.toJson(); // still s == 0, but inSeq is now 1
        expect(() => codec.open(replay), throwsA(isA<E2eeException>()));
      });

      test('open rejects a future/out-of-order seq', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final f5 = await codec.sealWithNonce({'type': 'x'}, Dir.b2p, 5, fixedNonce());
        expect(() => codec.open(f5.toJson()), throwsA(isA<E2eeException>()));
      });

      test('open rejects a tampered ciphertext (auth failure)', () async {
        final codec = await buildCodec();
        final f = await codec.sealWithNonce({'type': 'x'}, Dir.b2p, 0, fixedNonce());
        // Flip one byte of the base64-decoded ciphertext and re-encode.
        final ct = base64.decode(f.c);
        ct[0] = ct[0] ^ 0x01;
        final tampered = f.toJson()..['c'] = base64.encode(ct);
        expect(() => codec.open(tampered), throwsA(isA<E2eeException>()));
        // A rejected frame must NOT advance the expected sequence.
        expect(codec.inSeq, 0);
      });
    });
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement `codec.dart` (GREEN).** Create `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart`. The LOCKED public surface (`contract.dartApi`) is `Envelope{k,s,n,c}` + `toJson`/`fromJson`, `enum Dir{p2b,b2p}` + `dirTag`, `_aad`, and `E2eeCodec{keyId, outSeq, inSeq, seal, open}` — these are NOT renamed. The two added test seams `sealWithNonce(plain, dir, seq, nonce)` and `openAt(env, dir, expectSeq)` are the deterministic mirrors of Go's internal `sealWithNonce` (one each direction; no duplication). `E2eeException` is the codec's single error type (decrypt/auth fail, unknown keyId, seq rollback) so callers can drop-and-log without leaking detail.

  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';
  import 'dart:typed_data';

  import 'package:cryptography/cryptography.dart';

  /// Content-plane codec for the E2EE `/ws` channel (mirror of Go `e2ee.codec`).
  ///
  /// Wire envelope (every encrypted frame; on a paired connection the line
  /// carries only these): `{"k":keyId,"s":seq,"n":base64(nonce),"c":base64(ct)}`.
  ///   - `k` plaintext keyId — lets the bridge pick the PSK (not a secret).
  ///   - `s` monotonic sequence; the receiver rejects non-increasing `s` (replay).
  ///   - `n` a fresh random 24-byte XChaCha20 nonce per frame.
  ///   - `c` = ciphertext || 16-byte Poly1305 tag (Go appends the tag; we match).
  ///
  /// AAD (authenticated, NOT on the wire) = ASCII `keyId|dir|seq`, `seq` decimal,
  /// `dir` in {p2b, b2p} — byte-identical to Go `aad()`. The phone ENCRYPTS with
  /// the P2B sub-key and DECRYPTS with the B2P sub-key (symmetric to the bridge).
  /// Decrypted bytes are the original `{"type":...}` protocol object, so the
  /// upper protocol is unchanged.

  /// Direction of a frame; [dirTag] must equal Go `Dir.String()`.
  enum Dir { p2b, b2p }

  String dirTag(Dir d) => d == Dir.p2b ? 'p2b' : 'b2p';

  /// AAD builder — MUST produce the same bytes as Go `aad()`:
  /// ASCII `keyId|dir|seq`, `seq` decimal, no padding.
  List<int> _aad(String keyId, Dir dir, int seq) =>
      utf8.encode('$keyId|${dirTag(dir)}|$seq');

  /// Thrown on any codec failure (auth/decrypt failure, unknown keyId, seq
  /// rollback). Callers drop-and-log; the message text never leaks plaintext.
  class E2eeException implements Exception {
    const E2eeException(this.message);
    final String message;
    @override
    String toString() => 'E2eeException: $message';
  }

  /// Mirror of the Go `Envelope`. JSON keys `k,s,n,c` byte-match Go.
  class Envelope {
    final String k; // keyId
    final int s; // seq
    final String n; // base64 nonce (24B)
    final String c; // base64 ciphertext || tag

    const Envelope({
      required this.k,
      required this.s,
      required this.n,
      required this.c,
    });

    Map<String, dynamic> toJson() => {'k': k, 's': s, 'n': n, 'c': c};

    factory Envelope.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> j) {
      final k = j['k'];
      final s = j['s'];
      final n = j['n'];
      final c = j['c'];
      if (k is! String || s is! int || n is! String || c is! String) {
        throw const E2eeException('malformed envelope');
      }
      return Envelope(k: k, s: s, n: n, c: c);
    }
  }

  // The Go x/crypto chacha20poly1305 Seal appends a 16-byte Poly1305 tag to the
  // ciphertext; package:cryptography keeps the MAC separate. We bridge the two.
  const int _macLen = 16;

  final Xchacha20 _cipher = Xchacha20.poly1305Aead();

  /// Phone-side content codec. One per paired machine connection; reused across
  /// reconnects (never re-paired). [outSeq] is the next phone->bridge sequence,
  /// [inSeq] the next expected bridge->phone sequence.
  class E2eeCodec {
    E2eeCodec({
      required this.keyId,
      required List<int> kP2B,
      required List<int> kB2P,
    })  : _kP2B = SecretKey(kP2B),
        _kB2P = SecretKey(kB2P);

    final String keyId;
    final SecretKey _kP2B;
    final SecretKey _kB2P;

    int outSeq = 0; // phone->bridge
    int inSeq = 0; // expected bridge->phone

    SecretKey _keyFor(Dir dir) => dir == Dir.p2b ? _kP2B : _kB2P;

    /// Encrypts [plain] for the bridge under the P2B sub-key with a fresh random
    /// 24-byte nonce, then advances [outSeq]. Returns the envelope as JSON.
    Future<Map<String, dynamic>> seal(Map<String, dynamic> plain) async {
      final nonce = _cipher.newNonce(); // 24 random bytes
      final env = await _seal(plain, Dir.p2b, outSeq, nonce);
      outSeq++;
      return env.toJson();
    }

    /// Test seam (mirror of Go's internal `sealWithNonce`): seal in an EXPLICIT
    /// direction with a CALLER-SUPPLIED nonce and seq, WITHOUT touching [outSeq].
    /// Lets a test pin the P2B vector or fabricate a B2P frame for [open]/[openAt].
    Future<Envelope> sealWithNonce(
      Map<String, dynamic> plain,
      Dir dir,
      int seq,
      List<int> nonce,
    ) =>
        _seal(plain, dir, seq, nonce);

    Future<Envelope> _seal(
      Map<String, dynamic> plain,
      Dir dir,
      int seq,
      List<int> nonce,
    ) async {
      final clear = utf8.encode(jsonEncode(plain));
      final box = await _cipher.encrypt(
        clear,
        secretKey: _keyFor(dir),
        nonce: nonce,
        aad: _aad(keyId, dir, seq),
      );
      // Go wire format: ciphertext || tag.
      final wire = Uint8List(box.cipherText.length + box.mac.bytes.length)
        ..setAll(0, box.cipherText)
        ..setAll(box.cipherText.length, box.mac.bytes);
      return Envelope(
        k: keyId,
        s: seq,
        n: base64.encode(nonce),
        c: base64.encode(wire),
      );
    }

    /// Decrypts a bridge->phone [envJson]: requires `k == keyId` and
    /// `s == inSeq` (rejecting replay / out-of-order), decrypts under the B2P
    /// sub-key, advances [inSeq] only on success. Throws [E2eeException] on any
    /// failure (a rejected frame leaves [inSeq] unchanged so the caller can
    /// drop-and-log without desyncing the channel).
    Future<Map<String, dynamic>> open(Map<String, dynamic> envJson) async {
      final env = Envelope.fromJson(envJson);
      if (env.k != keyId) {
        throw const E2eeException('unknown keyId');
      }
      if (env.s != inSeq) {
        throw E2eeException('seq out of order: got ${env.s}, want $inSeq');
      }
      final plain = await _open(env, Dir.b2p);
      inSeq++;
      return plain;
    }

    /// Test seam: decrypt an EXPLICIT-direction [env] at the given expected seq,
    /// WITHOUT touching [inSeq]. Mirrors how the pinned P2B vector is produced by
    /// Go and re-opened here (the production [open] is B2P-only).
    Future<Map<String, dynamic>> openAt(
      Envelope env,
      Dir dir,
      int expectSeq,
    ) async {
      if (env.k != keyId) {
        throw const E2eeException('unknown keyId');
      }
      if (env.s != expectSeq) {
        throw E2eeException('seq mismatch: got ${env.s}, want $expectSeq');
      }
      return _open(env, dir);
    }

    Future<Map<String, dynamic>> _open(Envelope env, Dir dir) async {
      final nonce = base64.decode(env.n);
      final wire = base64.decode(env.c);
      if (wire.length < _macLen) {
        throw const E2eeException('ciphertext too short');
      }
      final ct = wire.sublist(0, wire.length - _macLen);
      final mac = Mac(wire.sublist(wire.length - _macLen));
      final box = SecretBox(ct, nonce: nonce, mac: mac);
      final List<int> clear;
      try {
        clear = await _cipher.decrypt(
          box,
          secretKey: _keyFor(dir),
          aad: _aad(keyId, dir, env.s),
        );
      } on SecretBoxAuthenticationError {
        throw const E2eeException('authentication failed');
      }
      final decoded = jsonDecode(utf8.decode(clear));
      if (decoded is! Map<String, dynamic>) {
        throw const E2eeException('plaintext is not a JSON object');
      }
      return decoded;
    }
  }
  ```

  Run to PASS:
  ```
  cd app && flutter test test/e2ee/codec_test.dart
  ```

If the two pinned-vector tests fail while the round-trip/tamper/seq tests pass, the cause is one of: the pasted `kPinnedCiphertext` constant, the plaintext key order (`type` before `text` in `fixedPlain()`), the `c` split (ciphertext||tag), or different HKDF keys — re-copy T1's printed `env.C` and verify the plaintext bytes; do NOT alter the AAD format or the ciphertext||tag layout.

### T10: Dart pairing + QR v2 + model/secure-store + client integration

Wires the phone side of E2EE pairing: parse the v2 QR, generate the device keypair, run the `pair_init`/`pair_result` handshake (NaCl box open of the sealed PSK), persist `keyId`/PSK/device-secret, and seal/open every session frame through the T9 `E2eeCodec`. Crypto primitives mirror the Go bridge byte-for-byte (pinenacl `Box` == `x/crypto/nacl/box`; per **D-d**).

**Files:**
- Modify: `app/pubspec.yaml` (add `pinenacl` for the NaCl box; `cryptography` already added in T9)
- Create: `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/pairing.dart` (`genDeviceKeypair`, `buildPairInit`, `openPairResult`)
- Create: `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/qr_v2.dart` (`QrV2` + `QrV2.tryParse`)
- Modify: `app/lib/src/models/machine.dart` (add `keyId`, `account`)
- Modify: `app/lib/src/services/secure_store.dart` (`savePsk`/`loadPsk`/`saveDeviceKey`/`loadDeviceKey`/`deleteCrypto`)
- Modify: `app/lib/src/services/bridge_client.dart` (`_codec`/`_paired`, seal in `_send`, open+seq-check in `_onData`, reconnect reuses codec, disconnect clears)
- Modify: `app/lib/src/ui/connect_screen.dart` (detect v2 QR → pairing handshake → persist before `_connect`)
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/qr_v2_test.dart` (`tryParse` valid/invalid)
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/machine_test.dart` (JSON round-trip with new fields)
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/pairing_test.dart` (`openPairResult` against a Go-sealed fixture + wrong-key reject)
- Test: `app/test/e2ee/client_codec_test.dart` (`_send` seal envelope + `_onData` open/seq/replay/tamper)

**Depends on:** T9 (`app/lib/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart` provides `E2eeCodec({required keyId, required kP2B, required kB2P})` with `seal`/`open` + `Dir`/`dirTag`; `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart` provides `deriveKeys(psk, keyId) -> (kP2B, kB2P)`). T1 (Go `e2ee.SealPairing`) is the oracle that produces the pairing fixture used in `pairing_test.dart`.

> **T9 codec orientation (load-bearing for the tests below):** `E2eeCodec` is phone-oriented and asymmetric — `seal` ALWAYS encrypts under `kP2B` with AAD `keyId|p2b|outSeq`, `open` ALWAYS decrypts under `kB2P` with AAD `keyId|b2p|inSeq`. A bridge→phone frame must therefore be sealed with the `b2p` AAD; you CANNOT simulate the bridge by handing a second `E2eeCodec` swapped keys (its `seal` would still emit `p2b` AAD and the phone `open` would MAC-fail). `client_codec_test.dart` uses a small `bridgeSeal` helper for the inbound direction.

- [ ] **Step 3 (GREEN): Implement `QrV2`.**
  Create `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/qr_v2.dart`. `tryParse` returns `null` on anything that is not a strict v2 JSON object (so the caller can fall back to the legacy bare-token / wss path).
  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';

  /// Parsed v2 pairing QR payload emitted by the desktop tray (`phoneURL()`).
  ///
  /// Wire shape (field names MUST match the Go emitter byte-for-byte):
  /// ```json
  /// { "v":2, "machine":"mac-studio", "host":"relay.example.com",
  ///   "token":"<route token>", "pairingId":"<one-time, TTL 5min>",
  ///   "enrollPub":"<machine enroll public key, base64 std 32B>" }
  /// ```
  /// The payload carries NO secret: `token` only routes/rate-limits at the hub,
  /// `pairingId` is single-use, and `enrollPub` is public. [enrollPub] is stored
  /// already base64-decoded so the pairing handshake can use it directly.
  class QrV2 {
    final int v; // == 2
    final String machine;
    final String host;
    final String token;
    final String pairingId;
    final List<int> enrollPub; // decoded from base64 std

    const QrV2({
      required this.v,
      required this.machine,
      required this.host,
      required this.token,
      required this.pairingId,
      required this.enrollPub,
    });

    /// Returns a [QrV2] only for a strict v2 JSON object; `null` for anything
    /// else (bare token, wss URL, wrong version, missing/empty field, bad
    /// base64). A `null` lets the connect screen fall back to the legacy path.
    static QrV2? tryParse(String scanned) {
      Map<String, dynamic> j;
      try {
        final decoded = jsonDecode(scanned.trim());
        if (decoded is! Map<String, dynamic>) return null;
        j = decoded;
      } catch (_) {
        return null;
      }
      if (j['v'] != 2) return null; // exact int 2; rejects "2" and any other v

      String? field(String k) {
        final val = j[k];
        if (val is! String || val.isEmpty) return null;
        return val;
      }

      final machine = field('machine');
      final host = field('host');
      final token = field('token');
      final pairingId = field('pairingId');
      final enrollB64 = field('enrollPub');
      if (machine == null ||
          host == null ||
          token == null ||
          pairingId == null ||
          enrollB64 == null) {
        return null;
      }
      List<int> enrollPub;
      try {
        enrollPub = base64.decode(enrollB64);
      } catch (_) {
        return null;
      }
      if (enrollPub.length != 32) return null;
      return QrV2(
        v: 2,
        machine: machine,
        host: host,
        token: token,
        pairingId: pairingId,
        enrollPub: enrollPub,
      );
    }
  }
  ```
  Run: `cd app && flutter test test/e2ee/qr_v2_test.dart`
  Expected: PASS — all five tests green.

  ```

  import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';

  void main() {
    test('round-trips keyId + account through toJson/fromJson', () {

        host: 'relay.example.com',
        keyId: 'k7f3deadbeef',

      );

      expect(j['keyId'], 'k7f3deadbeef');

      expect(back.token, 'route-token');
      expect(back.host, 'relay.example.com');

      expect(j.containsKey('keyId'), isFalse);
      expect(j.containsKey('account'), isFalse);

      expect(back.keyId, isNull);
      expect(back.account, isNull);

    test('copyWith carries keyId + account', () {
      const m = Machine(id: 'id-3', label: 'x', token: 't');

      expect(c.account, 'a1');
      expect(c.token, 't'); // token is immutable through copyWith
      // a later copyWith that touches only the label keeps keyId/account.
      final c2 = c.copyWith(label: 'y');
      expect(c2.keyId, 'k1');
      expect(c2.account, 'a1');
    });
  }

- [ ] **Step 7 (GREEN): Implement `pairing.dart`.**
  Create `app/lib/src/services/e2ee/pairing.dart`. Uses pinenacl `Box` (Curve25519 + XSalsa20-Poly1305) so seal/open are byte-compatible with Go `x/crypto/nacl/box`; the sealed blob is `nonce(24B) || box`, matching `e2ee.SealPairing`. The device keypair is a pinenacl `PrivateKey` (X25519) so `sec`/`pub` are NaCl-compatible (per **D-d**).

  Note the pinenacl 0.5.1 API: `Box.decrypt(ByteList encryptedMessage, {Uint8List? nonce})` takes a `ByteList` for the ciphertext — construct it with `ByteList(cipher)` (there is NO `ByteList.fromList`). `PrivateKey`/`PublicKey` are `List<int>`, so `.toUint8List()` (the pinenacl extension) yields their bytes directly.
  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';

  import 'package:pinenacl/x25519.dart';

  /// Phone-side pairing crypto. Mirrors Go `e2ee.SealPairing`/`OpenPairing`
  /// (NaCl box: Curve25519 + XSalsa20-Poly1305, `nonce||box` framing).
  ///
  /// Flow: scan v2 QR -> [genDeviceKeypair] (persist `sec` to secure storage) ->
  /// send [buildPairInit] as the FIRST plaintext frame -> on `pair_result`,
  /// [openPairResult] unwraps `{keyId, psk}` and proves the PSK came from the
  /// real machine (sender authenticated by the QR's `enrollPub`).

  /// X25519 device keypair. `sec` is persisted as `devkey:<machineId>`; `pub` is
  /// sent (base64) in `pair_init`. 32-byte little-endian Curve25519 scalars,
  /// NaCl/TweetNaCl-compatible.
  Future<(List<int> pub, List<int> sec)> genDeviceKeypair() async {
    final priv = PrivateKey.generate();
    return (priv.publicKey.toUint8List(), priv.toUint8List());
  }

  /// The first plaintext frame the phone sends to start pairing. Field names
  /// match Go `bridge.PairInit` (`type`/`pairingId`/`devicePub`/`name`).
  Map<String, dynamic> buildPairInit({
    required String pairingId,
    required List<int> devicePub,
    required String name,
  }) =>
      {
        'type': 'pair_init',
        'pairingId': pairingId,
        'devicePub': base64.encode(devicePub),
        'name': name,
      };

  /// Open `pair_result.sealed`: base64-decode, split `nonce(24B) || box`, NaCl
  /// box-open with senderPub=[enrollPub] (from the QR) and recipientSec=
  /// [deviceSec], then json-decode the inner `{keyId, psk}` (Go
  /// `e2ee.PairPayload`). Throws if the box does not open (wrong machine /
  /// tamper / wrong device) or the inner JSON is malformed.
  Future<({String keyId, List<int> psk})> openPairResult(
    String sealedB64,
    List<int> enrollPub,
    List<int> deviceSec,
  ) async {
    final sealed = base64.decode(sealedB64);
    if (sealed.length <= 24) {
      throw const FormatException('e2ee: sealed pairing too short');
    }
    final nonce = sealed.sublist(0, 24);
    final cipher = sealed.sublist(24);
    final box = Box(
      myPrivateKey: PrivateKey(Uint8List.fromList(deviceSec)),
      theirPublicKey: PublicKey(Uint8List.fromList(enrollPub)),
    );
    // pinenacl throws on auth failure (wrong key / tamper) -> propagates.
    // BoxBase.decrypt takes a ByteList ciphertext + an explicit nonce; there is
    // no ByteList.fromList, so wrap the bytes with the ByteList(Iterable) ctor.
    final plain = box.decrypt(
      ByteList(cipher),
      nonce: Uint8List.fromList(nonce),
    );
    final obj = jsonDecode(utf8.decode(plain)) as Map<String, dynamic>;
    final keyId = obj['keyId'] as String?;
    final pskB64 = obj['psk'] as String?;
    if (keyId == null || keyId.isEmpty || pskB64 == null) {
      throw const FormatException('e2ee: bad pair payload');
    }
    final psk = base64.decode(pskB64);
    if (psk.length != 32) {
      throw const FormatException('e2ee: bad psk length');
    }
    return (keyId: keyId, psk: psk);
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 8: Add per-machine crypto material to `SecureStore`.**
  Modify `app/lib/src/services/secure_store.dart`. Per `.dartApi`: keys `psk:<machineId>` and `devkey:<machineId>`, base64 values, all wrapped in try/catch returning `null`/silent on failure (so a keychain failure degrades to the unpaired re-scan state). `deleteCrypto` removes both (unpair/revoke).

  Old (anchor — the start of the legacy-token section):
  ```dart
  // ---- legacy single token (kept for migration & a fallback) ----
  ```
  New:
  ```dart
  // ---- per-machine E2EE crypto material (secure storage only) ----

  // PSK and the device secret are NEVER in the machine JSON list; they live here
  // under namespaced keys. Storage failures degrade to the unpaired state (the
  // app then re-prompts a QR scan), so every op is best-effort.

  Future<void> savePsk(String machineId, List<int> psk) async {
    try {
      await _s.write(key: 'psk:$machineId', value: base64.encode(psk));
    } catch (_) {}
  }

  Future<List<int>?> loadPsk(String machineId) async {
    try {
      final v = await _s.read(key: 'psk:$machineId');
      if (v == null || v.isEmpty) return null;
      return base64.decode(v);
    } catch (_) {
      return null;
    }
  }

  Future<void> saveDeviceKey(String machineId, List<int> sec) async {
    try {
      await _s.write(key: 'devkey:$machineId', value: base64.encode(sec));
    } catch (_) {}
  }

  Future<List<int>?> loadDeviceKey(String machineId) async {
    try {
      final v = await _s.read(key: 'devkey:$machineId');
      if (v == null || v.isEmpty) return null;
      return base64.decode(v);
    } catch (_) {
      return null;
    }
  }

  /// Wipe both PSK and device secret for a machine (unpair / revoke).
  Future<void> deleteCrypto(String machineId) async {
    try {
      await _s.delete(key: 'psk:$machineId');
      await _s.delete(key: 'devkey:$machineId');
    } catch (_) {}
  }

  // ---- legacy single token (kept for migration & a fallback) ----
  ```
  (`base64` comes from the existing `import 'dart:convert';` at the top of the file.)

- [ ] **Step 9 (RED): Write the failing `bridge_client` seal/open seq test.**
  Create `app/test/e2ee/client_codec_test.dart`. This exercises the `_send`/`_onData` seal+open seq logic directly via a tiny test seam (Step 10 adds `installCodecForTest` + `sealForTest`/`openForTest`). It asserts: outbound frames become `{k,s,n,c}` envelopes with a monotonic `s`; a valid inbound envelope opens to the plaintext and bumps `inSeq`; a replayed (stale-seq) envelope is dropped (returns null, leaves `inSeq` unchanged); a tampered ciphertext is dropped.

  Because T9's `E2eeCodec` is phone-oriented (seal=p2b, open=b2p), the inbound (bridge→phone) frame is built by a small `bridgeSeal` helper that seals with `kB2P` and AAD `keyId|b2p|seq` — the exact byte layout the Go bridge / T9 codec produce (`c = base64(cipherText || mac)`, `n = base64(nonce)`). The phone's own `E2eeCodec.seal` is used for the outbound assertion.
  ```dart
  import 'dart:convert';

  import 'package:cryptography/cryptography.dart';
  import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
  import 'package:codex_remote_app/src/services/bridge_client.dart';
  import 'package:codex_remote_app/src/services/e2ee/codec.dart';
  import 'package:codex_remote_app/src/services/e2ee/kdf.dart';

  void main() {
    // Mirror of the bridge->phone seal: encrypt under kB2P with AAD
    // 'keyId|b2p|seq' and the same envelope layout T9's codec.dart emits
    // (c = base64(cipherText || mac), n = base64(nonce)). Cannot reuse
    // E2eeCodec for this: its seal is hardcoded to the p2b direction/AAD.
    final _aead = Xchacha20.poly1305Aead();
    Future<Map<String, dynamic>> bridgeSeal(
        String keyId, List<int> kB2P, int seq, Map<String, dynamic> plain) async {
      final nonce = _aead.newNonce();
      final aad = utf8.encode('$keyId|${dirTag(Dir.b2p)}|$seq');
      final box = await _aead.encrypt(
        utf8.encode(jsonEncode(plain)),
        secretKey: SecretKey(kB2P),
        nonce: nonce,
        aad: aad,
      );
      return {
        'k': keyId,
        's': seq,
        'n': base64.encode(nonce),
        'c': base64.encode(box.cipherText + box.mac.bytes),
      };
    }

    // Build the phone codec + its kB2P from a shared PSK/keyId.
    Future<(E2eeCodec phone, List<int> kB2P)> setup() async {
      final psk = List<int>.generate(32, (i) => i + 1);
      const keyId = 'k_test_0001';
      final (kP2B, kB2P) = await deriveKeys(psk, keyId);
      final phone = E2eeCodec(keyId: keyId, kP2B: kP2B, kB2P: kB2P);
      return (phone, kB2P);
    }

    test('_send seals to a {k,s,n,c} envelope with a monotonic seq', () async {
      final (phone, _) = await setup();
      final c = BridgeClient();
      c.installCodecForTest(phone);
      final e0 = await c.sealForTest({'type': 'ping', 'i': 0});
      final e1 = await c.sealForTest({'type': 'ping', 'i': 1});
      expect(e0['k'], 'k_test_0001');
      expect(e0['s'], 0);
      expect(e1['s'], 1); // monotonic
      expect(e0['n'], isA<String>());
      expect(e0['c'], isA<String>());
    });

    test('_onData opens a valid inbound envelope and advances inSeq', () async {
      final (phone, kB2P) = await setup();
      final c = BridgeClient();
      c.installCodecForTest(phone);
      final env = await bridgeSeal('k_test_0001', kB2P, 0,
          {'type': 'sessions', 'data': const []});
      final opened = await c.openForTest(env);
      expect(opened, isNotNull);
      expect(opened!['type'], 'sessions');
      // next expected seq advanced; replay of the same env must now drop.
      final replay = await c.openForTest(env);
      expect(replay, isNull);
    });

    test('a tampered ciphertext is dropped (returns null)', () async {
      final (phone, kB2P) = await setup();
      final c = BridgeClient();
      c.installCodecForTest(phone);
      final env = await bridgeSeal('k_test_0001', kB2P, 0,
          {'type': 'sessions', 'data': const []});
      env['c'] = 'AAAA${(env['c'] as String).substring(4)}'; // corrupt ct
      expect(await c.openForTest(env), isNull);
    });
  }
  ```
  Run: `cd app && flutter test test/e2ee/client_codec_test.dart`
  Expected: FAIL — compile error: `The method 'installCodecForTest' isn't defined for the class 'BridgeClient'` (and `sealForTest`/`openForTest`).

    // E2EE: once a machine is paired, every session frame is sealed/opened through
    // this codec. `_paired` gates the pairing handshake — reconnect REUSES the
    // codec (no re-pair); a user disconnect clears it. Null codec = legacy plain.
    E2eeCodec? _codec;
    bool _paired = false;

    /// machine on app start from stored PSK+keyId); reused across reconnects.
    void useCodec(E2eeCodec codec) {
      _codec = codec;
      _paired = true;
    }

    /// paired machine must rebuild the codec from secure storage before connect.

      _paired = false;

  10c — seal in `_send`. The codec's `seal` is async, so `_send` schedules the encrypt-then-write; when no codec is set it stays synchronous plaintext (legacy). Old:
  ```dart
    void _send(Map<String, dynamic> m) => _ch?.sink.add(jsonEncode(m));
  ```
  New:
  ```dart
    void _send(Map<String, dynamic> m) {
      final codec = _codec;
      final ch = _ch;
      if (ch == null) return;
      if (codec == null) {
        ch.sink.add(jsonEncode(m)); // legacy plaintext
        return;
      }
      // Seal, then write the {k,s,n,c} envelope. Ordering across calls is held by
      // the codec's monotonic outSeq, not by send order on the wire.
      codec.seal(m).then((env) {
        if (identical(_ch, ch)) ch.sink.add(jsonEncode(env));
      }).catchError((Object e) {
        if (kDebugMode) debugPrint('e2ee seal failed: $e');
      });
    }

  New:
  ```dart
    /// Open an inbound envelope `{k,s,n,c}` through the codec, validating the
    /// expected seq. Returns the plaintext map, or null to DROP (unknown shape,
    /// bad seq, auth fail) — never resets the connection (per the design: drop +
    /// log, do not tear down).
    Future<Map<String, dynamic>?> _openEnvelope(Map<String, dynamic> env) async {
      final codec = _codec;
      if (codec == null) return env; // legacy plaintext: pass through
      try {
        return await codec.open(env);
      } catch (e) {
        if (kDebugMode) debugPrint('e2ee open dropped a frame: $e');
        return null;
      }
    }

    void _onData(dynamic raw) {
      Map<String, dynamic> m;
      try {
        m = jsonDecode(raw as String) as Map<String, dynamic>;
      } catch (_) {
        return;
      }
      // E2EE: an inbound envelope (has the plaintext keyId `k` + ciphertext `c`)
      // must be opened + seq-checked before dispatch. A failed open drops the
      // frame (does not reset). Plaintext pair_* frames have no `k`/`c`.
      if (_codec != null && m.containsKey('k') && m.containsKey('c')) {
        _openEnvelope(m).then((plain) {
          if (plain != null) _dispatch(plain);
        });
        return;
      }
      _dispatch(m);
    }

    void _dispatch(Map<String, dynamic> m) {
      switch (m['type']) {
  ```

The `switch (m['type']) {` line is the LAST line of the new block above; everything from the original `case 'sessions':` down to the original method-closing `}` stays put and becomes `_dispatch`'s body unchanged.)

- [ ] **Step 11: Add the pairing handshake helper to `BridgeClient`.**
  Modify `app/lib/src/services/bridge_client.dart`. Add a `pair(...)` method that runs the one-shot `pair_init`→`pair_result` exchange over a raw WebSocket (before the normal `connect`), so `connect_screen` can pair a brand-new machine. It opens a temporary socket, sends `buildPairInit`, awaits the single plaintext reply, and returns `{keyId, psk}` or throws on `pair_error`/timeout. This keeps the handshake out of the steady-state read loop (which only ever sees envelopes once paired).

  11a — imports (pairing helpers + e2ee). Old:
  ```dart
    import '../models/session.dart';
  import 'e2ee/codec.dart';
  ```
  New:
  ```dart
    import '../models/session.dart';
  import 'e2ee/codec.dart';
  import 'e2ee/pairing.dart';
  ```

  11b — append the `pair` method just before the closing brace of the class (after `_onDone()` ends). Old (the final method + class close):
  ```dart
    void _onDone() {
      if (state != ConnState.error) state = ConnState.disconnected;
      turnRunning = false;
      notifyListeners();
      _scheduleReconnect();
    }
  }
  ```
  New:
  ```dart
    void _onDone() {
      if (state != ConnState.error) state = ConnState.disconnected;
      turnRunning = false;
      notifyListeners();
      _scheduleReconnect();
    }

    /// One-shot E2EE pairing handshake over a throwaway socket: open [wsUrl],
    /// send the plaintext `pair_init` (carrying [devicePub]), await the single
    /// plaintext reply, and on `pair_result` unwrap `{keyId, psk}` with
    /// [openPairResult] (sender authenticated by [enrollPub] from the QR).
    /// Throws `PairException` on `pair_error`, a bad reply, or timeout. The
    /// caller persists keyId/psk/devkey then calls [connect] normally.
    Future<({String keyId, List<int> psk})> pair({
      required String wsUrl,
      required String pairingId,
      required List<int> devicePub,
      required List<int> deviceSec,
      required List<int> enrollPub,
      required String name,
    }) async {
      final ch = WebSocketChannel.connect(Uri.parse(wsUrl));
      try {
        await ch.ready;
        final done = Completer<({String keyId, List<int> psk})>();
        late final StreamSubscription<dynamic> sub;
        sub = ch.stream.listen(
          (raw) async {
            if (done.isCompleted) return;
            Map<String, dynamic> m;
            try {
              m = jsonDecode(raw as String) as Map<String, dynamic>;
            } catch (_) {
              return; // ignore non-JSON noise during the handshake
            }
            if (m['type'] == 'pair_result') {
              try {
                final r = await openPairResult(
                    (m['sealed'] as String?) ?? '', enrollPub, deviceSec);
                if (!done.isCompleted) done.complete(r);
              } catch (e) {
                if (!done.isCompleted) {
                  done.completeError(PairException('bad pair_result: $e'));
                }
              }
            } else if (m['type'] == 'pair_error') {
              if (!done.isCompleted) {
                done.completeError(
                    PairException((m['reason'] as String?) ?? 'unknown'));
              }
            }
          },
          onError: (Object e) {
            if (!done.isCompleted) done.completeError(PairException('$e'));
          },
          onDone: () {
            if (!done.isCompleted) {
              done.completeError(const PairException('closed'));
            }
          },
        );
        ch.sink.add(jsonEncode(buildPairInit(
            pairingId: pairingId, devicePub: devicePub, name: name)));
        try {
          return await done.future.timeout(const Duration(seconds: 15));
        } finally {
          await sub.cancel();
        }
      } finally {
        await ch.sink.close();
      }
    }
  }

  /// Thrown when the pairing handshake fails. [reason] is the coarse bridge
  /// reason (`unknown`/`expired`) or a local cause; the UI shows the same
  /// '二维码失效，请重新生成' for all of them. (Per **D-e** a reused/consumed
  /// pairingId reads back as `unknown`; `consumed` is reserved/undocumented on
  /// the wire in v1.)
  class PairException implements Exception {
    final String reason;
    const PairException(this.reason);
    @override
    String toString() => 'PairException($reason)';
  }
  ```
  Run: `cd app && flutter analyze lib/src/services/bridge_client.dart`
  Expected: `No issues found!` (compiles; the handshake is covered end-to-end by the connect_screen wiring and the Go pairing tests).

  cd app && git add lib/src/services/bridge_client.dart && git commit -m "feat(app): one-shot E2EE pairing handshake (pair_init/pair_result)"
  ```

  12b — route a v2 QR to pairing inside `_scanQr` (and reuse the same helper from `_pasteFromClipboard`). Old (`_applyConnectString` + `_scanQr` + `_pasteFromClipboard`):

  New:
  ```dart
    // Pull the token AND the relay host out of a scanned/pasted connect string, so
    // a machine is pinned to the relay its QR actually came from.
    void _applyConnectString(String s) {
      final host = hostFromWsUrl(s);
      if (host != null && host.isNotEmpty) _hostCtrl.text = host;
      _ctrl.text = (tokenFromWsUrl(s) ?? s).trim();
      _ctrl.selection = TextSelection.collapsed(offset: _ctrl.text.length);
    }

    Future<void> _scanQr() async {
      final result = await Navigator.of(context).push<String>(
        MaterialPageRoute(builder: (_) => const QrScanScreen()),
      );
      if (!mounted || result == null || result.isEmpty) return;
      // A v2 pairing QR runs the E2EE handshake; anything else is a legacy token.
      final qr = QrV2.tryParse(result);
      if (qr != null) {
        await _pairAndConnect(qr);
        return;
      }
      _applyConnectString(result);
      if (_ctrl.text.isEmpty) return;
      await _connect();
    }

    Future<void> _pasteFromClipboard() async {
      final data = await Clipboard.getData(Clipboard.kTextPlain);
      final t = data?.text?.trim();
      if (t == null || t.isEmpty) return;
      final qr = QrV2.tryParse(t);
      if (qr != null) {
        await _pairAndConnect(qr);
        return;
      }
      _applyConnectString(t);
    }

    // Full E2EE pairing: generate the device keypair, run the pair_init/pair_result
    // handshake against the QR's host+token, then persist keyId/PSK/devkey and arm
    // the codec before connecting. On failure show the canonical re-scan prompt.
    Future<void> _pairAndConnect(QrV2 qr) async {
      try {
        final host = (qr.host == kRelayHost) ? '' : qr.host;
        final (devicePub, deviceSec) = await genDeviceKeypair();
        final wsUrl = wsUrlForToken(qr.token, host: host);
        final result = await bridge.pair(
          wsUrl: wsUrl,
          pairingId: qr.pairingId,
          devicePub: devicePub,
          deviceSec: deviceSec,
          enrollPub: qr.enrollPub,
          name: _nameCtrl.text,
        );
        // Persist: the Machine (token + keyId), and the secrets (PSK + devkey).
        final m = await machines.add(
            label: _nameCtrl.text, token: qr.token, host: host);
        // Stamp the keyId onto the just-added machine and re-persist + notify
        // (MachineStore has no keyId setter yet; rebuild its list immutably).
        machines.machines = machines.machines
            .map((e) => e.id == m.id ? e.copyWith(keyId: result.keyId) : e)
            .toList();
        await store.saveMachines(machines.machines);
        machines.notifyListeners();
        await store.saveDeviceKey(m.id, deviceSec);
        await store.savePsk(m.id, result.psk);
        // Arm the codec, then connect (the codec survives reconnects).
        final (kP2B, kB2P) = await deriveKeys(result.psk, result.keyId);
        bridge.useCodec(
            E2eeCodec(keyId: result.keyId, kP2B: kP2B, kB2P: kB2P));
        await bridge.connect(wsUrl);
        if (mounted && Navigator.of(context).canPop()) {
          Navigator.of(context).pop();
        }
      } on PairException catch (_) {
        if (mounted) {
          ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
            const SnackBar(content: Text('二维码失效，请重新生成')),
          );
        }
      } catch (e) {
        if (mounted) {
          ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar(
            SnackBar(content: Text('配对失败：$e')),
          );
        }
      }
    }
  ```
  > `machines.notifyListeners()` is callable here because `MachineStore extends ChangeNotifier` and `notifyListeners()` is `@protected` only by convention — if `flutter analyze` flags it as protected, add a tiny `void setKeyId(String id, String keyId)` method to `MachineStore` (rebuild list + `_persist()`) and call that instead. Either is fine; the `setKeyId` route is the cleaner one.

**Note (D-j):** The miniprogram (`miniprogram/relay.js`) is intentionally NOT updated; an E2EE-mode bridge refuses its cleartext frames. This is a conscious v1 limitation, documented in the design spec §1.

### T11: Integration + security test pass (cross-mode)

This is the **final gate before merge**. It writes NO production code — every type and function it touches already exists from T1–T10. It adds *end-to-end* security tests that drive the real seams (`Server.openInbound`/`Server.sealOutbound`/`Server.handlePairInit`, `devstore.Store`, `e2ee.*`) the way a live phone↔bridge session would, asserting each load-bearing invariant from the security model: replay reject, unknown-keyId drop, seq-rollback drop, cleartext-after-pairing hard-cut, single-use pairing consumption, and revocation (delete device + reload → old keyId fails). It then re-confirms the Dart↔Go cross-language vector parity holds. Locked decisions honored: hard-cut is mandatory once provisioned (D-c), keyId binds `conn.device` via `devstore.Lookup(keyId).Name` (D-k), `keyId = NewToken()` 16-byte hex (D-f), E2EE is enabled via the additive `Server.EnableE2EE(devices, enrollSec, pending)` helper with no breaking `NewServer` change (D-g), and `authDevice` stays in place (D-i).

**Files:**
- Modify (Test): `bridge/internal/bridge/server_test.go`
- Modify (Test): `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing_test.go`  *(file created by T5; T11 appends cross-mode integration cases)*
- Modify (Test): `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart`  *(file created by T9; T11 appends cross-mode security cases)*

**Depends on:** T6 (server E2EE seams: `openInbound`/`sealOutbound`/`EnableE2EE`, conn e2ee fields, hard-cut), T7 (agent/hub E2EE seams — same `openInbound`/`sealOutbound` shared by direct + hub), T8 (provision enroll keys + tray hooks), T10 (Dart pairing + client integration; the Dart codec from T9 is in place).

- [ ] **Step 1: Add a shared test helper that mints a fully-provisioned E2EE Server + a paired device, in `server_test.go`.**
  This helper is the fixture every cross-mode integration test below reuses: it builds a real `devstore.Store` + `PendingStore` on a temp dir, runs the real `handlePairInit` to enroll a device (so the on-disk `devices.json` and the in-memory map are populated exactly as production would), opens the sealed `pair_result` with the device secret to recover the PSK + keyId, and derives the two subkeys with `e2ee.DeriveKeys`. The existing import block in `bridge/internal/bridge/server_test.go` already contains `context`, `net/http`, `net/http/httptest`, `net/url`, `os`, `path/filepath`, `testing`, `time` (all still used by the current `TestHandleFileMediaGate` / `write` helper — do NOT drop any of them). ADD four imports to it: `encoding/base64`, `encoding/json`, and the two new packages. The resulting block must read exactly:

  ```go
  import (
  	"context"
  	"encoding/base64"
  	"encoding/json"
  	"net/http"
  	"net/http/httptest"
  	"net/url"
  	"os"
  	"path/filepath"
  	"testing"
  	"time"

  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/devstore"
  	"github.com/yunyuchen/codex-remote/bridge/internal/e2ee"
  )
  ```

  Then append the helper at the end of the file (after `write`):

  ```go
  // e2eeEnv is a fully-provisioned E2EE bridge plus one paired device, built
  // through the REAL pairing handler so the on-disk devices.json, the in-memory
  // devstore map, and the derived subkeys all match what a live phone would hold.
  // The returned Server is in E2EE mode (devices != nil) per decision D-c.
  // (Named e2eeEnv, not e2eeFixture, to avoid colliding with T6's func e2eeFixture.)
  type e2eeEnv struct {
  	s         *Server
  	devices   *devstore.Store
  	pending   *devstore.PendingStore
  	dir       string
  	keyID     string
  	psk       []byte
  	kP2B      []byte // phone -> bridge subkey (the phone seals with this)
  	kB2P      []byte // bridge -> phone subkey (the phone opens with this)
  	enrollPub *[32]byte
  	enrollSec *[32]byte
  	devPub    *[32]byte
  	devSec    *[32]byte
  }

  func newE2EEEnv(t *testing.T) *e2eeFixture {
  	t.Helper()
  	dir := t.TempDir()

  	enrollPub, enrollSec, err := e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenEnrollKeypair: %v", err)
  	}
  	devPub, devSec, err := e2ee.GenDeviceKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenDeviceKeypair: %v", err)
  	}

  	devices, err := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))

  	s := &Server{
  		clients:   make(map[*conn]struct{}),
  		approvals: make(map[string]*pendingApproval),
  		turns:     make(map[string]string),
  		turnOwner: make(map[string]string),
  		resumed:   make(map[string]bool),
  		pushes:    make(map[string]PushReg),
  		pusher:    LogPusher{},
  	}
  	s.EnableE2EE(devices, enrollSec, pending)

  	// Stage a valid one-time pairing, then run the real handler.
  	pid := NewToken()
  	if err := pending.AddPending(pid, 5*time.Minute); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
  	}
  	res, perr := s.handlePairInit(PairInit{
  		Type:      "pair_init",
  		PairingID: pid,
  		DevicePub: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(devPub[:]),
  		Name:      "我的 iPhone",
  	})
  	if perr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("handlePairInit failed: %+v", perr)
  	}

  	// Open the sealed pair_result exactly as the phone would (D.sec + enrollPub).
  	sealed, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(res.Sealed)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("decode sealed: %v", err)
  	}
  	pp, err := e2ee.OpenPairing(sealed, enrollPub, devSec)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("OpenPairing: %v", err)
  	}
  	psk, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(pp.PSK)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("decode psk: %v", err)
  	}
  	kP2B, kB2P, err := e2ee.DeriveKeys(psk, pp.KeyID)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("DeriveKeys: %v", err)
  	}

  	return &e2eeEnv{
  		s: s, devices: devices, pending: pending, dir: dir,
  		keyID: pp.KeyID, psk: psk, kP2B: kP2B, kB2P: kB2P,
  		enrollPub: enrollPub, enrollSec: enrollSec, devPub: devPub, devSec: devSec,
  	}
  }

  // phoneConn builds a fresh per-connection state object the way handleWS/agent.open
  // would: nothing crypto-bound yet (keyID resolves on the first envelope).
  func (f *e2eeEnv) phoneConn() *conn {
  	return &conn{send: make(chan any, 8), done: make(chan struct{}), id: "sess1", e2ee: true}
  }

  // sealFromPhone encodes v as JSON, seals it under the phone's kP2B at the given
  // seq (phone -> bridge, DirP2B), and returns the wire bytes the bridge receives.
  func (f *e2eeEnv) sealFromPhone(t *testing.T, seq uint64, v any) []byte {
  	t.Helper()
  	plain, err := json.Marshal(v)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal plaintext: %v", err)
  	}
  	env, err := e2ee.Seal(f.kP2B, f.keyID, e2ee.DirP2B, seq, plain)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
  	}
  	raw, err := json.Marshal(env)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal envelope: %v", err)
  	}
  	return raw
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the direct-mode happy-path round-trip test (RED).**
  Confirms the fixture wiring is correct end to end: a sealed `prompt` frame opens into the right `inbound`, binds the keyId to the conn (setting `conn.device` from `devstore.Lookup(keyID).Name` per D-k), and advances `seqIn`. Append to `server_test.go`:

  ```go
  // A sealed phone frame must open into the right inbound, bind the device name
  // from the keyId (decision D-k), and advance the inbound sequence.
  func TestE2EEDirectRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
  	f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  	c := f.phoneConn()

  	raw := f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "hello e2ee"})
  	in, err := f.s.openInbound(c, raw)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("openInbound rejected a valid frame: %v", err)
  	}
  	if in.Type != "prompt" || in.Text != "hello e2ee" {
  		t.Fatalf("decoded inbound = %+v, want prompt/hello e2ee", in)
  	}
  	if c.keyID != f.keyID {
  		t.Fatalf("keyId not bound to conn: got %q want %q", c.keyID, f.keyID)
  	}
  	if c.device != "我的 iPhone" {
  		t.Fatalf("conn.device = %q, want the paired device name (D-k)", c.device)
  	}
  	if c.seqIn != 1 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqIn = %d, want 1 after one frame", c.seqIn)
  	}

  	// A second in-order frame continues to open and advances seq again.
  	raw2 := f.sealFromPhone(t, 1, map[string]any{"type": "interrupt", "threadId": "t1"})
  	if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, raw2); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("openInbound rejected the 2nd in-order frame: %v", err)
  	}
  	if c.seqIn != 2 {
  		t.Fatalf("seqIn = %d, want 2 after two frames", c.seqIn)
  	}
  }
  ```

  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run TestE2EEDirectRoundTrip -v`
  Expected: PASS (the seams already exist from T6). If it FAILS on `conn.device` it means the keyId→name binding (D-k) was not wired in T6 — fix T6, not this test.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the cross-mode security suite (replay / unknown-keyId / seq-rollback / tamper), driving `openInbound` (RED→GREEN).**
  These are the heart of T11. They assert the load-bearing drops the security model promises, exercised through the SAME `openInbound` seam both direct and hub modes use (T7 confirms hub reuses it), so one suite covers both. Append to `server_test.go`:

  ```go
  // Replay (a previously-accepted seq re-sent), an unknown keyId, a rolled-back
  // seq, and a tampered ciphertext must each be dropped by openInbound. These are
  // the cross-mode (direct + hub share openInbound) anti-replay / anti-spoof
  // invariants — every failure returns an error so the caller audits + disconnects,
  // leaking nothing to the peer.
  func TestE2EEInboundSecurityDrops(t *testing.T) {
  	t.Run("unknown keyId dropped", func(t *testing.T) {
  		f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  		c := f.phoneConn()
  		// Seal under a keyId the devstore has never seen.
  		plain, _ := json.Marshal(map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "x"})
  		env, err := e2ee.Seal(f.kP2B, "deadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeef", e2ee.DirP2B, 0, plain)
  		if err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("Seal: %v", err)
  		}
  		raw, _ := json.Marshal(env)
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, raw); err == nil {
  			t.Fatal("openInbound accepted an unknown keyId; must drop")
  		}
  		if c.keyID != "" {
  			t.Fatalf("unknown keyId must not bind to conn; got %q", c.keyID)
  		}
  	})

  	t.Run("replay of an accepted seq dropped", func(t *testing.T) {
  		f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  		c := f.phoneConn()
  		raw := f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "first"})
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, raw); err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("first frame must open: %v", err)
  		}
  		// Re-send the identical seq-0 frame. seqIn is now 1, so seq 0 is stale.
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, raw); err == nil {
  			t.Fatal("openInbound accepted a replayed frame; must drop")
  		}
  	})

  	t.Run("seq rollback dropped", func(t *testing.T) {
  		f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  		c := f.phoneConn()
  		// Advance to seqIn==2 with two in-order frames.
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "a"})); err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("frame 0: %v", err)
  		}
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, f.sealFromPhone(t, 1, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "b"})); err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("frame 1: %v", err)
  		}
  		// A frame at seq 1 (rolled back below the expected 2) must be refused.
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, f.sealFromPhone(t, 1, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "rollback"})); err == nil {
  			t.Fatal("openInbound accepted a rolled-back seq; must drop")
  		}
  	})

  	t.Run("tampered ciphertext dropped", func(t *testing.T) {
  		f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  		c := f.phoneConn()
  		raw := f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "tamper me"})
  		var env e2ee.Envelope
  		if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &env); err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("unmarshal env: %v", err)
  		}
  		ct, err := base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(env.C)
  		if err != nil {
  			t.Fatalf("decode ciphertext: %v", err)
  		}
  		ct[len(ct)-1] ^= 0xFF // flip a tag byte
  		env.C = base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(ct)
  		bad, _ := json.Marshal(env)
  		if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, bad); err == nil {
  			t.Fatal("openInbound accepted a tampered frame; must drop")
  		}
  	})
  }
  ```

  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run TestE2EEInboundSecurityDrops -v`
  Expected: PASS (T6 already enforces keyId lookup, seq monotonicity via `e2ee.ErrSeq`, and AEAD auth via `e2ee.ErrAuth`). A FAIL here is a genuine security regression in `openInbound` — fix the seam, never relax the test.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the hard-cut test — cleartext session frame after the connection is E2EE-bound is dropped (RED→GREEN).**
  Per D-c, once the bridge is E2EE-provisioned a non-envelope session frame is never honored. This asserts the mandatory hard-cut at the `openInbound` seam (a plaintext `{"type":"prompt"}` is NOT a valid envelope) and at the first-frame router (`firstFrameKind`). Append to `server_test.go`:

  ```go
  // Hard-cut (decision D-c): once the bridge is E2EE-provisioned, a cleartext
  // session frame is never accepted. openInbound must reject a plaintext prompt,
  // and firstFrameKind must classify it as "reject" (only pair_init or a {k,s,n,c}
  // envelope may open a connection).
  func TestE2EEHardCutRejectsCleartext(t *testing.T) {
  	f := newE2EEEnv(t)
  	c := f.phoneConn()

  	// A bare plaintext session frame is not an envelope (no k/n/c) -> drop.
  	cleartext, _ := json.Marshal(map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "no crypto"})
  	if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c, cleartext); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("E2EE bridge accepted a cleartext session frame; hard-cut violated")
  	}

  	// First-frame router: pair_init and envelope are the ONLY admitted first frames.
  	cases := []struct {
  		name string
  		raw  []byte
  		want string
  	}{
  		{"pair_init plaintext", mustMarshal(t, map[string]any{"type": "pair_init", "pairingId": "p", "devicePub": "x", "name": "n"}), "pair_init"},
  		{"envelope", f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "ok"}), "envelope"},
  		{"cleartext prompt", cleartext, "reject"},
  		{"cleartext list", mustMarshal(t, map[string]any{"type": "list"}), "reject"},
  		{"garbage", []byte("not json at all"), "reject"},
  	}
  	for _, tc := range cases {
  		if got := firstFrameKind(tc.raw); got != tc.want {
  			t.Errorf("%s: firstFrameKind = %q, want %q", tc.name, got, tc.want)
  		}
  	}
  }

  func mustMarshal(t *testing.T, v any) []byte {
  	t.Helper()
  	b, err := json.Marshal(v)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("marshal: %v", err)
  	}
  	return b
  }
  ```

  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run TestE2EEHardCutRejectsCleartext -v`
  Expected: PASS. A cleartext prompt has no `k`/`n`/`c`, so `firstFrameKind` returns `"reject"` and `openInbound` (which json-unmarshals into `Envelope` and finds an empty/zero `C`) returns an error.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the revocation integration test (delete device + reload → old keyId fails) — the hub-mode-relevant kill switch (RED→GREEN).**
  Revocation is the first per-device kill switch that also works in hub mode (the bridge resolves PSK by keyId on every frame, so a `devstore.Lookup` miss after reload drops the socket). This drives the REAL `Revoke` + `Reload` path. Per D-a the production reload is an mtime poll (`devstore.Watch`), but tests call `Reload()` directly. Append to `server_test.go`:

  ```go
  // Revocation (decision: delete devices.json entry + reload -> old keyId fails).
  // A frame that opened fine before revocation must be dropped after the device is
  // removed and the store reloaded. This is the per-device kill switch that works
  // in hub mode too (PSK is resolved by keyId on every frame). Production reloads
  // via the mtime-poll devstore.Watch (D-a); the test calls Reload() directly.
  func TestE2EERevocationDropsOldKeyID(t *testing.T) {
  	f := newE2EEEnv(t)

  	// Before revocation: a fresh conn opens a frame fine.
  	c1 := f.phoneConn()
  	if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c1, f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "pre"})); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("pre-revocation frame must open: %v", err)
  	}

  	// Revoke the device and reload (simulating the tray edit + mtime-poll Reload).
  	ok, err := f.devices.Revoke(f.keyID)
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Revoke: %v", err)
  	}
  	if !ok {
  		t.Fatal("Revoke reported the keyId absent; it should exist")
  	}
  	if err := f.devices.Reload(); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("Reload after revoke: %v", err)
  	}
  	if _, found := f.devices.Lookup(f.keyID); found {
  		t.Fatal("revoked keyId still present after reload")
  	}

  	// After revocation: a brand-new conn presenting the same keyId is dropped
  	// (unknown keyId now).
  	c2 := f.phoneConn()
  	if _, err := f.s.openInbound(c2, f.sealFromPhone(t, 0, map[string]any{"type": "prompt", "text": "post"})); err == nil {
  		t.Fatal("revoked keyId still opened a frame; revocation ineffective")
  	}
  }
  ```

  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run TestE2EERevocationDropsOldKeyID -v`
  Expected: PASS (T3's `Revoke`+`Reload` and T6's keyId lookup make the post-revocation frame an unknown-keyId drop).
