- [ ] **Step 8: Append the single-use pairing consumption integration test to `pairing_test.go` (RED→GREEN).**
  T5's `pairing_test.go` already unit-tests `handlePairInit` valid/expired/consumed in isolation. T11 adds the *integration* assertion that a captured-QR replay (the exact threat from the spec §2) cannot re-bind: the SAME `pairingId` consumed once yields a second `pair_error` whose wire `reason` is `"unknown"` (per D-e, a reused id is indistinguishable from never-existed). Append to `bridge/internal/bridge/pairing_test.go` (its import block already includes `time`, `encoding/base64`, `path/filepath`, and the `devstore`/`e2ee` packages from T5 — T5's handler needs them; the test below uses only those):

  ```go
  // Single-use pairing (spec §2: a photographed QR must not re-bind a device).
  // The first pair_init consumes the pairingId and succeeds; an identical replay
  // is refused with reason "unknown" (decision D-e: a reused id is indistinguishable
  // from a never-existed one, both surface as "unknown").
  func TestPairingSingleUseReuseRejected(t *testing.T) {
  	// A self-contained server whose enrollSec matches this test's enroll keypair.
  	_, enrollSec, err := e2ee.GenEnrollKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenEnrollKeypair: %v", err)
  	}
  	devPub, _, err := e2ee.GenDeviceKeypair()
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("GenDeviceKeypair: %v", err)
  	}

  	dir := t.TempDir()
  	devices, err := devstore.Open(filepath.Join(dir, "devices.json"))
  	if err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("devstore.Open: %v", err)
  	}
  	pending := devstore.OpenPending(filepath.Join(dir, "pending-pairings.json"))
  	s := &Server{
  		clients:   make(map[*conn]struct{}),
  		approvals: make(map[string]*pendingApproval),
  		pushes:    make(map[string]PushReg),
  		pusher:    LogPusher{},
  	}
  	s.EnableE2EE(devices, enrollSec, pending)

  	pid := NewToken()
  	if err := pending.AddPending(pid, 5*time.Minute); err != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("AddPending: %v", err)
  	}
  	init := PairInit{
  		Type:      "pair_init",
  		PairingID: pid,
  		DevicePub: base64.StdEncoding.EncodeToString(devPub[:]),
  		Name:      "replay-victim",
  	}

  	// 1st use: succeeds.
  	if _, perr := s.handlePairInit(init); perr != nil {
  		t.Fatalf("first pair_init must succeed: %+v", perr)
  	}
  	// 2nd use (the photographed-QR replay): refused with reason "unknown".
  	res2, perr2 := s.handlePairInit(init)
  	if perr2 == nil {
  		t.Fatalf("replayed pairingId must be refused; got result %+v", res2)
  	}
  	if perr2.Reason != "unknown" {
  		t.Fatalf("reused pairingId reason = %q, want %q (D-e)", perr2.Reason, "unknown")
  	}

  	// And the consumed pairing is gone from the pending file (single-use): a
  	// direct Consume of the reused id now returns ErrPairUnknown.
  	if err := pending.Consume(pid, time.Now()); err != devstore.ErrPairUnknown {
  		t.Fatalf("Consume of a reused id = %v, want ErrPairUnknown", err)
  	}
  }
  ```

  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run TestPairingSingleUseReuseRejected -v`
  Expected: PASS. The first `handlePairInit` calls `PendingStore.Consume` which removes the id; the second `Consume` returns `devstore.ErrPairUnknown`, which T5's handler maps to `PairError{Reason: "unknown"}`.

- [ ] **Step 9: Commit the pairing single-use integration test.**
  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./internal/bridge/ -run 'TestPairing|TestE2EE' -v`
  Expected: all pairing + E2EE tests PASS.
  Then: `git add bridge/internal/bridge/pairing_test.go && git commit -m "test(e2ee): integration — photographed-QR replay cannot re-bind (single-use pairing)"`

- [ ] **Step 10: Add phone-side cross-mode drop cases to the Dart codec test (RED→GREEN).**
  T9's `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart` already owns the cross-language AEAD/HKDF parity: it pins the Go-generated `kP2B`/`kB2P`/ciphertext constants and asserts `open(pinned)==plaintext`, `sealWithNonce(fixed)==pinned C`, PLUS tamper + seq-rollback unit cases against that pinned vector. T11 does NOT re-implement AEAD-level tamper/parity (that is T9's job, and there is no contracted Dart API to forge a valid *bridge→phone* (`b2p`) frame — only the Go bridge produces those; the pinned vector is `p2b`). What T11 adds is the two phone-client drop checks that `E2eeCodec.open` enforces on the envelope *fields before* the AEAD step, so they need no valid `b2p` ciphertext: a wrong `k` (keyId mismatch) and a non-monotonic `s` (seq rollback / replay). Both are built by sealing a real frame with the codec's own `seal` and then mutating the envelope's `k`/`s` — `open` rejects on the field check (`k==keyId`, `s==inSeq`) before ever decrypting. Append a new `group` to `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart` (it already imports `package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart`, `dart:convert`, and the codec from T9; reuse the same pinned `kVectorKP2B`/`kVectorKB2P`/`kVectorKeyId` constants T9 declared at the top of the file):

  ```dart
  group('T11 cross-mode security (phone-side envelope-field drops)', () {
    // E2eeCodec.open rejects a frame on its FIELD checks (k==keyId, s==inSeq)
    // BEFORE the AEAD step, so these drops need no valid bridge->phone ciphertext.
    // AEAD-level tamper + the pinned-vector cross-language parity are owned by the
    // T9 cases in this same file. kVectorKP2B / kVectorKB2P / kVectorKeyId are the
    // pinned Go-vector constants T9 declared at the top of this file.
    late E2eeCodec codec;

    setUp(() {
      codec = E2eeCodec(
        keyId: kVectorKeyId,
        kP2B: base64.decode(kVectorKP2B),
        kB2P: base64.decode(kVectorKB2P),
      );
    });

    test('wrong keyId on the envelope is rejected before decrypt', () async {
      // Seal a real frame, then swap its k to a foreign keyId. open() must reject
      // on the k==keyId check regardless of the (irrelevant) ciphertext.
      final env = await codec.seal({'type': 'prompt', 'text': 'x'});
      final bad = {...env, 'k': 'someotherkeyid00'};
      expect(() => codec.open(bad), throwsA(anything));
    });

    test('seq rollback / replay is rejected before decrypt', () async {
      // open() expects s == inSeq (starts at 0). A frame whose s is anything but
      // the next expected seq is dropped. Use a too-high s here so the check that
      // fires is the seq check (not the AEAD). A replay would present a stale s the
      // same way once inSeq has advanced.
      final env = await codec.seal({'type': 'prompt', 'text': 'x'});
      final rolled = {...env, 's': 99};
      expect(() => codec.open(rolled), throwsA(anything));
      // inSeq must not advance on a rejected frame.
      expect(codec.inSeq, 0);
    });
  });
  ```

  Command: `cd app && flutter test test/e2ee/codec_test.dart`
  Expected: PASS. `E2eeCodec.open` (T9) requires `env['k']==keyId` and `env['s']==inSeq` before attempting the XChaCha20-Poly1305 open, so both mutated envelopes throw and `inSeq` stays 0. AEAD-tamper parity and the pinned cross-language vector are asserted by the T9 cases in this file (re-run in Step 11).

- [ ] **Step 11: Run the FULL cross-language parity gate (Go + Dart) — the final merge gate.**
  Command: `cd bridge && go test ./... && go vet ./... && cd ../app && flutter test`
  Expected: every Go package test PASSES (including `internal/e2ee`, `internal/devstore`, `internal/bridge` with the new `TestE2EE*`/`TestPairing*` cases), `go vet` clean, and every Dart test PASSES (including the pinned-vector parity from T9 and the new T11 field-drop cases). This is the single command that proves Dart↔Go vector parity still holds end to end. If the Go vector and Dart pins ever diverge, T9's `kVector*` assertions FAIL here — regenerate the pinned constants from the Go oracle (T1's `codec_test.go` printer), never hand-edit the Dart constants.

- [ ] **Step 12: Commit the Dart cross-mode drop cases and close the gate.**
  Command: `cd app && flutter test`
  Expected: all Dart tests PASS.
  Then: `git add app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart && git commit -m "test(e2ee): phone-side envelope-field drops (wrong-keyId/seq-rollback) + cross-language parity gate"`

## Out of Scope / Known Limitations

- **`/file` media stays plaintext** over the existing token-gated endpoint (nginx TLS in transit; hub sees bytes). Not E2E-encrypted this round — the extension allowlist + magic-byte sniff remain the load-bearing exfil control.
- **Miniprogram `relay.js` is intentionally NOT updated** (D-j). An E2EE-provisioned bridge refuses miniprogram cleartext session frames (hard-cut); the miniprogram front-end will not connect until a future round mirrors the codec.
- **Pairing forward-secrecy is deferred**: the PSK is sealed to the device's long-term key, so a future compromise of the device secret *plus* a recorded pairing exchange could recover that one PSK. Single-use is enforced by `pairingId` consumption; FS is a later hardening.
- **`pair_error` collapses `consumed` -> `unknown` on the wire** (D-e); the app shows one message for all reasons.
- **Physical near-field QR snatch** (an attacker who watches your screen in real time and races the pairing) is not defended in v1.
- **Server-side accounts / seats / billing** and replacing the global `HUB_AGENT_KEY` with a server-issued one-time challenge are a separate commercialization milestone (spec §11), not in this plan.

---

## Cross-Language Vector Handoff

The Go `e2ee` test is the **oracle** for cross-language parity. `bridge/internal/e2ee/codec_test.go` (T1) computes — from the fixed inputs — and **prints** `base64(kP2B)`, `base64(kB2P)`, and `base64(ciphertext)`.

**Fixed inputs (identical on both sides):** `psk` = 32 bytes `0x01..0x20`; `keyId` = `"k_test_0001"` (also the HKDF salt); `nonce` = 24 bytes `0xA0..0xB7`; `seq` = `0`, `dir` = `p2b` (encrypt with `kP2B`, `aad = "k_test_0001|p2b|0"`); `plaintext` = compact JSON of `{"type":"prompt","text":"hello e2ee"}`; info strings = `"codex-e2ee/v1/phone->bridge"` / `"codex-e2ee/v1/bridge->phone"`.

Run T1's vector test, capture the three printed base64 constants, paste them into `app/test/e2ee/kdf_test.dart` and `app/test/e2ee/codec_test.dart` (T9) **before** T9 can pass. See **B6/B7** for the constant-naming alignment and the missing pairing-vector producer.

---

## Execution Order (dependency DAG)

```
T1 -+- T2 -+
    |       +- T5 - T6 - T7 -+
    +- T3 -++- T4 -----------+
           +- T8 ------------+
T1 - T9 - T10 ---------------+- T11
```

- **Go crypto core first:** T1 (codec+kdf, prints the vector) -> T2 (pairing box + see B7).
- **Storage in parallel:** T3 (devstore, no deps) -> T4 (pending).
- **Bridge wiring:** T5 (pairing handler) -> T6 (direct seams; honor B1) -> T7 (hub seams; honor B5). T8 (provision/tray/prod-wiring) needs T3+T4, parallel to T5–T7.
- **Dart mirror:** T9 needs T1's pinned vector; T10 needs T9 (honor B6/B8).
- **Final gate:** T11 (cross-mode integration + security; honor B2/B4).
